# Unfilter digest 13 — pregnancy, birth and the first weeks

Prepared 2026-11-17 from all 1,169 quote records in voice/reddit-ur08.jsonl (416), voice/reddit-ur21.jsonl (414) and voice/reddit-ur32.jsonl (339), all three ledgers’ “What these posts add” sections, the build README and the current findings. The comparisons below use the 2026-11-16 revised findings, including its restoration and later-wave conclusions.

These are deliberately selected re-read passages from previously excluded material. Every count is a count of quotes, never people or prevalence. Multiple quotes may belong to one account or related posts. All subjects remain evidence: abortion, coercion, forced sex, violence, substance use, loss, stillbirth, suicidal or homicidal thoughts, disability, racism and teenage or trans pregnancy are preserved in the source wording. Source medical, causal, legal and policy statements remain attributed accounts.

The pattern sections give the conclusion to revise, the proposed change, full opening quotations and further supporting or qualifying IDs. The evidence appendix reproduces every record that has not already appeared in full. Reader context is copied from the record’s context field and labeled separately; the blockquote is the complete, unedited quote field. Some quotes support several patterns, so IDs and some full quotations recur.

Most consequential revisions: distinguish pregnancy number from loss history and living children; separate unequal labor from coercion; treat prenatal mental health and reproductive decisions explicitly; develop bereavement and hospital-separated first weeks; qualify the idea that the right information, format or calendar can supply care, safety or support. The evidence still establishes no purchase or price acceptance of the proposed Mamivio guide.

## Patterns

### 1. After loss, body signals and reassuring milestones can recall danger rather than settle fear.

**Current findings location:** §§2, 6 (waiting/reassurance), 9, 10 Against 5; §§16.5, 17.3.

**Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “Trimester, pregnancy number and a calendar are not sufficient personalisation by themselves.”

**What should change:** Name losses separately from pregnancy number and living-child count, including gestation and the way the loss was discovered. A normal scan, movement, nausea or reaching twelve weeks may not feel protective. A previous missed miscarriage can undermine trust in an absence of symptoms or bleeding; a previous blighted ovum can undermine trust in rising hCG. Do not describe this simply as unfamiliarity or replaceable lack of information.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0192]** — Reader context: Normal tests and regular movement do not remove fear after stillbirth.

> I'm still scared. The only thing they said is she's measuring a bit small, not enough to raise any real concern, though. I keep wondering, what if it's happening again and we missed it this time? At this point, I'm afraid I'll be spending this entire pregnancy thinking my baby may not make it. 
> 
> Is this normal after loss? I'm feeling her move regularly now and the only thing I can think is "what if that's the last time?"
> 
> Logically I know she's probably going to be fine. I'm just terrified of experiencing the same thing twice 😭

**[pbu08-0201]** — Reader context: Relief from nausea becomes frightening because symptoms had felt protective after loss.

> I found out I was pregnant about 3 weeks ago (today I am about 7 weeks and 2 days) and for the past two weeks I have been very nauseous, grossed out by most food, and exhausted. I considered this as a good sign, since back in November I had a miscarriage and did not have any of these symptoms. Wednesday I was absolutely exhausted and extremely nauseous. Thursday was better, still tired, a bit nauseous and unable to eat certain things. Now today, Friday, I woke up feeling pretty good, not tired or nauseous, and able to eat my normal breakfast without any issues. I know these things can fluctuate, but since I’ve had a miscarriage before, it’s making me so anxious and scared :( Supposed to have my first ultrasound on Tuesday, was feeling better about it until now. Just looking for advice or anyone who’s been through this before?

**[pbu08-0243]** — Reader context: Appointments themselves become feared repetitions of the previous loss diagnosis.

> Last July my husband and I found out I was pregnant but sadly by August our baby didn’t have a heartbeat anymore. I found out I was pregnant again in April and was excited. But every doctors appointment or blood work has made me stressed and anxious before and during the appointment because I’m worried I’m going to hear that something is wrong or there’s no heartbeat again. I try to ration with myself that I’m still feeling all of the symptoms and everything is fine but it’s hard at times.

**[pbu08-0314]** — Reader context: Seeks preparation for the scan room itself after a prior loss.

> How do you all calm yourselves down for the anatomy scan? After our last loss, I have a hard time with ultrasounds, I just cry. I’m scared the entire time. I want to be in a zen spot for my anatomy scan, it’s with MFM so I know it will be even longer. I just want to start working on getting in the right headspace for the next time I enter that ultrasound suite.

**[pbu08-0347]** — Reader context: Feeling well reproduces a symptom pattern preceding an earlier miscarriage.

> Hey friends,
> 
> I am 6 weeks and 4 days pregnant and this is my first pregnancy after a miscarriage. I haven’t had many symptoms since it’s so early but have felt incredibly tired/sleepy/ winded after walks etc. today I woke up and I feel really good? Last time my preg symptoms (nausea at the smell of beyond meat) faded two weeks before I miscarried so I’m anxious now that I feel good today? My appointment for the bloodwork and ultrasound are next week. Any advice?

**[pbu08-0403]** — Reader context: Wants repeated assurance after the devastation of stillbirth.

> My first pregnancy ended in a full term stillbirth and left me devastated. I want to be assured and reassured that all is well with this little one.

**[pbu21-0031]** — Reader context: Repeated early losses changed what a positive test means.

> The second time around, we tried for a while. I was pregnant about 8 times in the past year and a half and it always ended in an early loss around week 6-9. I decided not to take any pregnancy ‘seriously‘ before week 12 to ‘guard my heart’. I could only scoff at movie scenes where women get excited over a positive pregnancy test. A positive pregnancy test meant nothing to me, I had one in almost every cycle. I think the whole experience left me a bit cynical.

**[pbu21-0032]** — Reader context: Reassurance lasts seconds and bonding differs from the first pregnancy.

> I am 20 weeks pregnant today but I don‘t feel the excitement of my first pregnancy at all. I thought it would get better by week 12, but there is a lingering feeling of impending doom, like something will go wrong. I also don‘t feel any kicks yet so that also doesn‘t help. Before every checkup I imagine what could go wrong. Baby is always fine but I’m relieved for a second only. And I never really feel a connection to the baby. I also don‘t want to think of names yet and it drives my husband a little crazy I think… he is so excited and wants to talk about it and share his ideas!

**[pbu21-0055]** — Reader context: A new pregnancy does not erase pain around the previous due date.

> Then the next day I received an invite to a more thoughtful friend‘s kid‘s 2nd birthday and it happens to be on the day I was due originally. While I appreciated the low pressure text and her stating it isn’t necessary to attend, I feel sad and a little guilty that this also upsets me. I know I need to protect myself and grief and trauma tend to show up around milestones, but damn I wasn’t expecting this to be so hard, especially in a new pregnancy. Just wondering if others have felt or are feeling this way. It’s making it hard to be present for new milestones and also making it hard to grieve the past when I have so much to be thankful for.

**[pbu21-0309]** — Reader context: Prior stillbirth delays readiness and car-seat purchase even after prior loss milestone.

> I just really need to vent. I am currently 35 weeks pregnant with my second baby boy after a 3rd trimester stillbirth in 2022. I honestly thought that after I made it past the point in which I lost his big brother that I would begin to feel more excited for this baby, but I have honestly felt anything but. It’s been so bad that I literally JUST purchased his car seat & have been dragging my foot packing his hospital bag.

**[pbu32-0007]** — Reader context: Appointment date misunderstanding delays reassurance by one day.

> Miscarriages are always horrible, but the missed miscarriage experience has just caused me so much anxiety because I don’t trust my body to let me know if something is wrong.

**[pbu32-0171]** — Reader context: Three losses and reproductive-immunology treatment complicate usual reassurance advice.

> I know everyone will say just go for a reassurance scan, but literally every reassurance scan has ended badly for us so the trauma is real and my next clinic scan is in 9 days.

**[pbu32-0190]** — Reader context: Pushes for more HCG tests to gain advance warning, not simply repeated reassurance.

> Part of me wants the levels bc my last miscarriage I was having my levels checked just randomly and that’s how we found out that my pregnancy was not viable. I appreciated knowing way before I started bleeding and it wasn’t just unexpected.

**[pbu32-0239]** — Reader context: Bleeding and declining fetal heart rate; compares OB assessment with hopeful peer stories.

> This is my 5th pregnancy, including a stillbirth, in 13 months.    I realize these mostly heal on their own but would love to hear if anyone has been in a similar boat or is currently experiencing this.  My OB is not hopeful for this pregnancy but I hear so many success stories …

**[pbu32-0257]** — Reader context: Rising HCG cannot reassure after previous anembryonic pregnancy also had rising levels.

> I don’t go in for my ultrasound for another 3 weeks but my hgc keeps rising (it did with the blighted ovum as well).

### 2. Early pregnancy can mean weeks of unresolved viability, discrepant dates and contradictory results.

**Current findings location:** §§2, 5 (care), 6, 16.2, 17.4.

**Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “Care navigation is its own pain.”

**What should change:** Add the period of diagnostic uncertainty itself: repeat blood draws, heartbeat checks and scans may be days or weeks apart, while work and daily life continue. Separate normal uncertainty about timing from reported contradictions, a suspected laboratory error, and findings not explained to the parent. A contact list cannot supply the missing result. Parents' interpretations of hCG, heart rate and dates remain testimony.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0181]** — Reader context: Reports positive home tests, negative hospital tests and feeling disbelieved; pregnancy remains author-reported.

> So we go to the hospital, they run two tests a urine and blood & tell me a weird answer. They tried not to deny I was pregnant but they also denied it too. Saying my blood work came back negative & they refused to give me an ultra sound. They said it could have been too early to tell & I should test in a week & see my obgyn in the state I live in. We are all the way across the country at this point & my husband got me pregnant. And now no on believes me. I'm scared for me and my baby.

**[pbu08-0232]** — Reader context: Blunt prediction of miscarriage and criticism of agitation linger after an IVF scan.

> Fast forward to noon and doctor calls me telling me, “ your baby is measuring 6 weeks instead of 6 weeks 3 days and has a lower heart rate. We want 110 - 160. You are going to miscarry.” Then after I’m short with her, she says, “you seem agitated…” like COME ON!

**[pbu08-0244]** — Reader context: Unfamiliar bleeding and falling hCG after repeated loss produce uncertainty and hopelessness.

> Hello all, my hcg went from 130 on saturday, to 123 on monday and today it was at 116. My doctors have called me in for a scan tomorrow, what should I be expecting? I had brown bleeding with black clots, light pink and red bleeding with black clots, my cervix is still closed, i havnt passed anything fully, is this ectopic? Ive misscarried alot now but never have i been through this, i dont really know what to expect or what's going to happen. Im starting to completely lose hope that my body can support pregnancy anymore after cancer treatments etc. Maybe scar tissue from my various surgeries has a part to play? I dont know anymore. I feel hopeless.

**[pbu08-0282]** — Reader context: Third pregnancy differs from the previous two and may require D&C.

> I’m so conflicted and heartbroken. This is my 3rd pregnancy and I’ve never been through anything like this is my last two. If the new ultrasound doesn’t show anything I have to prepare for a D&C. 
> 
> I do have a tilted uterus, unsure if that’s makes a difference. Has anyone been misdiagnosed with a BO and had a healthy normal pregnancy?

**[pbu08-0289]** — Reader context: Must initiate every contact and cannot obtain an ultrasound explaining uncertain hCG results.

> My doctors are not reaching out to me unless I call and leave a message or write on the portal to them... They aren't concerned it's ectopic, but I'm just surprised that with my levels and the weird spotting, that they havent thought to give me an ultrasound to tell me why she this is viable (I don't think it will be, based on my levels...) or not, or to check if it is ectopic. I'm sitting in a limbo, kinda fling crazy. Either waiting for a miscarriage, or everything is fine and I just have to sit here blind.

**[pbu08-0408]** — Reader context: Provider's refusal to interpret changing scan measurements intensifies an eight-day wait.

> I'm extremely worried because the embryo appears to have grown only about 3 days' worth in the 6 days between scans, and the heart rate increased by only 6 bpm. The midwife wasn't willing to comment on whether these numbers were reassuring or concerning, which has left me feeling very anxious. She did indicate that i am at a high risk of miscarriage.
> 
> I have another ultrasound scheduled in 8 days, and waiting feels incredibly difficult.

**[pbu08-0413]** — Reader context: Normal-for-dates reassurance conflicts with advice not to announce the pregnancy.

> A nurse came and spoke with me and said they typically can’t see a heartbeat this early but still suggested I not tell anymore people than I’ve already told that I am pregnant. I will go back in a week to check for the heartbeat again. They also told me everything is looking normal for my gestational age. Despite them saying everything is normal, I left feeling like they were just preparing me for bad news which did not help my anxiety ☹️

**[pbu08-0414]** — Reader context: Had to remove medical detail to ask about staff behavior rather than viability.

> Edit: post originally got flagged, I removed some medical info and modified my post to be more clear and centered around my actual question. I am not asking for anyone to reassure me that my pregnancy is okay.  
> My question is about the behavior of the medical staff and whether that is typical.

**[pbu21-0191]** — Reader context: Two-week wait after an inconclusive first appointment feels mentally trapping.

> I had my first ultrasound appointment and they couldn’t tell me if I was too early or if I’m having a miscarriage. My period is really irregular so it’s hard to know how far along I am…. I have another appointment in 2 weeks to see if there’s any growth/ heartbeat. It’s my first pregnancy and I’m feeling so nervous. The doctor said she was unable to give me an answer if it’s a viable pregnancy or not. I’m stuck in my anxious mind for 2 weeks. Has anyone experienced something like this before? How did you keep your mind busy and positive?

**[pbu21-0235]** — Reader context: Suspected lab error and relayed result lead to stopping medication before confirmation.

> **The Current Lab Limbo:**  
> I repeated my Beta-hCG on Sunday. A few hours later, the lab called my husband directly. They verbally stated the machine read the hCG at **1,100** (a massive drop from Friday's 5,907). However, **the lab coordinator explicitly told us they froze the report and refused to release it on the online portal because they suspect a technical, machine, calibration, or dilution mistake**—they said a drop that massive doesn't align with my complete physical stability. They requested an immediate repeat sample today (Monday) for verification.  
> Because my husband relayed that verbal 1,100 phone call to my OB, she tentatively assumed early pregnancy loss and told me yesterday to stop all my medications (Duphaston and Transamin). I stopped them yesterday, but now I am terrified that if the 1,100 was indeed a lab glitch, I am putting a viable pregnancy at risk by leaving the medications off.

**[pbu21-0269]** — Reader context: Must participate in normal work while unsure whether the baby still lives.

> The most difficult part for me is having to get through normal life in the process. I will still have to go to work. I will still have to participate while not knowing if my baby still has a heart beat.

**[pbu21-0315]** — Reader context: Technician changes viability assessment during the same examination.

> The scan was incredibly confusing. At first, he told me the sac was still low. Then he said the pregnancy was no longer viable because the sac was empty and there was no heartbeat. A minute later, he stopped, found what he called a bundle of cells, showed it to both me and my husband, and then said, “No, it’s still viable, but there’s no heartbeat yet.”

**[pbu21-0395]** — Reader context: Written radiology report contradicts reassuring verbal scan description.

> Hi all, I had a 6 week scan last week and I thought I was 6 weeks 5 days, turns out baby is 6 weeks three days. The tech told us (I know they really aren’t supposed to give diagnostic info but she could tell how scared I was) that she could see a verryyy faint heart beat measuring 119-122 bpm. We were very excited! Well, yesterday my radiology report came back and everything looked normal except it said: Cardiac Activity: Not definitely seen on current examination. The fetal pole is 0.6 cm. I’m so beyond terrified and called my OB to see if I need another ultrasound (even though I have another scheduled for next Friday) and I haven’t heard back yet. At this point I had the ultrasound about a week ago and should be measuring 7 weeks 4 days. Anyone have similar stories? I’m so afraid of what is to come. Thank you all 🫶🏻

**[pbu21-0396]** — Reader context: Follow-up scan finds a strong heartbeat after confusing report.

> Thank you so much❤️ I went today and my OB didn’t seem happy with what the radiologist put in the report, she did her own and they found bubs strong beat! 163❤️

**[pbu32-0148]** — Reader context: Alternating scan interpretations leave author uncertain about proceeding with scheduled procedure.

> I've been told "congratulations you're pregnant" and "I'm so sorry you've miscarried we should schedule a D&C" 4 times in the last month now. I now have a D&C scheduled for Tuesday and unsure what to do.

**[pbu32-0230]** — Reader context: Rising HCG conflicts with small sac growth and clinician's concern about nonviability.

> What confuses me is that my hCG has continued to rise significantly, so I’m struggling to understand how that fits with what was seen on ultrasound.

### 3. Monitoring, stories, apps and AI can either help or intensify checking, and parents want different forms of reassurance.

**Current findings location:** §§4 (apps/search), 6, 11, 15, 16.2, 16.4, 17.6.

**Relationship:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “some people seek direct information and others seek less.”

**What should change:** Explain the mechanism: checking strips or portal notes can overturn a reassuring appointment; continuous glucose data can create perfectionism; professional NICU experience can supply only frightening examples. Keep the request for company without hope alongside requests for good outcomes, blunt facts and actual data. Retain the contrary example of Claude helping a partner process an emergency. These are reported uses and effects, without validating a chatbot's medical claim.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0053]** — Reader context: Anticipates reassurance moving from one milestone to the next without ending anxiety.

> I know the ultrasound probably won’t magically cure the anxiety either. I’ll probably worry until the next appointment, then until I feel movement, then until birth, and then I’ll have a whole new list of things to worry about keeping this beautiful little parasite alive.

**[pbu08-0125]** — Reader context: Reading Reddit turns lack of symptoms into fear of missed miscarriage.

> I have absolutely zero symptoms. No boobs tenderness, no nausea/vomitting. Im extremely anxious if this is a missed miscarriage cause i made the mistake of going down the reddit rabbithole and now this is etched in my brain
> 
> Any similar symptoms?? I am extremely anxious, literally biting my nails away. Please can anyone offer any words of comfort :(

**[pbu08-0195]** — Reader context: Online claims of kicks at ten weeks make seventeen weeks without movement feel behind.

> I am 17 weeks and have not felt baby at all yet (as far as i know). Every time I thought it was, it definitely wasn't. I'm scared something is wrong because I see a ton of other FTMs talk about how they felt kicks at 10 weeks and I feel super behind.

**[pbu08-0258]** — Reader context: Uses chatbot and online research to question a clinician's repeated miscarriage predictions.

> I asked chatgpt and it said about 200-300. My OB is weird she is constantly telling me i will miscarry . I am noticing light spotting which disappears after 15 20 mins . I got my TVS ultrasound done today. I cant attach the photo here but there is a tiny gestational sac and doc said this is not a promising result please tell me if this is true for 4weeks and 4 days pregnancy .

**[pbu08-0301]** — Reader context: Wants someone to talk with rather than optimistic reassurance about a likely loss.

> Not really looking for hope because I know what this means, but anyone with similar stories just to talk through it with someone.

**[pbu08-0372]** — Reader context: Chatbot response about ectopic risk causes panic in an expecting father.

> So I started asking chat gpt. And it started talking about how conception, before the next period after taking plan B, has a risk of ectopic pregnancy (conception in the fallopian tubes). And im kinda freaking out and havin panic attacks about it because this is my first supposed baby and were both very excited and I really dont want anything to go wrong or for her to be in danger. So does anybody have any experiences with this or with ectopic pregnancy. Please let me know. Freaking tf out a bit. Wish I never consulted chatgpt

**[pbu21-0019]** — Reader context: Deleted Instagram after repeated frightening pregnancy content.

> I deleted my only social media last week (Instagram) because my algorithm was feeding me non-stop videos of stillbirths, newborns with debilitating disabilities, etc. Truly disturbing and horrific things. That was all on top of fearmongering content like, “You’re going to RUIN your baby if you feed them cake with SUGAR on their first birthday!” It was either horror-instilling videos or insanely guilt-tripping tradwife mom content.

**[pbu21-0020]** — Reader context: Reports relief after deactivating Instagram.

> I deactivated my instagram and have enjoyed not seeing that content for the last two weeks, and would recommend it to anyone else being digitally pelted with the horrors.

**[pbu21-0104]** — Reader context: Six scans and recent reassurance do not resolve fear when sickness stops.

> im really really struggling and panicking im convinced that babys heart has stopped my morning sickness has just disappered for past two days i had a scan four days ago and baby was fine but since then sickness has stopped i have severe anxiety and ocd and feel like im going insane ive already had 6 scans already because im petrified something is going to go wrong ive had miscarriages in past and this is driving me crazy google says that hormones go up at 8 weeks so sickness should peak but mine has gone im so so scared and feel like im going insane

**[pbu21-0196]** — Reader context: Continuous glucose monitoring creates new distress despite easier testing.

> This week I started doing insulin shots instead, and I have a glucose sensor in my arm to measure my blood sugar so I don't have to prick my finger 8x a day anymore. And I was super excited because it felt like I finally got the "right" treatment, but now I can SEE how much my blood sugars are spiking and falling without me being able to control it, and the sensor is reading the sugars wrong (both too high and too low readings) and I just feel like giving up and eating whatever and let nature run its course. Worst case my baby will be too big, birth will be induced early and then I get to both see my child earlier AND get rid of the diabetes faster ://

**[pbu21-0197]** — Reader context: Clarifies that frustrated thoughts coexist with following treatment.

> I am, however, going to do everything I can to avoid these risks and to follow the treatment plan. I don't want the baby to get too big or to need an induction if I can avoid it. And I might still be able to, I just need to rant.

**[pbu21-0198]** — Reader context: NICU work makes positive outcomes hard to imagine.

> Yeah I have struggled with the "burden" of only having seen the worst of the worst that can happen in pregnancy and birth (normal babies don't end up in the NICU) so it is hard for me to imagine things might work out okay :'))

**[pbu21-0199]** — Reader context: Clinical team explains perfect glucose readings are not the treatment goal.

> I think those strict limits have been the cause of my frustration too, since they're not really realistic when a person has GDM, but the endocrinologist team has assured me that being in range 100% of the time is not even the goal (which I have been beating myself up over when I have one 5 minute period of time over/under my range) - I think it's mostly the hormones making me super sensitive and feeling like I am "failing at being pregnant", but realistically I know well that I am managing the GDM better than actually expected +++

**[pbu21-0316]** — Reader context: ChatGPT interpretation of portal notes reverses reassurance from two scans.

> Backstory: I have had a previous miscarriage at 7 weeks, did not get to see that baby’s heartbeat. Now, I finally have a new pregnancy and am very nervous. I had an 8 week scan last week and all looked good with a strong heartbeat! I had another scan today for reassurance. Heartbeat was strong once again, even saw baby wiggling around. My provider called and told me the heartbeat was good and to enjoy my upcoming vacation. Now I see the ultrasound sheet on MyChart, and I have two findings that I don’t understand. ChatGPT tells me one could be a SCH and now I’m freaking out. Anyone have any experience with this??

**[pbu21-0327]** — Reader context: Daily test strips undermine reassurance from heartbeat scan.

> All that being said, my anxiety has just been through the roof. I worry non stop. Especially because I was having light cramps on and off almost every day, and then didn’t have them the past two days. I also have been peeing on the hcg test strips every day, and today’s looked lighter. Google says to stop doing that and that the strips aren’t accurate for tracking hcg progression…. I know that worrying won’t change the outcome and that I should just try to relax and focus on other things, but I am really struggling with the what ifs! I don’t have another appointment for 9 days.

**[pbu21-0373]** — Reader context: Becomes anxious about anxiety after imagining future loss.

> I go on a cycle of imagining the future, getting excited, remembering miscarriage is a thing that exists, get sad, get anxious, remember that stress and anxiety is bad for the baby so now I’m anxious about the fact that I’m anxious and it repeats over and over again at moments throughout the day.

**[pbu32-0136]** — Reader context: Pregnancy book's reassuring nausea framing alarms reader who lacks nausea.

> That made me feel so awful when i read that at about week 5, because i (still) haven’t experienced any sickness to that degree. So therefore my chances are higher??!

**[pbu32-0137]** — Reader context: Objects to timing of miscarriage and birth-defect pages in Your Pregnancy Week by Week.

> Like, i understand the importance of knowing about these kinds of things, but what an odd choice of placement. I believe the week before did the same thing but with several pages on all the birth defects your baby can have…

**[pbu32-0274]** — Reader context: New passage: French OB's reported kick-count advice precedes emotional relief; attributed advice, not verified clinical guidance.

> She said, "Girl, if you do that you will drive yourself absolutely crazy. If your baby is moving, your baby is safe. Trust your baby. If you feel something is genuinely wrong, call me. But do not hold your hand to your belly for hours a day making sure you count the "safe" about of kicks".

**[pbu32-0275]** — Reader context: New passage names mechanism of relief beyond previously quoted realization about enjoyment.

> I felt something crack open slightly in my chest. Like this armor of anxiety I have mistaken for caution/doing everything right was starting to break open.

### 4. A wanted, planned and supported pregnancy can still bring detachment, panic, anger and fear of never loving the baby.

**Current findings location:** §§1, 2, 6 (fear/shame/identity), 9, 10.

**Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “This reader is also trying to be believed, retain a say in birth decisions, afford care and remain able to function.”

**What should change:** Make prenatal mental health explicit rather than letting postpartum depression stand for it. Prepared nurseries, therapy, stable finances, an excellent partner and a longstanding wish for a baby do not guarantee excitement. Include fears about permanent changes to self and relationship, without turning a poster's tentative interpretation into a diagnosis. Preserve the author who explicitly rejects a depression interpretation.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0026]** — Reader context: Wanted pregnancy feels unsupported rather than special.

> Pregnancy has gone nothing like I’ve expected at all.  From how I’ve felt mentally/emotionally to how people in my life have been taking it. None of this has felt magical or special or celebrated. My partner is a literal saint and I just feel like I can’t put him through what this pregnancy and motherhood are probably going to do to me. Plus, I really thought we were going to have more support, but based on people’s reactions I’m starting to realize we really might be on our own.

**[pbu08-0028]** — Reader context: Finds pregnancy mental distress less discussed than physical symptoms.

> This is really helpful to read, thank you. I feel like I never read about how horrible people’s mental health is when they’re pregnant. Only how bad the physical symptoms are. But like, even though I knew I had a lot of shit that was gonna come up when I got pregnant I didn’t expect it to be this hard or this painful and lonely.

**[pbu08-0029]** — Reader context: Advice to plan without help makes her feel more depressed.

> Thank you for saying all of this. I’m trying so hard to “Plan as if nobody is coming to save you or help you” but whenever I do it just makes me more depressed. I also don’t know how long I can wait for then to offer to help when I really need help now…
> 
> It’s not like I need someone else to “handle” it, it’s just I don’t want to have to feel like I’m in this alone and now that I already do I’m just like fuck this sucks. It wasn’t supposed to be like this.

**[pbu08-0030]** — Reader context: Wants support and celebration alongside wanting motherhood itself.

> Of course I wanted a baby. I wanted a baby more than anything. I also wanted a family and I wanted a village. I’m having this baby because I want to be a mom and experience all the joy of raising a little human, but can’t I \*also\* want to be celebrated and gifted and cared for? Especially when I’ve watched SO MANY other women in my life have that experience? What’s wrong with wanting what I’ve seen other women in his family have?

**[pbu08-0049]** — Reader context: Knew about postpartum depression but not severe distress during pregnancy.

> I knew about postpartum depression but I had no idea it could get this bad during the pregnancy.
> 
> Please tell me I'm not the only one losing my mind during the pregnancy and that it gets better.

**[pbu08-0065]** — Reader context: Rapid conception after an ectopic pregnancy brings shock and concealed lack of excitement.

> Here I am now, 4.5 weeks pregnant and it’s all happened so SUPER fast that I can’t seem to get my head around it, can’t feel excited and don’t feel all the “happy” feelings you’re supposed to have. I just end up feeling like “what the F have I done?!?”
> 
> I tried bringing a bit of this up with my partner (who is also a bit shell shocked bless him) and he asked me whether I felt bits of excitement mixed in with it. I sort of bent the truth a little and said I did, but the honest answer right now is that I don’t.

**[pbu08-0207]** — Reader context: Nursery, letters, daily reading and excellent support do not prevent fear of failing or not connecting.

> I have done everything “right” so far, I have made her a beautiful nursery, I’ve written her letters and read her books and spoken to her every day. But suddenly I am so nervous, I’m nervous I won’t connect with my baby when I meet her, I’m nervous I won’t be good at this at all, nervous for all the ways my life will never be the same, nervous for all the mistakes I’ll inevitably make. I have dreamt of this baby forever, I have an incredible support system and the greatest possible husband but some how I suddenly feel like a failure and I haven’t even done it yet. Help?

**[pbu08-0332]** — Reader context: Stopped effective medication, cannot function at work, and waits for a doctor amid isolation.

> It didn't feel like anyone else was excited when I announced the pregnancy the reactions felt flat. I don't have friends to hang out with and I'm still waiting for my doctor to get off vacation so I can get more help because at the moment I'm off work and unable to function half the time anyway. I feel so useless, incompetent and just awful. I did and do struggle with depression aside from this pregnancy, but I was on medication that was helping and now I can't take it. I just want to stop crying and feeling like this. I feel like I could disappear and no one would notice for weeks. It's so isolating and lonely right now.

**[pbu08-0397]** — Reader context: Wanted pregnancy turns into panic and loss of quality of life with HG.

> Doctors diagnosed me with prenatal anxiety and depression, and I developed HG at 6 weeks. I lost all quality of life. My fiancée left for rotation again last week and I wake up every morning like “what the fuck have I done”. I’m gonna be 22 with a 2 month old, it just happened so quickly and too soon.

**[pbu08-0398]** — Reader context: Asks whether fear and regret can coexist with later connection to the baby.

> I’m scared I’ve made a horrible choice and that by the time I realize what I’ve done, it’ll be too late to go back. Did anyone else feel this way? Were you still able to connect with your baby? how long into the pregnancy did it take for you to feel some kind of excitement?

**[pbu21-0109]** — Reader context: Planned pregnancy immediately triggers panic after medication taper.

> The shock hit the exact second I saw the positive test. It felt like a fuse blew in my brain. Instantly, a violent, paralyzing panic triggered, and it hasn't stopped since.    
> The biggest issue—the thing that is completely breaking me mentally and making me feel like an absolute monster—is that instead of joy, any discussion or thought about the baby triggers physical repulsion, emotional nausea, and extreme anxiety. When I try to look at the future, I see only a cold, pitch-black screen. I am crying uncontrollably out of guilt toward my family and the baby.    
> I am scared to death that I will be incapable of loving my child, that I will look at them and only feel this crushing anxiety, and that I will ruin their life. I feel like I've made a massive mistake and my mind constantly tells me to run, even though logically I know there is nowhere to run from this.

**[pbu21-0110]** — Reader context: Asks whether medication can restore love and connection.

> Please, I am begging for some honesty: are there any other moms out there with a history of anxiety/depression who experienced this brutal detachment, panic, or even repulsion toward their pregnancy in the first trimester? Do you get your mind back once you restart medication? Can love really grow after going through this kind of medication?

**[pbu21-0181]** — Reader context: Body distress changes clothing, bathing and social plans.

> None of my clothes fit, I feel like a house, my belly is getting bigger everyday and I cry every single day and night because I cannot stand my body and I can’t stand getting dressed and I hate myself more than I ever thought possible. None of this feels magical or anything of the sort I just feel an insane pit of self loathing and whenever I tell people in my life that they say “oh well you’re pregnant!” Or that I’m “making the baby feel bad” with my negative self talk, Yipee thanks for the guilt trip on top of it aughhhh. I’ve even decided no baby shower because the idea of people seeing me like this quite literally gave me hives. I do not want anyone to see me like this.

**[pbu21-0282]** — Reader context: Did not know prenatal depression existed before experiencing it.

> I also don't really feel motherly, but I've always thought it was because it was other people's kids, and I would be fine when I had my own. I honestly didn't even know prenatal depression was a thing before I started experiencing it.

**[pbu21-0335]** — Reader context: Seeing baby move at ultrasound shifts feelings toward love.

> Once 8 weeks hit and I got to do my ultrasound, everything changed. There my baby was, on the screen, wiggling it's bottom. It looked like a little bean, but it was oh so cute. After that, I got this overwhelming sense of love for my baby. Ever since, I've loved being able to nurture and cherish this little thing. I've never wanted to meet my baby so bad.

**[pbu21-0340]** — Reader context: Planned pregnancy in stable relationship triggers persistent panic.

> However. Two weeks ago I found out I was pregnant (first pregnancy, planned, and I’m in a happy, stable relationship), and now I feel like I’m on the verge of a panic attack every second of every day. I’m wondering if any others have gone through pregnancy with panic disorder and could give me any advice. I’m so terrified.

**[pbu32-0131]** — Reader context: Wanted pregnancy; wonders whether sertraline-related blunting explains absent excitement.

> Instead, the week since finding out have felt emotionally flat, and it’s making me feel really guilty, like there’s something wrong with me or that I’m not reacting the “right” way, as I know this is what I want and I want to be so excited!!!

**[pbu32-0307]** — Reader context: Follow-up explicitly rejects depression interpretation; preserves author's distinction.

> But what I am feeling isn't depression. I am a psychologist and I do not meet the criteria for being depressed.

**Further supporting or qualifying IDs:** [pbu08-0006] [pbu08-0027] [pbu08-0050] [pbu08-0090] [pbu08-0104] [pbu08-0174] [pbu08-0176] [pbu08-0194] [pbu08-0195] [pbu08-0247] [pbu08-0248] [pbu08-0333] [pbu08-0342] [pbu08-0348] [pbu08-0349] [pbu08-0363] [pbu08-0364] [pbu08-0368] [pbu08-0371] [pbu08-0379] [pbu08-0384] [pbu08-0405] [pbu21-0001] [pbu21-0027] [pbu21-0051] [pbu21-0052] [pbu21-0108] [pbu21-0186] [pbu21-0209] [pbu21-0213] [pbu21-0217] [pbu21-0218] [pbu21-0234] [pbu21-0250] [pbu21-0283] [pbu21-0320] [pbu21-0321] [pbu21-0334] [pbu21-0336] [pbu21-0342] [pbu21-0348] [pbu21-0368] [pbu21-0373] [pbu21-0382] [pbu21-0383] [pbu21-0385] [pbu21-0386] [pbu21-0412] [pbu32-0011] [pbu32-0041] [pbu32-0075] [pbu32-0105] [pbu32-0120] [pbu32-0139] [pbu32-0151] [pbu32-0152] [pbu32-0153] [pbu32-0159] [pbu32-0178] [pbu32-0181] [pbu32-0187] [pbu32-0200] [pbu32-0237] [pbu32-0248] [pbu32-0265] [pbu32-0279] [pbu32-0282] [pbu32-0284] [pbu32-0288] [pbu32-0289] [pbu32-0306] [pbu32-0329]

### 5. Postpartum mental illness and intrusive thoughts can persist despite preparation, treatment or strong support.

**Current findings location:** §§2 (first weeks), 5, 6, 10 For 5, 17.4.

**Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “The first weeks include physical incapacity, feeding pressure, conflicting instructions, loneliness, regret and delayed connection.”

**What should change:** Add prenatal planning for the parent's actual mental-health history and later recurrence, rather than implying preparedness prevents collapse or the problem ends at two weeks. These accounts include suicidal or homicidal thoughts, intrusive fears of hurting the baby, fear of contamination, rage, dissociation, and screening scores that the parent feels miss their distress. Describe each author's own report; do not equate unwanted intrusive thoughts with intention. Include help that worked and support that remained insufficient.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0015]** — Reader context: Recalls immediate regret, anxiety and delayed connection after the first birth.

> I was so excited to be a mom even though I had no experience with babies. Unfortunately when I finally had him, I was instantly regretting it. I was so anxious when he would cry, and not sleeping was really getting to me. I felt no connection for a long time. I just didn’t feel confident when he was a new born and was scared.   
> That did go away and I love my son more than anything; he’s great.

**[pbu08-0016]** — Reader context: Better finances and preparation do not remove fear of repeating newborn distress.

> This time has been different because we both have better jobs, make more money and are way more prepared. I’ve been so excited for her, but lately I’m starting to feel less excited and nervous because I’m worried I will be the same way I was with my son.   
> I’m 31 now and in a better place in my life. I wake up in the middle of the night and can’t fall back asleep cos I’m thinking about what will happen once she’s here. I want it to be different this time, I don’t want to be anxious and scared of my baby like I was the first time.  
> Wondering if anyone has gone through anything similar.

**[pbu08-0075]** — Reader context: Therapy and regular family help did not prevent repeated severe postpartum distress.

> I have two kids and my mental health was terrible after both (not that it’s amazing generally either, but on another level). With my second I was prepared, was already seeing a therapist who knew what was coming, slept over at in laws house almost every week, etc yet I almost feel traumatized now by how horrible I felt. I saw a newborn baby the other week and immediately my stomach turned and I felt the classical FIGHT OR FLIGHT turn on inside.

**[pbu08-0076]** — Reader context: Fears initial medication effects while considering another pregnancy.

> I feel like the only option left is to take antidepressants next time immediately after birth (but they supposedly also make you feel terrible the first couple weeks so how is that going to work without me becoming actively suicidal?!)

**[pbu08-0077]** — Reader context: Can imagine repeating pregnancy and birth but fears postpartum collapse.

> Should I accept that i can’t have more ? I feel like this is such a short time of their life so it doesn’t feel ”fair”. I can go through four months of throwing up and nine months of nausea and food aversions again. I can give birth (I loved it lol). I can breastfeed. Just clearly my brain can’t handle postpartum without collapsing upon itself. I’m thinking about looking into homeopathic or ayurvedic treatments. Anything.

**[pbu08-0087]** — Reader context: Recalls begging for company during postpartum distress and being dismissed.

> I don’t have a village. I can’t just give someone my kids and be like I need a break. I remember going through PPD begging my mom- like crying and begging her to come sit with me. telling her I just didn’t want to live anymore. She pretty much told me she was going through a lot herself and not to be saying stuff like that because if something would happen it would make her life harder. yeah thats my village if you want to consider that a village.

**[pbu08-0089]** — Reader context: Describes postpartum rage followed by cyclical suicidal and homicidal thoughts.

> I gave birth last September and I struggled really bad with postpartum rage. It was so bad all the time. Fast forward to now, the rage in general has gone away but the week right before my period, I get very rageful. I’m talking like EVERYTHING agitates me and I legit have suicidal and homicidal thoughts.

**[pbu08-0093]** — Reader context: After NICU and traumatic birth, fears touching the baby and repeatedly checks feeding preparation.

> My baby was born at 36weeks 6 days and spent 5 days in the NICU. He’s been home 2 weeks and I’m dealing with awful anxiety and OCD around thinking I’m going to somehow hurt my baby. I have an infected hang nail and I’m afraid to even touch him because I’m afraid somehow the infection is going to spread to him. It doesn’t help that I have the start of mastitis and I’m taking antibiotics. I feel like if I touch my sweatshirt and hold him somehow the infection will be on my sweatshirt and spread to him. I’m afraid to touch anything in my house for fear of it spreading. I had a traumatic birth experience and I think that really is what jump started this. Although I did have this during pregnancy. I also have to ask my husband a million times, 3 scoops of formula for 6 oz of water? And then I’m afraid I accidentally only put 2 in or 4. I’m washing and sterilizing his bottles every single day I’m just so nervous about his little immune system. His 11 year old sister (my stepdaughter) is also here and she has cough and congestion which the doctor said was just allergies and if she doesn’t have a fever by the end of the weekend she’s ok to be around the baby but I just can’t help but want to say no don’t come near him. I’m really struggling with this and wondered if anyone felt the same.

**[pbu08-0138]** — Reader context: Questions screening threshold and how to access help while caring for an attached baby.

> At my 6 week pp check up, they did ask but I apparently scored a 7 and I needed a 12 for it to be a concern. I feel like that test isnt accurate or maybe Im answering wrong because of how the questions/answers are worded. I dont know if I should make an appointment to talk to my OB or if I should just go into therapy but then I dont know how to go about that when my son cannot be away from me for a very long time.

**[pbu08-0149]** — Reader context: Feels the baby is not hers and wonders whether difficult birth or life change explains it.

> Finally, I read someone on here describe it as though they don’t feel like their baby is theirs, and I absolutely feel like that. It’s hard to process that this is forever and that this is my baby, and I don’t know if that’s because of the labour I had and the start of our journey, or whether that’s a common feeling because it’s such a huge change.

**[pbu21-0071]** — Reader context: Physically recovered after uterine rupture but mentally feels unrecognizable.

> I’m a little over one month postpartum after a traumatic uterine rupture that nearly killed both me and my daughter. During delivery I experienced massive blood loss, went into shock and my daughter’s heart rate was undetecteable for 7 minutes. We are both physically okay now, but mentally I feel like my brain has become someone I don’t recognize.

**[pbu21-0072]** — Reader context: Reports hurtful arguments she cannot later remember.

> The scariest part is that during some arguments I apparently say extremely mean/hurtful things and later have zero memory of saying them. I only know because I later read the text thread afterwards. Memory gaps has never happened to me outside of college alcohol blackouts, and I have not drank at all since becoming pregnant.

**[pbu21-0073]** — Reader context: Looks for EMDR because daily functioning and marriage are affected.

> I’m looking into EMDR because this honestly feels more like PTSD/dissociation than “normal postpartum hormones,” but I’m scared and honestly ashamed of how much this is affecting my marriage and daily functioning already.

**[pbu21-0116]** — Reader context: Recalls emergency care for suicidal thoughts one week after birth.

> Sleep-deprived mothers are in one of the most vulnerable stages of their lives. Looking back over the last 12 weeks, I've had just one full night's sleep, and that was only because I was medicated after going to A&E with suicidal thoughts a week after my daughter was born.

**[pbu21-0117]** — Reader context: Credits partner, in-laws and midwife with active support.

> Postpartum depression and postpartum psychosis still aren't talked about enough. They are life threatening illnesses, not personal failings. I'm incredibly fortunate to have had a loving partner who stood beside me during my darkest days, in-laws who held my daughter so I could sleep, and a community midwife who went above and beyond (even on her day off) to make sure I was safe and getting the help I needed.

**[pbu21-0118]** — Reader context: Urges planning support and contact numbers before crisis.

> Please contact your midwife team if you’re struggling or go to A&E. If you’re prone to MH issues, make sure your partner and loved ones know what to look out for. I started on antidepressants when my daughter was about 4w. I’ve always refused them in the past. But I was so scared that at some point I might be so sick I would hurt her without meaning to. Have the numbers you need ready and to hand before you need them.

**[pbu32-0067]** — Reader context: Three months postpartum, looks back at tracking and anxiety preventing delegation.

> PP was the hardest for me, Tracking each feed and poop made the day feel endless, Not being able to zone out or take much needed breaks was so hard, Feeling touched out and losing myself each day was burning me out so much i reached out to a therapist and to family for help but the anxiety and over protectiveness made it so hard to delegate i was a “cat mom”

**[pbu32-0107]** — Reader context: Follow-up accepts clinician's PPD/PPA assessment and disability extension.

> Now a few days out and talking more with my husband I’m really glad she did say something and I’m getting some more support.

### 6. Wanting motherhood, continuing this pregnancy, adoption and later trying again are separate decisions, with different forms of grief and relief.

**Current findings location:** §§1, 6, 9, 10 (decision/care experience); new reproductive-decision subsection.

**Relationship:** Extends; challenges an implicit starting assumption.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “The decision and care experience need their own treatment.”

**What should change:** Broaden 'decision' beyond the birth route. The reader may still be deciding whether to remain pregnant, even in a stable marriage or after infertility. Abortion can be described as the right decision, as traumatic, as regretted, or with no regret; miscarriage can be grieved after an unwanted pregnancy. Adoption or surrogacy can be contemplated or rejected. Do not collapse these accounts into a single emotional result or a moral rule, and do not assume that continuing is already decided.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0004]** — Reader context: Late discovery and travel requirements shape the decision to continue.

> I’m 31 weeks pregnant. I’m very small and I found out extremely late. I found out so late that my first instinct was an abortion. My husband and I have been married for a year and with everything going on in the world I didn’t think it was the best time. I had an abortion early on when we first wed. I found out and realized that I was so far along that I couldn’t get it done in my state and would have to travel so we decided to keep it. (He wanted to keep this and the last one).

**[pbu08-0024]** — Reader context: Cancer diagnosis forces termination and years without another pregnancy.

> I (34f) have a beautiful 21m old toddler at home and all I wanted to do was give them a sibling. I was pregnant over the winter and had a miscarriage and finally got pregnant again in March. I was 7 weeks and having crazy shortness of breath..went to the hospital and was diagnosed with leukemia. I had to terminate my pregnancy at 8 weeks. Now I’m being told I will be in treatment for 4-5 years and can’t get pregnant during this time.

**[pbu08-0025]** — Reader context: Saved infant belongings become constant reminders of the hoped-for sibling.

> My whole life has been turned upside down. I kept all my toddlers infant things with the intention of using it all for my next baby and I just am constantly looking at it/thinking about it. I’m devastated. I don’t even care about the cancer. I care that I can’t give my baby a sibling.

**[pbu08-0078]** — Reader context: Partner shifts from wanting a child to preferring termination over career timing.

> I’m only about 5 weeks, and even though logically I know how this happened 😅 it still feels shocking. What makes it harder is that now my husband seems unsure. He says he supports whatever I choose, but leans more toward abortion because of the timing with us starting our careers.

**[pbu08-0079]** — Reader context: Makes and cancels appointments while feeling frozen by either permanent choice.

> I keep making appointments and then backing out at the last minute because I feel frozen. I am stressed trying to make a decision that feels so permanent either way.

**[pbu08-0129]** — Reader context: Difficult prior pregnancies and an uncertain future shape a grieving termination decision.

> I just ordered the pills off of aid access and i feel so heavy and devastated. this is not what i wanted. i know it's the right choice though. i have 2 under two and this was a major slip up on my husband. pregnancy was so awful for me both times i'd never be able to go through it for a third time. we are getting out of military next year so who knows what the future holds especially with two kids. i feel so selfish doing this and this awful heavy feeling in my chest. my poor innocent baby. it hits so much harder since i have two.  before i had kids i probably wouldn't have been this worked up about it, but growing life two times and going through pregnancy- it's changed me. this is my first and last abortion and i am just so devastated. what kind of personality would my baby have? would they look like me? would they look just like their brother and sister? i can't believe I'm taking this life away but i know deep down it's the best decision. my punishment will be having this live with this choice for the rest of my life. sorry i just needed to get this off my chest.

**[pbu08-0171]** — Reader context: Wedding, travel and prior emotionally difficult abortion complicate a new pregnancy.

> we have not told anyone. this happened to us a little over a year ago and we decided to abort as we were not even engaged yet- so i am not against the decision but the last time i went through a lot emotionally and said id never do it again. here i am now considering doing it again, but heavily weighing my options as this baby would be loved, and part of me is excited at the idea of having our first baby be in attendance at our wedding. i have a fear of missing out on the fun things that come with a wedding- such as drinking and partying with our friends. we also had travel plans this year that we would not be able to follow through with if i decide to keep this baby. the timing just absolutely sucks, and im 50/50 on keeping it. i am struggling so hard.

**[pbu08-0208]** — Reader context: Grief, couple time and fear of losing another chance make an unplanned pregnancy hard to decide.

> Now I'm pregnant, I have no idea which way to go and it's killing me. He is 60 /40 to have an abortion. I had one a few years ago and ended up struggling with some regret even though it was the right decision at the time, so don't love the idea of doing it again. But alas here we are! I would  be very sad to miss out on special pre-kid time with my partner. Also think giving us both a moment to breath and enjoy life after the past year is important for our relationship and our own mental health!
> 
> But obviously I'm terrified if I terminate that I won't get another chance and I will regret it forever. Plus the idea of having a baby also makes me really happy (and terrified). Anyone had a similar experience and have any advice? Thank you

**[pbu08-0356]** — Reader context: Prior unsupported abortion makes current decision-making feel like reliving trauma.

> that’s all the practical stuff, but the dilemma for me is everything i feel emotionally.  
> i went through an abortion at 12 weeks a couple years ago and it was extremely hard on me physically and mentally. i really wanted that baby no matter the circumstances but i had no support from my partner at the time nor my family.   
> it’s shaped me into the person i am today and i feel like i’m reliving that trauma now every time i try to decide if i should go ahead with this pregnancy or not.

**[pbu08-0391]** — Reader context: Feels caught between readiness for parenthood now and uncertainty about another chance later.

> My biggest fear about having an abortion is not necessarily that I don’t want to be a mother, I do, but that I might later want a baby and be unable to conceive. As I am overweight. I am also afraid I would feel guilty or grieve the baby I didn’t choose to have.  
> My biggest fear about continuing the pregnancy is that I may not be ready or capable of giving the child the life and stability I want for them, and that they could eventually suffer or resent me.  
> Ultimately, I feel caught between two futures I could both imagine wanting: becoming a mother now, which feels frightening and earlier than planned, or ending this pregnancy and trying for a baby later, which feels closer to the life I originally envisioned but carries the fear and uncertainty of letting this pregnancy go.

**[pbu21-0036]** — Reader context: Prior dangerous births shaped an earlier termination decision.

> I have two healthy children with my husband. A 4 year old and 18 month old. This past winter I fell pregnant, had a mental breakdown because both births almost killed me, and I made the decision with my husband to terminate. I had a really hard time coming to that decision but ultimately for a multitude of reasons, it felt like the right one for me.

**[pbu21-0037]** — Reader context: Another unplanned pregnancy reopens fear of traumatic surgery and suicidal postpartum depression.

> What do I do? It feels so wrong to terminate again but I was trying to prevent this. Part of me feels like God really wants me to have a third baby. But I had two very traumatic C-sections and I had PPD and was suicidal during my last postpartum. What can I do? What should I do? I am so lost.

**[pbu21-0080]** — Reader context: Knows logically that ambivalence did not cause miscarriage but still blames herself.

> I never thought that I would be a mom to begin with. My first baby was a complete surprise and the most beautiful thing that had ever happened to me. I found out I was pregnant a couple of weeks ago. I honestly didn’t know how to feel. My baby just turned one a few months ago. I was not expecting to be pregnant so soon. I know it’s not logical but, I can’t help but feel that my mixed emotions caused this miscarriage. I was overjoyed when I found out I was pregnant with my first baby. I was a bag of mixed emotions with my second. I know logically I didn’t cause this but, I can’t help the way I feel. I’m not even sure why I’m making this post.

**[pbu21-0219]** — Reader context: Sixteen-year-old describes family and religious pressure to continue pregnancy.

> I don’t know what to do, in my state you need parental consent to get an abortion and my parents are Jehovahs Witnesses and our religion abortions are strictly forbidden. im panicking , I can’t raise a kid and my boyfriend can’t either. all of my family agrees with my dad I dont know what to do! i want to go to college and have a career I can’t raise a kid

**[pbu21-0237]** — Reader context: Grieves likely loss despite not initially wanting pregnancy.

> This was an uplanned pregnancy as I just got married 3 months and most likely won’t be successful according to my gynaec. Will get another blood test done in 48 hours to see if it will double. But I’m crying and getting emotional, I don’t understand why. Is this normal? I didn’t even want the baby but suddenly I’m feeling guilty…

**[pbu21-0352]** — Reader context: Wishes for more time and mental health support before abortion.

> I know logically that having another pregnancy may be possible, but emotionally I’m struggling with the fact that I *was* pregnant, and I made the decision to end it. There are moments where I desperately wish I could go back and give myself more time, get better mental health support, and figure out what I actually wanted before making such a permanent decision.

**[pbu21-0353]** — Reader context: Supportive marriage does not resolve uncertainty over wanting children.

> I’m happily married, and my partner is incredibly supportive. He is willing to work through all of this with me and has been there for me emotionally. So this isn’t about not having a supportive partner or having doubts about our relationship. If anything, having his support makes me realize how much of this is something I need to work through within myself.

**[pbu21-0362]** — Reader context: Seventeen-year-old names $110 pill cost and asks for cheaper termination options.

> We live in a state where abortions are illegal, and my mom is heavily against abortion (believes it’s murder and yada yada). Neither my boyfriend or I make enough money to move out and support a baby right now. I’m guessing I’m about a month pregnant as I got my period in the beginning of July. I was considering ordering abortion pills from a neighboring state as I don’t need parental consent to buy them, and they’re about 110 dollars. However I’m unsure of how far along I am and feel insane guilt aborting the baby. Is there any advice anyone could offer me on how to feel better about my decision? Is there a cheaper way to terminate the pregnancy?

**[pbu21-0407]** — Reader context: Repeated pregnancy follows traumatic abortion with competing fears about both choices.

> My first abortion was probably the most traumatizing experience of my entire life. I don’t think I’ve even begun to properly process it. I’ve been drinking every single day since, and mentally, I’ve been having an extremely difficult time.  
> Part of me feels like having another abortion would completely destroy me emotionally, but another part of me knows that having a baby with this man would mean being permanently tied to someone who has repeatedly abused, cheated on, and disrespected me.  
> I’m also terrified of potentially raising a child alone, especially after seeing how little effort he puts into being a father to the child he already has.  
> I feel completely stuck between two decisions that both seem impossible to live with.

**[pbu21-0409]** — Reader context: Physical recovery remains incomplete when another pregnancy starts at four months postpartum.

> Anyways here’s the dilemma. My partner has left it up to me what we do. He says we want 2 and he’s happy if we keep the baby, but it’s also my body & I should do what’s best for me.  
> Sounds simple, but I’ve never wanted to have an abortion. Trust me I’m pro-choice, but I never thought I’d be in this situation. At the same time, I’m not healed at all. I’ve heard it takes 18m. I had preeclampsia, then 2 weeks after birth I was in and out of hospital. I just started feeling better physically like 2 weeks ago & even that’s not completely better. The past 2 days I’ve been feeling depleted, which makes sense now. But even if I can mentally do two under one, I don’t think I physically can. I think ik what I have to do, but I’m having trouble coming to terms with it.

**[pbu32-0085]** — Reader context: Objects to uniformly joyful pregnancy-test advertising.

> Not all of us would be so happy and joyful over a positive result. Where's the clips representing the rest of us?
> 
> Where are the ads of women in absolute horror, swearing in frustration and disbelief, and immediately booking in for an abortion?

**[pbu32-0154]** — Reader context: Pregnancy after infertility is wanted in principle but not with current partner; distinct passage from concurrently quoted crosspost.

> So finding out I’m pregnant naturally was a complete shock. But, It honestly broke my heart because this is something I always wanted… but it’s not with the person I still love. Madly love.

**[pbu32-0162]** — Reader context: Continued pregnancy after canceling abortion decision; partner expects happiness about baby girl.

> How can I be excited when I almost ended this pregnancy? I can’t pick out names and be happy when this baby almost didn’t exist and I feel so guilty.

**[pbu32-0172]** — Reader context: Nineteen-year-old surprised by grief after unwanted pregnancy and reported miscarriage.

> Did anyone else experience this after a miscarriage they didn't want? Why does it hurt emotionally even when the pregnancy wasn't planned or wanted?

**[pbu32-0214]** — Reader context: Two men urge abortion; author considers adoption if unable to parent.

> I just know that I need to make the choice that I can live with not the choice other people want me to make.

**[pbu32-0224]** — Reader context: Seventeen-year-old has supportive family and legal access but strong grief and guilt.

> I know i will get the abortion, but does anyone have advice how to cope now, during and after the abortion?

**[pbu32-0241]** — Reader context: Does not regret prior abortion but fears missing future chance of motherhood at thirty-seven.

> Has anyone else found the right partner later in life and gone on to have a healthy pregnancy in their late 30s or even 40s? Or has anyone else struggled with these feelings after a medical abortion?

**[pbu32-0243]** — Reader context: May be pregnant despite implant; debt and new job shape decision to terminate.

> I feel so preemptively guilty about this, I’ve always wanted to be a mum and it feels like both things can’t be true.

**[pbu32-0292]** — Reader context: Casual worker with job-seeking boyfriend requests honest accounts of both decisions.

> If you terminated, did you regret it? If so, did that feeling change over time?  
> If you continued the pregnancy, did you initially feel unprepared or wish you’d terminated, and how do you feel about your decision now?

**[pbu32-0332]** — Reader context: Prior abortion without regret coexists with current desire for future motherhood.

> It was the right decision for where we were in life, and I don't regret making the best choice we could at the time.

### 7. Some partners control reproduction, force sex, assault pregnant or recovering parents, and threaten the newborn.

**Current findings location:** §5 (partners), §6, §10 For 6 and partner checklist; §17.7 selection gap.

**Relationship:** Contradicts the remaining broad partner framing.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “From her side, the gap is mental load more than absence:”

**What should change:** Replace this lead with separate accounts of ordinary unequal labor, unsupported parenting, and coercion or violence. An unread book or a short task list cannot explain forced sex, ejaculation after a refusal, assaults, threats to cause an abortion, a $20K inducement to terminate, threats to remove the newborn, or preventing purchased supplies from entering the home. Pressure can be to continue, terminate or adopt. The record also includes a pregnant author's admission that she hit her partner; retain it. Delivery-room exclusion may be a response to fear of the partner. These selected accounts do not establish which partner pattern is most common; the contradiction concerns the breadth of the framing.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0041]** — Reader context: Asks whether an abdominal punch warrants care.

> I got punched in the stomach today. I’m 16 weeks pregnant. It was right above my belly button and my bump is below my belly button. Could this have hurt my baby or would she only be where the bump is? Should I get medical attention?

**[pbu08-0047]** — Reader context: Nursery preparation escalates into frightening physical aggression.

> Everything came to a head while we were working on our baby’s nursery. We disagreed about where to place a wall stencil. He was right about the first placement, so we started over. While lining it up again, I told him it needed to shift to the right. After we taped it down, it still wasn’t where I thought it should be, so I said, “I told you to shift it to the right.” He became increasingly upset, grabbed me by both shoulders while yelling, and I remember telling him multiple times to let go because I was scared. He eventually did.

**[pbu08-0128]** — Reader context: After leaving an abusive partner, gives birth alone, stays with family and reports a happy later life.

> Hello everyone!!! I posted this over 4 years ago! I found out I was pregnant shortly after it happened. I haven’t seen that guy since and he hasn’t been involved. He got arrested and we got a restraining order, didn’t hear from him again. I gave birth alone and stayed with family for a while. My daughter is amazing and so smart, my son looks just like me like I made him by myself lol. Everyone is so happy and amazing and life is great.

**[pbu08-0131]** — Reader context: After assault and bleeding, withholds the cause from hospital because of prior disbelief.

> My partner physically assaulted me a few weeks postpartum (dragged and punched me in the back). I later went to hospital due to heavy bleeding after this. I didn’t tell the hospital what had truly happened since they had a track record of not believing me anyways, and I just felt too scared and depressed to do anything anymore. He also refused to take his full paid parental leave and went back to work after only 2 weeks postpartum.

**[pbu08-0183]** — Reader context: Partner reverses promises of support after a positive test and pressures termination.

> Just a week ago i asked him what would happen if we got pregnant because of what we were doing and that it would be scary and if we’d be ready to have a child considering we are young. he said everything would be ok. He’s able to take care of the both of us and that i wouldn’t have to work just take care of baby. Fast forward, this week i have been feeling sick, took a pregnancy test and boom positive. i tell him and he is now forcing me to abort my baby, telling me i am a horrible person for not wanting to do so, telling me he wants nothing to do with me or my pregnancy, telling me im ruining his life if i have this child, telling me im an idiot and can’t think logically, telling me he doesn’t want a baby with ME specifically and that i am not the mother of his child. lol.

**[pbu08-0263]** — Reader context: At six months, describes physical injuries, shame outside home, verbal abuse and financial dependence.

Nakakahiya tuwing lumalabas ako na nakikita ako ganito ng ibang tao 😭 and always pinag sasalitaan ng masasakit na salita ng partner ko, minumura at palaging sinasabeng malas ako 😭🥹 Pati mga needs ko sinusumbat nya palagi at sasabihing wala akong pera. Sana hindi nya na lang ako kinuha sa nanay ko kung ganito lang din 🥺 And nakikipag kita sa ex nyang kupal din na tinotolerate wrong doings nya and proud pa ex nya na sasaktan ako. Napapatanong ako minsan kung deserve koba lahat to, wala naman ako masamang ginawa towards them.

**[pbu08-0266]** — Reader context: Partner shifts from support to a monetary offer and threat of disappearance if pregnancy continues.

Now that I’m leaning toward keeping it, he’s completely changed—he says he can’t handle another child, feels overwhelmed, and is now strongly pressuring me to abort. It’s escalated to offering me money ($20K) to terminate and saying he’ll disappear if I continue.

**[pbu08-0312]** — Reader context: Author reports hitting her partner during an argument and feeling ashamed.

**[pbu08-0336]** — Reader context: Stores feeding equipment and recovery supplies in a hot car or storage unit at the partner's insistence.

Just today, I decided to just bring in the bottle sterilizer, my pumps, baby bottles, some creams, & postpartum supplies and got lots of negative remarks and feedback on that. He told me to put the stuff back in my car. I don’t want the things to get damaged and it’s exhausting running back and forth to a storage unit alone putting baby items back and forth.
