### 8. Partners can provide extensive practical care while experiencing their own fear, grief, regret or need for support.

**Current findings location:** §5 (other side), §9 (partners), §17.6.

**Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “From his side, he wants to help and doesn't know how:”

**What should change:** Add the partner as a person affected by the pregnancy, emergency or loss, rather than only a support role to train. One father does the chores, attends appointments and privately feels he has destroyed his life. Partners can grieve differently, fear future pregnancy or need help processing events. Include the pregnant wife supporting her grieving wife and the two-mother family with a supportive partner but insufficient nearby help. A useful response must not assume every partner is male, uninformed or unwilling.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0044]** — Reader context: After a mediated conversation, reports the husband understanding and apologizing.

> But anyways husband and I had an amazing conversation and he's finally understanding the severity of everything going on with me. When I explained everything yesterday he cried with me and apologized for making things worse on me. I believe he is actually understanding my situation and it has made me feel so much better that he used his brain and realized he was wrong.
> Thank you everyone for the comments I have read them all.

**[pbu08-0139]** — Reader context: Partner's traumatic recollection changes plans for another birth and prompts adoption or surrogacy discussion.

> I feel like I have gained an understanding for what happened now. We would love to have another baby,I want to experience pregnancy again ,give my baby another sibling and grow as a family. I feel I would be selfish of me to want to do that you know? My husband had made it clear yes to another baby but that was extremely traumatic for him. I understand like I said I don’t remember any of that but I respect that. We’d rather have me here than not. So we talked about adoption and or having a surrogate . Adoption seems easiest route financially and we have lots of love to give. Having a surrogate could be up to $200k based off others experiences who were able to do that route. Has anyone ever gone through this or something similar? What are your thoughts?

**[pbu08-0152]** — Reader context: Parents grieve a loss at twenty-three weeks while grandparents care for the older children.

> Dads, long time lurker here. My wife and I have 2 beautiful kids, 12 & 3. She was pregnant but we just lost our baby at 23 weeks. We are both distraught and heartbroken, it’s hard to eat and sleep at this point. Our 2 kids are with grandparents at the moment but we are going to regroup and be strong for them, but that doesn’t take the pain away.

**[pbu08-0153]** — Reader context: Seeks affordable ways to support his wife's grief on Mother's Day.

> What are some ideas to help my wife grieve while still celebrating her Mother’s Day with our 2 kids? Money is a barrier for us but we can still afford to go out if that’s the best course of action. I don’t really care if we do celebrate, I just want what’s best for her at this moment. I am so lost at this point and could use the ideas. Thank you all

**[pbu08-0154]** — Reader context: Practical support and preparation coexist with deep paternal regret and limited emotional help.

> We are six months along now, I'm still really struggling. Most days I feel pretty alright. I try to think of the positives, but some days I just feel like I've completely fucked up my life because I couldn't just man up and break up. So far, the pregnancy has been wretched, she has been sick the entire time, probably with hyperemesis. I have been very supportive of course, I have picked up all the house work, I've been going to all appointments, engaged in the future planning and trying to fix up the house to be ready for when the baby arrives. I haven't been wallowing in self pity, but I still struggle deeply internally. I have a psychologist, but it doesn't help much, and most of my male friends are classic emotionally insensitive guys or just a bit unhelpful, so it's hard to talk to anyone. I am also just embarrassed by all this. everyone is so happy when we tell them, and I'm here thinking "haha yeah how lovely I've destroyed my life".

**[pbu08-0155]** — Reader context: Past loss and paid-only support complicate a father's wish for another child.

> Some trauma. We could technically have three kids by now. An early miscarriage. A perfect baby, home run. And around a year and a half ago we went through losing a pregnancy in one of the worst ways imaginable. I have my home run baby. I sometimes feel like expecting another is just greedy, gambling with my wife's health.
> We're totally on our own. MIL comes around a bit but everything that resembles help, support, etc. is paid. Nanny, sitters, etc.

**[pbu08-0220]** — Reader context: Partner feels helpless about rib pain that keeps both awake.

> One minute she’s laughing at my jokes, the next minute she’s mad at me because I breathed too loudly or fell asleep before her. She wants me awake with her half the night because she says her ribs hurt when she lies down, and honestly I feel helpless watching her uncomfortable like that. Is rib pain a common discomfort around this stage normal for pregnant women?

**[pbu08-0221]** — Reader context: Feeling movement makes impending fatherhood feel real.

> But hearing the baby move for the first time? Man… that changed me completely. I just sat there shocked. It really made me realize how amazing women’s bodies are and how deep all this actually is. Respect to every mother honestly.

**[pbu21-0087]** — Reader context: A routine 35-week appointment changed date, team and hospital without warning.

> The long story is this: my partner (40F) and I (40F) live approximately 4-5 hours away from our family.  We both have full time jobs, friend groups, and have purchased a home where we currently live.  We went in for a routine ultrasound and NST at 35 weeks.  We anticipated that l would have a scheduled c section at 37 weeks due to a previous uterine surgery. However, big shocker, I was told I had a chorioamniotic separation during the visit and that I needed to have my son that day (or the next day), not with my original OB team and not at the hospital we planned to deliver due to how early it was and the potential need for the NICU (our original hospital had a lower level of care and no room).  C section is had, baby boy is born with some breathing trouble (all resolved), and a little trouble feeding.  I’m discharged after 4 days, baby discharged from NICU and sent home after 5 days.

**[pbu21-0088]** — Reader context: Newborn hospitalizations prompt reconsideration of job and distance from family.

> All of this is to say: we’ve had a rough fee days of motherhood. While my partner is very much with me in all respects, I’m realizing now that I need the additional support of family.  However, our families are largely unable to travel to us - my parents are still working, my extended family is elderly and/or disabled.  This has made me consider finding a new job and moving back home.

**[pbu21-0154]** — Reader context: Young father feels trapped despite reassurance.

> Last Saturday I just found out my girlfriend was pregnant. We are both 22 and just about to finish college. Ever since then I’ve only had panic attacks and when I’m calm I’m depressed. She had an iud that failed so we were definitely not planning it. Everyone around me is telling me that everything will be fine and only go one day at a time. That doesn’t make me feel better at all. It’s like I’m in a dark tunnel with no way out of it. If anyone has a similar experience to me please let me know.

**[pbu21-0155]** — Reader context: Names pay, savings and graduate-school fears.

> First off, I’m worried about my finances. I work as a waiter that pays decently well(500-800 on average). It doesn’t come with any benefits but it has allowed me to have a decent saving (upwards of 30k). I just don’t know if it’ll be enough. I’m worried about whether or not I’ll be able to go to grad school. I’m worried that I won’t have any time for myself as selfish as it sounds. This all just came out of left field.

**[pbu21-0156]** — Reader context: Reports moving hospitals during maternal ICU care to reach a trusted obstetrician.

> After a few days in the ICU, my wife asked me to transfer her to another hospital because she was getting more stressed with the OBs assigned to her. I signed a waiver and moved her to a hospital where her trusted OB-GYN could take care of her.

**[pbu21-0157]** — Reader context: Reports another hospital transfer to afford emergency surgery.

> But the cost was very high, so we transferred again to another hospital that was more affordable.

**[pbu21-0158]** — Reader context: Father recalls being asked who should be prioritized during surgery.

> “If something happens during surgery, who do you want us to prioritize saving your wife or your child?”

**[pbu21-0260]** — Reader context: Pregnancy intensifies affection and gratitude for partner.

> Everything he does seems endearing. I keep thinking about how lucky I am to have him, how excited I am to raise a child with him, and how grateful I am that he’s my partner. It’s almost overwhelming.

**[pbu21-0266]** — Reader context: Pregnant wife wants space for her own grief alongside her spouse’s.

> At the same time, I’m 28 weeks pregnant, taking care of our 2-year-old, trying to support my wife through the loss of her mom, and honestly struggling myself.  
> My wife and I have had some ups and downs recently. During one conversation, she told me she feels like she has to choose between grieving her mom and saving our marriage. Hearing that broke my heart because I never wanted her to feel that way. At the same time, I feel like whenever I try to express my own struggles, it gets interpreted as me criticizing her ability to grieve or somehow making it about me.  
> The truth is, I’m grieving too. I’m scared too.  
> I’m exhausted. I don’t feel like myself. My mental health has been suffering. I feel lonely most days. On top of everything else, there are things happening at work that make me unsure if I’ll even have a job by the end of the year.  
> I feel guilty even writing this because I know my wife is going through one of the hardest things a person can experience. But I also feel like I’m drowning and don’t know where to put my own feelings. I spend so much time trying to be strong for everyone else that I don’t know what to do when I need someone to be there for me.  
> I don’t want my wife to grieve less. I just wish there was room for my pain too.

**[pbu21-0341]** — Reader context: Pregnancy number differs between partners, changing the shared sense of first experiences.

> I’m grieving the fact that I see, day in and day out, that this isn’t my partner’s first baby.  
> I imagined tummy kisses. I imagined him talking to our baby. I imagined him being fascinated by every little thing I was experiencing and asking me lots of questions about what it felt like.  
> Instead, sometimes I feel like I’m experiencing this enormous, life-changing thing almost by myself.  
> And the hardest part is that my partner is someone who is so emotionally in tune. He understands feelings, communicates well and shows me love in many ways.

**[pbu32-0114]** — Reader context: Partner reports support starting in pregnancy; current pressures concern toddler-era work and finances.

> Since the pregnancy, I have been doing most of the household chores—cooking, laundry, lawncare, shopping, dusting, vacuuming, and dishes—as well as taking care of our disabled senior dog, so that my wife could focus on her wellbeing and finishing her dissertation.

**[pbu32-0115]** — Reader context: Partner processes birth emergency while wife and baby are in separate hospitals.

> As I didn’t process it all at the time, I recorded as much as I could, took notes, fed it all into Claude to help assist, and wow did this help so much. Getting it out of my head was a huge life saver, and I prolly would be so much worse if I didn’t do this.

**[pbu32-0116]** — Reader context: Update after ICU/NICU care and uncertain neurological prognosis.

> I took both my girls home today, and both are doing really well and they are both healhly.

**[pbu32-0118]** — Reader context: Recently had second child; says fathers receive little mental-health attention.

> Most of us only get a week or two off to help and support our wives with our newborn and try to make a new routine before we're straight back to work full time, worrying about money, juggling our time between our kids and wife, lack of sleep, as well as trying to find the time to do something for ourselves, which often gets overlooked.

### 9. Housework, food, sleep, newborn care and emotional support can remain with one parent even when the partner is present.

**Current findings location:** §§3, 5, 6, 10 For 6, 17.1.

**Relationship:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “Format helps the partner who wants to learn; it does not create the wish”

**What should change:** Keep the format limit and show what the missed support consists of: cooking during nausea, protected sleep, diapers, emotional recognition and use of paid leave. Presence at appointments does not establish engagement. A partner may refuse better-paid, shorter work hours or regard paid leave as vacation. These accounts support checking actual capacity and willingness, rather than making the pregnant parent responsible for managing another adult.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0042]** — Reader context: Pregnant stepparent describes symptoms and a husband's dismissal while caring for his children.

> I have been having a terrible pregnancy. Horrible morning sickness, constipation, depression, anxiety and rage! My body no longer feels like mine I don't feel like a person anymore and I am constantly uncomfortable, and the depression and rage are starting to pop up a lot. I'm incredibly exhausted and have slacked off of housley duties(I'm a SAHW) but this pregnancy has me exhausted and so have his kids. My husband loves to ask what's wrong and then tell me "pregnancy is not an excuse" and in a sense I understand that that is true, but in another sense he's not in my body, he's not feeling how tired, uncomfortable, depressed and angry I am. I feel if I didn't have his kids 24/7 things could be easier and I could get chores done, and not lash out so much. But taking care of 2 babies leaves no time for me to take care of myself. I know there are women out there that do it but I just don't have the strength they have, because this shit is tough. I'm at a loss and have no idea what to do. I also can't take my husband comparing my pregnancy to both of his BM pregnancies. I'm having an incredibly difficult time right now and need help.

**[pbu08-0043]** — Reader context: Asks for relief from childcare; the husband briefly puts down his phone, then resumes.

> I do see his kids as mine and I love them so much. He doesn't work those hours every day, that's when he's on call every other week. But when he comes home he immediately sits on the couch and watches tik tok. And it's exhausting taking care of them, I have asked him when he comes home to let me clock out, for him to take over so I can breathe. There has been so many time where he has come home and I have started dinner( which kills me because I'm pregnant and nauseous and cooking is a huge struggle) and the kids are behind me asking question after question. There was one time where this happened and all I did what turn around and told him "put your phone down I want to show you something" and as soon as he put his phone down the kids turned to him and started talking to him. That lasted for a solid 2 minutes before he picked his phone back up and they came right back to me.

**[pbu08-0046]** — Reader context: Wants dinner and household initiative during pregnancy rather than managing each task.

> What I wanted most was practical support at home. I asked if he could take over more household responsibilities and make sure we had dinner when I got home because I often was just exhausted. From my perspective, I still carried most of the mental load.

**[pbu08-0051]** — Reader context: A promised period of partner support becomes only one week off work.

> Everything since then has been a total shit show. My husband ended up taking a week off of work and went back on sunday. So I have been home by myself to care for her. Which wouldn't have been an issue had he not gassed it up that he would be here. So I sort of let my gaurd down.

**[pbu08-0052]** — Reader context: Insurance calls compete with feeding, pumping, changing and eating.

> I thought I was doing fine with it until today when I found out my insurance is all messed up. So I am having to call insurance while changing diapers and pumping. All the while I have my husband reminding me to eat. Which yes I get I need to eat but where do people find the time?? She's 2 weeks old today and up every 2ish hours to eat, change diapers, burp ect. And then I pump. That basically takes up all my time and then people also tell me to sleep.

**[pbu08-0088]** — Reader context: Recalls being left caring for five children, including newborn twins, after hospital discharge.

> i think maybe it’s resentmen? he is not much help and has barely showed me any compassio. when my twins were born he brought his son home the day I got out of the hospital. despite the fact that I told him that this is the closets to a break I’ll get and I just don’t want to be around kids. he would constantly leave his kids on me while I was pregnant and have me watch them. one time I was so sick i could barely move and he knew how sick I was. after work he leaves me with 5 kids. newborn twins, a onr year old, a 3 year old, and a 5 year old. he ended up going to his buddies house. not to mention alcohol has changed him so much. I feel like I am a single mom.

**[pbu08-0203]** — Reader context: Relatives prescribe movement and criticize sleep or ordered food during persistent vomiting.

> i genuinely feel insane, im 13 weeks pregnant and still throwing up almost everyday. I keep getting told by my mom, my fiancee, even his parents to move, get up, walk around but Its constant nausea and even going on car rides I throw up. Me and my fiancee are currently living with his parents and anytime im asleep during the day, I’ll hear about it the next. They got mad about me doordashing food when if I dont eat what I crave, I will throw up.

**[pbu08-0204]** — Reader context: Simple requests for water or food are met with sighs and groans.

> this whole pregnancy ive felt so unsupported my fiancee sighs and groans at me anytime I ask him to get up and fill up my water bottle, or get me strawberries out of the fridge in the morning so i dont throw up. 
> 
> I miss my old self already.

**[pbu08-0311]** — Reader context: Exhausted and hungry, asks partner to cook and hears his tiredness outweighs hers.

> money has been really tight while i’m waiting to get food stamps. there have been times where he’ll spend the last of his money on nicotine, weed, or a sandwich for himself while i’m hungry. one time we had ground beef at home and i hadnt eaten anything all day, but i was so exhausted and nauseous that i couldn’t bring myself to cook it. i asked if he could make it, and he said no. i work full time too. when i told him i was too tired to cook, he said, “oh yeah, you’re tired? try doing what i have to do every day.” (he works in a warehouse 40 hours a week)

**[pbu08-0313]** — Reader context: Partner initially rejects delivery-room support, then says he might stay to protect the baby.

> today we were watching big mouth, and there was an episode about a baby being born. he said he didn’t want to be in the delivery room because he didn’t want to deal with me being upset snd screaming and crying. then he changed it and said he’d probably stay because he didn’t want them taking the baby anywhere after it was born. what hurt was that it felt like the reason he’d stay was for the baby, not for me.

**[pbu21-0097]** — Reader context: Preparedness for baby and birth does not resolve fear of unsupported mental illness.

> I can’t even understand completely what is going wrong, because I have everything ready for the baby, and I am overall mentally prepared to give birth. Nothing about those things makes me anxious. What does make me anxious is worrying that my husband won’t know how to support me postpartum. I tried to explain to him that my mental health might get rocky, but he thinks I’m basically telling him that I’m going to turn into a bitch and use him as a punching bag. That was mostly because I told him that my depression usually manifests as irritability and anger, so I need him to be gentle with me postpartum if I get upset and help to watch for these things. He basically gets really offended and takes it personally when I have these episodes, and says mental health isn’t an excuse to be an asshole. It’s not, but it means the person needs help and intervention, and not somebody to make it worse. He always makes it worse. I told him that the hormone crash after I give birth might cause me to have some issues, and I just need to know that I can trust him to emotionally support me.

**[pbu21-0131]** — Reader context: Fears recurrent depression with partner refusing a better job and shorter hours.

> My fiance only got a week off work after baby was born and even though he got a better job offer with better pay and way more reasonable hours ($5/hr more and steady 10hr days instead of 12hr days) he still refuses to leave his current job and signed a contract for another several months after I begged him not to. It’s like he wanted to have a baby just to be able to say he’s a dad and now she and I are both just accessories to him and the novelty of parenthood has worn off.

**[pbu21-0132]** — Reader context: Recalls paid leave treated as vacation and no care on returning home.

> The way he treated me postpartum was so awful that I cannot go one day without thinking about it and I have told him that. When I told him that, he got offended that I would say such a thing. He watched me have two natural births, he watched me in excruciating pain two separate times. And even after seeing me like that, he still wouldn't even change a diaper in the hospital. Hell? He wouldn't even go into Walmart to get me my mommy diapers!  He wouldn't do anything, literally not one single thing to help me when we got home also. He wouldn't keep wood in the fire, he wouldn't even bring wood in for me to add myself, he wouldn't hold the baby so I could shower. He got paid leave that he called a "free vacation."  He expected dinner the night we got home, didn't do a single chore to help out. He never went to a single pregnancy or baby appointment. Never made dinner, never went shopping, I can't think of one thing he did besides go to work sometimes and pay the bills, the easiest part if you ask me.

**[pbu21-0228]** — Reader context: Requests to stop vaping nearby and take over risky chores fail.

> We argue about everything. Primarily about his vaping, drinking (i wish he’d slow down, but he still drinks every night) and housework. I also don’t want him to vape around me (I don’t want to breathe it in, and the smell makes me nauseous) but he continues to do it. I feel like I’m cleaning up after him constantly. I do the dishes, laundry, and all other housework. He’ll do a load of his own laundry if he needs something washed, or a load of dishes if I ask him, but never on his own. I asked him to start cleaning the litter box, only to discover 4 days later he still had not, so i ended up cleaning it despite dr’s recommendation to stop due to the risks to the baby. I’m so tired, i work full time, i feel like shit. I tried apologizing to him about my attitude, and he just said ‘you can keep apologizing, but it gets to a point. You act like everything is my fault, you gripe at me like my mom.” I can’t stop crying. I don’t know what I’m doing. I’m scared for what life will look like when the baby comes.

**[pbu21-0365]** — Reader context: Partner responds to distress by criticizing crying and refusing a requested hug.

> My partner tells me I’m being overdramatic during these breakdowns. That I need to stop crying so he can help. That he hates when I get like this cause it makes him feel bad. I’ll tell him all I need is a hug and I need his help but he will tell me that he can’t. He also tells me it’s like being with a teenager the amount that i cry. That he hasn’t been with someone who cries as much as i do.

> Almost immediately after she was born, my husband completely changed. He became distant, emotionally unavailable, slept constantly, avoided spending time with me, and stopped showing affection.

### 10. Disclosure and relatives can bring support, judgement, unwanted exposure or isolation, especially after loss.

**Current findings location:** §§2 (secret), 3, 5 (relatives), 6 (visitors), 16.1, 17.4.

**Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “Helpers vs visitors is the distinction she draws:”

**What should change:** Add control of the announcement, whether to disclose loss, religious disagreement, racialized remarks and the hoped-for celebration itself. Early disclosure can secure support or produce guarded reactions that intensify loneliness. A rule to wait until twelve weeks does not fit everyone; buying, naming and telling can be emotionally costly after loss. Helpers can be valuable, intrusive, conditional or unable to travel. Bereavement in the wider family can coexist with pregnancy and newborn care.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0168]** — Reader context: Mother calls delayed announcement ridiculous after a previous ectopic pregnancy.

> This escalated because the last time I was “critical” was when I told her I was upset at how she was treating my early pregnancy. The usual, she wanted to share with lots of people and I was adamant about waiting until 12 weeks, mainly because I had an ectopic last year and was very anxious. She kept saying I was “ridiculous” for not sharing, that the extended family had a “right to know,” that I needed to learn to manage my anxiety, etc. And I was being “critical” for saying that I thought she was being inconsiderate of my feelings, especially after my previous loss.

**[pbu08-0196]** — Reader context: Sister-in-law greets pregnancy news with a reference to her own miscarriage.

> When I did tell her, she said “hopefully you don’t miscarry like I did”.

**[pbu08-0197]** — Reader context: Relative invites herself to appointments and talks as though she will attend delivery.

> When I would have appointments, she’d INVITE HERSELF and she’s been walking around talking about how she’s going to act in the delivery room even though I’ve made it SUPER CLEAR she’s not going to be there.

**[pbu08-0240]** — Reader context: Father's wish for miscarriage confirms a boundary against newborn visits.

> My father also told me he hopes I miscarry which solidified my decision.

**[pbu08-0241]** — Reader context: Relative interprets diabetes management as a way to become thin postpartum.

> We finally ended up talking about gestational diabetes and what I had to track for my midwife and doctor, both excercise and diet in another phone call. She then said: wow, if you really stick with this you'll be soooo thin and fit after giving birth. 
> 
> I told her that I wasn't dieting and exercising to lose weight right now, but to handle my diabetes. And that it isn't advised for pregnant people to lose weight. She said ah, yeah... that's right. She has 3 kids of her own.

**[pbu08-0242]** — Reader context: Relative postpones a gift in case the baby dies.

> She said she wanted to gift us something she appreciated with her kids but she didn't want to buy it before I gave birth in case the kid died during these last months or during birth. I was too stunned to think of a proper answer so I said in that case we either have to store the things somewhere or sell a lot of unused baby things.

**[pbu08-0251]** — Reader context: Pregnancy changes others' assumptions about touching, commenting and giving instructions.

> Before I started showing I wasn’t prepared for how quickly everyone would decide that the normal rules about social interaction no longer apply to me. People who would never touch a stranger suddenly feel comfortable touching my stomach. People who know absolutely nothing about my medical history now have vocal opinions about what I’m eating, drinking, lifting, or planning for birth.

**[pbu08-0252]** — Reader context: Unsolicited horror stories arrive during mundane errands.

> And oh my God, everyone has a story. Not a nice story, either. It’s always a detailed account of the worst obstetrical outcome they’ve personally witnessed. I’ve had complete strangers tell me about stillbirths, emergency C-sections, birth injuries, and NICU stays while I’m standing in line trying to buy groceries or walking to my car.

**[pbu08-0318]** — Reader context: Wants private adjustment time but questions whether early recovery will require more help.

> So, given that context, I felt like she didn't have the right to demand space at my home for her and her two dogs when I'm in labor and freshly post partum. My husband and I want this to be a safe and sacred time bonding and adjusting with our baby. Is asking for 2 weeks alone unreasonable? I struggle with this so much because I don't want to hurt her but I'm just tired of people pleasing. I'm thinking of asking her to come after my husband goes back to work. Will I REALLY need more help those first two weeks? (Although I suspect she wants to come for selfish reasons). I don't want to deal with a falling out or be gaslit, or called controlling or ridiculous. That's how she can operate sometimes. Man, I'm 32 years old, why is this so hard still?! Thanks in advance!

**[pbu08-0409]** — Reader context: Moving for family support means accepting repeated body criticism during pregnancy.

> The only issue is my mom is a difficult person to be around. She's been very critical of me this entire pregnancy. Making comments about my weight, eating habits, catasptrophizing every symptom I've had, name calls me, and ruins every baby name. I was 118lb and only gained 20 pounds this whole pregnancy... I've struggled with an ED before, so some of the comments cut deep. I've laid down boundaries, asked her to stop multiple times and she won't. when called out on her behavior, she plays the victim and shuts down for hours or even days.

**[pbu21-0042]** — Reader context: Wants a joyful baby shower that includes a deceased mother.

> Im a ftm at 18, 32 weeks pregnant, and my baby shower for my baby girl is this Saturday. Im having trouble thinking of a way to honor my mom who passed away at the shower. I want to find a way to include her because i know she’d be thrilled about being a grandma, but preferably in a way that doesnt take away from it being a happy day (dont want like a full table of photos or anything). im not religious so i cant do the signs about watching from heaven, and im just really lost on what to do. anyone have any ideas?

**[pbu21-0074]** — Reader context: Widowhood during pregnancy combined grief with debilitating symptoms and toddler care.

> In March of 2025, I suddenly became a widow while 5 months pregnant with my second child. My other child was 2.5 years old. That pregnancy proceeded to be extremely physically and mentally debilitating. I developed polyhydramnios, severe pelvic pain, a horrible rash over most of my body, and had such bad heart burn that I was going through a bottle of tums every 2 days. On top of all of that, I was taking care of an extremely strong willed toddler and the summer heat that came a little later on made things 10x harder. Needless to say, I had so much on my plate that it seemed I didn't have time to properly grieve my husband.

**[pbu21-0076]** — Reader context: Mother-in-law requested a grandchild after miscarriage and later announced pregnancy first.

> This was about a month after I had had my first miscarriage. When she left, her parting words to me were, “I love you guys, give me a grandbaby soon” She also announced she was going to be a grandma on social media before we announced the pregnancy.

**[pbu21-0077]** — Reader context: A ten-minute post-birth visit centered photos and breastfeeding judgment.

> We didn’t have our baby girl until 4 years later, last fall. His mom didn’t check on me or the baby the entire pregnancy, but of course she wanted to come and see her once she was born. We allowed her to come and visit a couple weeks after the baby was born. She literally came in, held the baby, had my husband take pictures of her, told me I need to be breastfeeding and then left. Literally was there for 10 minutes. Then proceeded to post all over social media that she was soo in love with “her baby”

**[pbu21-0079]** — Reader context: Family illness discovered during pregnancy, with little support after the baby arrived.

> My family has gone through a major loss during this time; my husbands' father and only family. This has been the hardest part for our family. We found out his father had a glioblastoma brain tumor when I was in my second trimester. They tried surgery, that just took his ability to formulate sentences. Then the chemo which just took everything he had. He passed when our son was just 5 months. Devastated is an understatement. On top of that, there was painful family conflict afterward involving stolen inheritance, and it made an already terrible situation even more traumatic and complicated. What a mess, just when I wanted to snuggle and cuddle our baby. This has obviously had ripple effects since. So much for us to navigate and nowhere near enough support.

**[pbu21-0178]** — Reader context: Partner departure, opposed family and job loss frame pregnancy.

> My whole pregnancy has been a giant shitshow tbh, my partner of 3 years straight up leaving after I didn’t get an abortion, family who wouldn’t have even let me get an abortion, losing my job etc etc. but the most irritating thing is the racist remarks…..

**[pbu21-0179]** — Reader context: Reports racist assumptions about an absent father.

> From strangers all I hear is ‘I bet the dad is black’ Just bc he left. And it’s always these typical white guys who see themselves as better than others just bc of their skin colour that say this. It’s a disgusting stereotype first of all

**[pbu21-0180]** — Reader context: Family’s racial jokes drown out the author’s own answers.

> I’ve told them to stop, they don’t. Embarrassing me constantly. When we’re outside and see somebody we know, they will obviously ask me about the pregnancy, if I have a name yet, etc. But before I can say anything the ‘Yes he’s Chinese, his name is lingling’ starts and then everybody is laughing and talking over me while I try to explain the truth and just wanted to give an answer about MY son.

**[pbu21-0345]** — Reader context: Relatives shared uncertain pregnancy and demanded attendance despite sciatica.

> My MIL and SIL told me they wished my husband ended up with another woman a month before we got married. They told everyone about our pregnancy despite telling them not to tell anyone during my first trimester (it was suspected ectopic and things didn’t look good the first trimester). My SIL told me to “get over the sciatica pain and learn to walk because I want to know the baby’s gender and I don’t want you to cancel the gender reveal”.

**[pbu21-0354]** — Reader context: Generic advice about telling IVF relatives does not fit a sibling already pregnant.

> Hey yall, there’s a bunch of people who’ve already asked a similar question. And I’ve read those posts. But my bit of nuance here is that my sibling (F) is pregnant, too. So she used IVF, and most of the advice is for telling friends who are currently TTC with IVF, not specific for those who are pregnant. I imagine she will still have feelings and may need some space. I plan to text. Should I tell her I know she might want some space? Do I just tell her without caveats?

**[pbu32-0053]** — Reader context: Mother conditions postpartum support on older child staying elsewhere.

> What I can see is a lot of negativity from her regarding the whole situation and as if she has made up her mind to creat a difficult time for me unless I decide to part ways with my child for the initial 45 days. Which I am not comfortable doing, i told her I will miss him a lot and the stress of missing him might affect my milk supply too.

**[pbu32-0103]** — Reader context: Looks back at stepmother's reaction to delayed newborn visit.

> She made my own birth story and first two weeks postpartum about her and how 'hurt she was' that I didn't invite her over right away after a traumatic birth to meet my daughter.

**[pbu32-0128]** — Reader context: Mother-in-law expects possible attendance at large shower three days after due date.

> Maybe the baby will come a bit early and you'll be home and feeling great already LOL. Plus we can make it a group effort and all go together.

**[pbu32-0150]** — Reader context: Told family early to get support during sickness; responses instead increase anxiety.

> All they keep saying is “you told us too early” and “i’m not gonna be excited until the second trimester or later.” It makes me feel as if i did something wrong.

**[pbu32-0184]** — Reader context: Family abroad repeatedly request bump photos after previous thirteen-week loss.

> I know they come from a different generation but I simply feel too exposed and judged by a microscope. I don’t feel comfortable.

**[pbu32-0189]** — Reader context: Partner's travel creates need to disclose early despite ectopic and chemical pregnancy history.

> I’m only 7 and a half weeks and I want to tell my parents I’m pregnant so I’m not alone while he is away.

**[pbu32-0197]** — Reader context: Post title reports acquaintance's belief; body says acquaintance subsequently miscarried.

> When I told her to be careful telling people before 3 months, she told me that a miscarriage won't happen to her because they're healthy.

**[pbu32-0206]** — Reader context: Challenges secrecy rule as withholding support during sickness or loss.

> And also, if I were to get pregnant, and have a miscarriage, I would want a support system to lean on when something that life changing and sad happens, so that would make me more inclined to tell people pretty early on

**[pbu32-0261]** — Reader context: Mother's reply undermines announced no-kissing boundary for autumn newborn.

> My mom swiped up and said “she will be kissing him. Lol” i dont know if my hormones are on extra defense mode or what but that rubbed me the wrong way and as well as her calling the baby her baby.

**[pbu32-0299]** — Reader context: Living with supportive in-laws still creates loss of privacy and disappointed expectations.

> Our baby won't even have more than a small section in our room. We won't have the opportunity to just have alone time with the baby when the time comes.

### 11. Stillbirth and neonatal death include birth, pain relief, recovery, clothes, keepsakes, death paperwork and leaving without the baby.

**Current findings location:** §§2 (birth/first weeks), 5 (partners), 6, 10 For 5; new loss-and-bereavement subsection.

**Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “The first weeks include incapacity, feeding pressure and delayed connection.”

**What should change:** Develop the sequence rather than naming loss only as a historical exclusion. A father is shocked that his wife must labor; direct explanations and control of timing matter. Selecting the baby's only outfit, autopsy, organ donation, registration, cremation or burial, and returning home without the baby are part of the immediate birth experience. A memory box can comfort one partner and remain unbearable for the other. Keep gratitude for brief contact and keepsakes beside grief and anger. These quotes are accounts, not conclusions about which intervention would have prevented a death.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0156]** — Reader context: Told earlier arrival would not change the outcome, a father finds no comfort in this reassurance.

> The nurse who broke the news said that even if we had come earlier, the outcome would have been the same. That provided no comfort nor would it ever.

**[pbu08-0157]** — Reader context: Learns that confirmed fetal death is followed by labor and birth.

> After the phone calls, we get briefly explained the process from the nurses and I get the shock of my life. They mention key words and phrases like labour and ‘c section is more dangerous’. It dawns on me that my Wifey was going to have to give birth to our dead baby.

**[pbu08-0158]** — Reader context: Values compassionate, direct explanations and control over timing.

> To their credit, the nurses were very compassionate and straightforward with their outlining of the process. They maintained that we are in charge of the time frames and we can go as fast or as slow as we want.

**[pbu08-0159]** — Reader context: Wants plain facts rather than softened or spiritual reassurance.

> I do remember her saying the nurse was very forward and direct, which I appreciated at the time. In times like this, nothing will soften the blow. If anything, those beating around the bush or softening their language would really grind my gears. I just want facts. No bullshit. No god shit. No ‘she will be with you forever in your hearts’ shit. Just facts.

**[pbu08-0160]** — Reader context: Memory box comforts his wife while he cannot face looking inside it.

> Don’t get me wrong, I could see how this could be beneficial to others and how it can make someone feel. My Wifey liked it. That is what mattered to me more than my own disdain and we agreed to this box. To this day, I have not looked inside the box. Maybe 1 day. For now, I can’t. I won’t. I can’t fathom seeing a ‘certificate of life’ with her name on it. She was never alive. She never got to meet her Daddy.

**[pbu08-0161]** — Reader context: Many bought going-home outfits become the choice of a stillborn baby's only outfit.

> The nurses ask what clothes to put her in. Earlier in the week of the due date, I was going to the likes of Next and Zara to find the perfect ‘going home’ outfit. I couldn’t decide so bought so many different outfits. My Wifey asks me which one they should put her in and I choose the outfit. Tears stream down my face as I deliberate over which one. I choose such a cute floral one. 100% pure cotton. The only outfit she will ever wear in her life.

**[pbu08-0162]** — Reader context: After birth, must make immediate decisions about death registration, donation and funeral.

> We also have to discuss registering her death, organ donations and the funeral, amongst other heavy shit. Cremation or burial. No parent should ever have to even entertain this kind of conversation. Even in a ‘hypothetical’ sense. We must entertain it because we have to action it.

**[pbu08-0163]** — Reader context: Contrasts going home after the first birth with leaving after stillbirth.

> I left with my baby boy in his car seat when he was born. I left this time as I came. Just with my Wifey. Nobody spoke a word on the way back to the house.

**[pbu08-0292]** — Reader context: Passes baby and placenta at home immediately after being reassured this would not happen.

> Hello everyone, I(22F) recently had a miscarriage two days ago at 12 weeks 3 days. I spent hours in the ER and eventually was sent home being told i would follow up with OB the next day to remove the fetal tissue. I was scared to leave and worried it would come out at home but they assured me it wouldn’t. Within minutes of arriving in my house, my husband ran me a bath and instantly when i sat in it, the baby and placenta came out. Probably the most horrifying experience in my life.

**[pbu21-0061]** — Reader context: Retrospective account of interpreting slowing movement before scheduled induction.

> So on Monday I get a confirmation that my induction is scheduled to be on Friday afternoon. The week leading up to it was full of nerves, waiting for any signs of labor. I would get contractions here and there, as well as some aches and pains but never having consistent constant labor pains. Later in the week his kick counts have slowed, but still at the 10 kick check. He was also very weird about kicking. Some days very active, some pretty quiet. I chalked up the last two days of barely any movement normal.

**[pbu21-0062]** — Reader context: Believes earlier induction would have prevented loss and did not know she could question timing.

> I’m not blaming myself or the wonderful nurses on Friday. We’re mad that we waited. More specifically, we are furious at our OB/GYN. I felt stupid because she said she’d schedule us the following week. If we induced at 40 he would have been alive. My sister who gave birth last year got scheduled an induction right at her 40 weeks. It felt cruel and unfair. Even the doctors at L&D were shocked learning about this. Unfortunately there’s a loophole because she “recommended” inducing at 41 weeks and we could have pushed back and said no we want to be induced today. We had no idea we could say anything, and ultimately, we trusted her advice. Looking back at it, she really didn’t give a reason why we should wait a week, but we blindly took her recommendation because… she’s a doctor and it’s our first pregnancy. We were naive and clueless.

**[pbu21-0063]** — Reader context: Birth choices, autopsy and death paperwork arrive while grieving and medicated.

> Throughout the evening, many doctors have come visited us explaining different options of birthing and what to do after he’s born. We decided that if they have no idea how he died then we would send him to autopsy. Then the options of a funeral and whether we want him cremated or not. As they were talking options (while high from pain meds) they silently handed my partner a form. After discussing some things and they left, he started sobbing. The paper they gave him was his death certificate. Now, I understand that they need this information, but we could not fathom how cruel and twisted this became in an instant.

**[pbu21-0064]** — Reader context: Reports ineffective epidural top-ups and dismissed pain during stillbirth delivery.

> Unfortunately it was a terrible induction. It took two days. I had two top offs but by the time it was getting close I had feeling back in my lower body. By that point I had a different anesthesiologist and very reluctantly gave me a tiny bit more. It did nothing and the pain tenfolded. She claimed that I needed to feel pressure and a little bit of pain but it did nothing. I had labor pains the rest of the time. I pushed for 3 1/2 hrs with basically no epidural. considering his size, it makes sense because his shoulders were stuck for the longest time. In the end though it was worth it seeing him. He came into this world October 18 2026. Trying not to bring fear into you guys, I’m pretty sure I was a special case cause most epidurals I’ve heard have been fine. The first one was way more kind. The second one had shitty bedside manners and gaslit me saying that I should feel pain and disregarded my request to have more. I don’t wish that pain on anyone.

**[pbu21-0137]** — Reader context: Stillbirth delivery included long induction, surgery, infection and hemorrhage.

> I had a C-section almost 5 weeks ago after induction and a very long labor. We found out our son had passed which is what necessitated the induction in the first place, and then he was a pretty large baby (I was full term) and I labored for 36 hours before pushing for 2 and developing an infection. The C-section went mostly ok, I did hemorrhage a bit and had a cervical extension but it could have been worse.

**[pbu21-0138]** — Reader context: Postpartum bleeding after activity must wait for a weekday reply.

> I had the normal actual bleeding for the first week, then lochia serosa for probably 10 days and after that it seemed to have progressed to lochia alba but it's really mucusy. A couple of times in the past week and a half, there's been bloody streaks in it after a little bit of activity (vacuuming last week, running a bunch of errands this week). I assume this is normal but this is my first time being postpartum. I did message my doctor but it's the weekend so I won't hear back for a couple of days. Nothing is serious enough for me to consider going into the hospital either, I don't feel unwell or anything like that, just like I overdid it a little.

**[pbu21-0240]** — Reader context: Feels the body failed despite following known preparation steps.

> I wish I could have him here with us. I wish I could have given birth and taken him home with us. I wish that my body was safe enough for him to have gone full term. I did everything I was supposed to do as much as I knew to do them. It was my first pregnancy after trying for a year or two and a miscarriage last year.

**[pbu21-0241]** — Reader context: Values natural birth, brief family contact and keepsakes despite loss.

> I'm trying to concentrate on the positives. I gave birth to him naturally like I wanted to. We got time with him before he passed. He got to hear his family love him, he got to feel our kisses. And he got to reach out and touch us. It was beautiful. Truly. We got him cremated and the hospital/funeral home were so generous with things for him and us! We got so many beautiful keepsakes!

**[pbu21-0325]** — Reader context: First encounter with bereavement is stillbirth; explanation may take months.

> Trigger warning: stillbirth.   
> My daughter was stillborn on the 1st of August 2026. I was 40 weeks and 3 days, she was my first baby. I got pregnant at 19, healthy pregnancy, although I developed anemia over the last few weeks . Post mortem will take months and I’m completely at a loss. I’ve never felt anything like this, I feel like I’m trapped in a nightmare. I don’t know how I can ever recover from this, and all I can think is if I went to the hospital for reduced movements maybe they could have saved her. I can’t comprehend that my baby is dead. I am so lost, I’ve never even been to a funeral before or dealt with any grief. My grandparents are still alive yet I had to burry my daughter on Wednesday. I’ve never felt so hopeless, I can’t fathom what the next step is…

**[pbu21-0351]** — Reader context: Holding the fetus after loss provides recognition and a chance to say goodbye.

> Something inside me told me to reach in and hold it, so I scooped this clot out of the blood-stained toilet and just held it in my hand for a little bit. I looked closer, and it seemed like there was something inside of it. That inside voice told me to open it up, so I cut it open, and clear as day, my tiny, blueberry baby was in the palm of my hand. Maybe this is an insane thing to say, but there was such an intense feeling of gratitude that I felt in that moment. To know that what I was experiencing was all real, and that I was truly growing a tiny life inside of me. It’s helped me to feel like I can actually say goodbye.

**[pbu32-0028]** — Reader context: Currently pregnant after a stillbirth; pregnancy number differs from living-child count.

> When people ask how many kids I have I always want to say “6” but then that requires an explanation when they ask about genders and names and all that. I don’t want my son to be forgotten or overlooked but I also don’t always want to talk about the situation.

**[pbu32-0034]** — Reader context: After membrane rupture at sixteen weeks, feared causing baby's delivery.

> But i was too scared to go washroom cuz baby would come out because of me. It was mentally a torture for me cuz i heard the baby’s heartbeat that morning and i coudnt do that to the baby.

**[pbu32-0035]** — Reader context: Rejects reassurance that a future baby could replace the baby lost.

> Cuz im tired of ppl telling me ill get more babies in the future or god will give me better than this. How can anything be better cuz my baby was perfectly healthy and there was nothing wrong with him.

**Further supporting or qualifying IDs:** [pbu08-0152] [pbu08-0153] [pbu08-0392] [pbu21-0078] [pbu32-0047] [pbu32-0065] [pbu32-0066]

Full wording for these IDs is either elsewhere above or in the evidence appendix below. Primary grouping is for retrieval; it does not make the quotes independent.

### 12. Birth preferences weigh previous injury, pain, safety, anxiety and future fertility, and can change before or during labor.

**Current findings location:** §§4 (preferences/consent), 6 (birth fear), 9, 10, 17.1.

**Relationship:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “Flexibility alone does not explain a good or difficult birth.”

**What should change:** Keep competing preferences visible: requesting induction earlier after loss, wanting to avoid it, preferring a section, reconsidering a booked section, seeking VBAC, and worrying about later pregnancies. Explain the range of what authors weigh without adopting their risk estimates or predicting a better route. Consent can feel informed, rushed, pressured or absent; an unfamiliar device can still be described as fully explained.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0098]** — Reader context: Chooses induction date to avoid an abuser's birthday but emergency birth happens on it.

> My OB gave me a range of dates to schedule my induction for, and I intentionally picked the day I did because it was the furthest date from my ex abusers birthday, but then was in labor for almost three days straight and had to have an emergency c section, *on* **his** birthday. Dreaded that day every year since, because it's a yearly reminder of him, and now I have a mini reminder of that day following me everywhere I go. It's been a few months now, and the initial PPD and residual birth trauma numbness have worn off, and I am absolutely enraptured by her  but I still can't get over the hurdle in my head about that date. My family says "now you have a good association with that date, to help reframe it!" but that's just not how it's working out 😞

**[pbu08-0111]** — Reader context: Previous mixed twin delivery and helpful EMDR shape a planned section and fear of renewed trauma.

> I decided to opt for a c-section this time around as I was induced at 38 weeks with my girls and ended up needed interventions to deliver my first vaginally and then immediately had to have an emergency c-section for my second. I was so so so adamant to stick to what I thought was a perfect birth plan (don’t we all want a water birth with minimal interventions!) and when it didn’t work out that way and all went down south, I felt very low and awful afterwards and just finishing EMDR therapy for it all which really helped so much. I just worry birth approaching will be re-traumatising and I worry how I will cope with newborn twins and toddlers.

**[pbu08-0118]** — Reader context: Following spacing advice, wants lived experience of recovery after three or more sections.

> I've had two C-sections - my first was an unplanned emergency and my second was planned. I healed really well after my second. My surgeon said everything looked good during the operation and I have spoken to a consultant who confirmed that he'd be happy for me to have another baby if I wanted to. He advised waiting 18 months before trying to conceive again and I am following this advice.
> 
> We'd love to have a third (or even a fourth!) baby if we're able to, so I'm interested in hearing from people who've had three or more C-sections.
> 
> I'd love to know:
> 
> How was your recovery after your third (or fourth+) C-section compared to the others?  
> How long did you wait before conceiving again?  
> Did you have any complications, or was everything fairly straightforward?  
> Is there anything you wish you'd known beforehand?

**[pbu08-0121]** — Reader context: Despite missing the birth under general anesthesia, reunion feels worthwhile and pain manageable.

> unfortunately i had to be put under general anaesthetic so missed the birth and my partner couldn't be in the theatre but once we were all reunited and i got to meet my baby the whole ordeal was worth it for sure, am in a bit of pain due to the c section but its managable and am looking forward to going home as we are still in hospital at the moment

**[pbu08-0123]** — Reader context: Author reports an explanation of an unfamiliar assisted-delivery device and explicit consent; one account.

> The consultant offered the Odon Assist as our first option. She explained that it’s a relatively new, specialised device used instead of forceps or ventouse. It uses a soft, plastic sleeve that is gently placed around the baby's head to help guide them out. She was totally honest that she had only used it a few times herself, but explained the massive benefit: it drastically reduces the risk of marks, bruising, or swelling on the baby’s head. I felt completely informed and gave 100% consent.

**[pbu08-0230]** — Reader context: Anatomy findings raise a feared section and possible hospital stay before the care plan is explained.

> Hi! I’m 20w today and just has anatomy scan. Was told I have VCI and Type 1 Vasa Previa. Still have to talk to my doctor tomorrow and see what they say / the plan of action. I’m spiraling though… already cried like 10 times because the absolute last thing I wanted was a C-section as I’m terrified more than anything. I can’t even deal with getting an IV… has anyone’s VP resolved itself before having to do a hospital stay / C-section? Or how did anyone’s experience go who has dealt with this? I am so so scared right now.

**[pbu08-0262]** — Reader context: Hospitalized for growth restriction, fears awake surgery and effects of offered anxiety relief.

> I’ve always been sooo scared of the thought of having a c section, so now knowing that I’m definitely going to have one and also don’t even know when or if my baby will be okay is scaring me so much. Can someone who’s had a c section give me some sort of peace of mind? I’m so scared of being awake. They say I can have anxiety meds but I’m afraid the meds might make it even harder for him to breathe when I already know he’s going to be in distress from being premature and only weighing 2-3lbs. I’ve been in the hospital living on edge terrified of what’s to come for 7 days now.

**[pbu08-0366]** — Reader context: Painful nights without labor progression create constant analysis of symptoms.

> And then there’s the prodromal-labor mindfuck. I can have period cramps for hours, especially overnight, think “maybe this is finally starting,” and then… nothing. 😭 My normal Braxton Hicks usually don’t hurt, so whenever something actually DOES hurt I start analysing whether these are contractions, early labor, baby position, pelvic pressure, or just another fun late-pregnancy symptom.

**[pbu08-0367]** — Reader context: Tries multiple ways to start labor for reassurance but also fears induction.

> I think part of why I desperately want labor to start is honestly because I just want him HERE so I can see that he’s okay.
> 
> Meanwhile I’ve become completely obsessed with getting labor started. Walking, staying active, stretches, trying nipple stimulation/breast pumping, wondering how long you’re actually supposed to pump and whether you do one breast or both, looking up ways to increase oxytocin, wondering about membrane sweeps, and basically interrogating the universe about the probability that I’ll give birth within the next week.
> 
> If I go past my due date I already know I’m going to be asking about induction because psychologically I genuinely don’t know how much longer I can tolerate the waiting. But then again, I’ve read inductions are not the best - so I am also afraid of that.

**[pbu21-0059]** — Reader context: A positive GBS result arrives at the gestation of prior stillbirth.

> I’m now 33+0 which is the exact gestation I had my stillborn son. I was pretty happy to wait until 37 weeks for my induction despite having no mental health follow up or support this entire pregnancy, until I received a GBS+ result on a swab.

**[pbu21-0060]** — Reader context: Seeks earlier induction to manage competing fears and mental deterioration.

> This has understandably caused me to spiral and I want to take 0 risks with this baby. I was thinking of requesting induction at 36+0-36+3 with this baby, the reason being I’m terrified my waters break at home and I cannot access IV antibiotics in time due to the nature of my rapid labours even at an earlier gestation. My body clearly springs into action and births babies quickly. I’d much rather take the risks of baby needing a bit of extra help due to being “late preterm” than leave the hospital with my baby in a mortuary again.

**[pbu21-0111]** — Reader context: Suspected growth and glucose issues prompted booking an elective section.

> Hello! I am a FTM and have been very anxious due to previous health issues for my entire pregnancy. My baby was measuring really big up until the past few weeks - he’s now 57th percentile but he started in the high 90s. I panicked about size and a potential GD diagnosis (which later turned out to just be some mild insulin spiking) and booked in an elective c section.

**[pbu21-0112]** — Reader context: Later uncertainty prompts asking whether birth plans can change.

> But now we’re five or so weeks out and I’m having cold feet. Has anyone changed their mind about how they choose to give birth? I’m about 33 weeks and so uncertain and worried about all the risks and variables. My consultant says if I go into labour before my elective date she can give me a category 3 c section, I’m wondering if I should ask to give it a go vaginally if that happens.

**[pbu21-0127]** — Reader context: Repeated reduced movements cause panic and sleepless monitoring.

> I saw a consultant 2 days ago who recommended induction at 39 weeks but wants to see what my scan shows on Monday (assuming to see if I need to be induced sooner or if they are happy to stick to the timeline of 39 weeks). However, I’m now at the point that all these episodes are taking a massive toll on me mentally. Everytime I have reduced movements I have panic attacks and leave myself feeling physically sick and exhausted afterwards. I’m now at the point I’m not sleeping either due to laying awake all night ensuring that I feel him. Which I know also isn’t good for baby. I’m just exhausted and feel unwell. Im meeting with the antenatal team following my scan on Monday I’m assuming to discuss if the scan was fine and book the induction for 39 weeks or see if it needs to be brought forward.

**[pbu21-0128]** — Reader context: Requests earlier induction to preserve capacity for labor.

> I want to request to be induced at 38 weeks just because of the physical toll this is all taking on me. Im in triage almost weekly sometimes twice weekly for reduced movements and im worried that if I continue down this path by the time im induced at 39 weeks, my body is just going to be too exhausted to labour.

**[pbu21-0139]** — Reader context: Felt pressured to accept induction partly because partner agreed.

> By 2pm, I agreed to take a cytotec. At 6pm, still no progression and now my husband was also on board with me agreeing to Pitocin. I wanted to wait longer but ultimately felt a bit pressured to move forward with it and agreed to go low and slow with it. My biggest hold up is I didn’t want an epidural which seemed likely with Pitocin.

**[pbu21-0193]** — Reader context: Rejects delivery recommendation because it feels insufficiently supportive of VBAC.

> One of the nurses at the hospital come in and say “there’s a 98% chance you’re staying and delivering today.“ I’m thinking why if my blood pressure is fine?? Five minutes later, the DR comes in and tells me, “since your liver enzymes are high and protein urine is high we’re going to deliver today. you’re showing signs of pre eclampsia.“ Mind you, this is because of the slightly elevated enzymes and protein. I don’t doubt that if my baby is overdue that it may turn into that, but again my goal is for VBAC.   
> I spoke with my partner and we both don’t feel like this is reason enough to do c section. We don’t feel like they are trying hard enough for the VBAC. They want me in and out.

**[pbu21-0370]** — Reader context: Suspected late diabetes creates competing fears about vaginal birth and future sections.

> So now I’m worried about possible undiagnosed late-onset GD and possible harm caused to my baby, and on top of that, terrified of vaginal delivery. I’ve read AC over 35cm increases risk for shoulder dystocia and birth injuries, and generally if abdomen/shoulder size is proportionally bigger than head. And he already has AC of 35cm at 37 weeks… I’m seriously considering a planned C-section, but I’m also scared of the risks for future pregnancies.

**[pbu21-0371]** — Reader context: Prior termination for medical reasons makes losing another baby unbearable.

> Any advice? Has anyone had a similar situation with a very large AC and/or possible late GD? Is it too risky to try vaginal delivery - I can’t lose this baby too, I already lost one due to TFMR. The TFMR was also an induced vaginal delivery at 20 weeks, taking about 8-9 hours from first misoprostols, if that tells anything.

**[pbu32-0080]** — Reader context: Declines local induction trial after reading older studies.

> Since I posted this I've decided it's not for me tbh. I've looked at some older studies (albeit from 10 years ago) and and it didn't fill me with confidence tbh.

**[pbu32-0083]** — Reader context: Reported new monitoring requirements affect birth preference and separation from children.

> water birth is out of the question as baby has to be constantly monitored during labour. Having to stay in longer than 24 hours and missing my other children, my last water birth was with my first 10 years ago.

**[pbu32-0093]** — Reader context: Itching and rising bile-acid results rapidly replace planned repeat water birth with new options.

> Finding it hard to come to terms with the drastic change in my situation in such a short period. Have to now decide between an induction or c section so advice happily and gratefully received!!

**[pbu32-0144]** — Reader context: Disputes dating that ignores late ovulation; fears future induction pressure and damaging ongoing doctor relationship.

> What’s a diplomatic way to advocate for myself without coming off as combative?

**[pbu32-0188]** — Reader context: Induction at thirty-eight weeks after prior stillbirth; wants hopeful accounts with living babies.

> I have a few hopes and goals for this birth, but at this point all I really care about is having a healthy living baby. I wouldn’t be afraid at all if I just knew that he was going to be fine!

**[pbu32-0192]** — Reader context: Breech position and possible nuchal cord alter first birth plan and future-pregnancy thinking.

> However with this being our first baby, planning for 1 or 2 more and it does raise slightly the anxiety of a future VBAC / TOLAC knowing the small increased risks.

**[pbu32-0323]** — Reader context: Four days postpartum; emergency section after reaching full dilation defeats hoped-for healing VBAC.

> I started sobbing because immediately it felt like my body had failed me and I was gonna have to recover from surgery. My first recovery was rough and I was terrified of not feeling connected to my baby like I did with my first.

### 13. An emergency can replace the expected date, team and hospital and separate the parent, baby and partner.

**Current findings location:** §§2, 4, 5, 10 (plan B), 16.5, 17.2.

**Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “include plan B for the birth, not only plan A”

**What should change:** Add abrupt admission, general anesthesia, premature birth, NICU transfer, different levels of neonatal care and prolonged hospital life. A routine appointment may become same-day birth. Partners may miss surgery despite intending to attend; the recovering parent may remain elsewhere from the baby. An uneventful pregnancy does not guarantee an uneventful birth. Include positive reunion and grateful accounts of staff rather than treating the procedure or outcome as an emotional verdict.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0035]** — Reader context: A succession of complications makes the pregnancy feel like repeated bad news.

> Fortunately her pregnancy has been going well. Mine on the other hand, not so great. I’ve been hospitalised with blood clots in my leg and lung, it looks like I’m developing hyperthyroidism and to top it all off we found out we’d lost one of our twins, followed by a huge bleed the next day that by some miracle was an SCH rather than a miscarriage. I feel like I’m careening from one piece of bad news to the next and am honestly so paranoid about the outcome.

**[pbu08-0037]** — Reader context: Emergency anesthesia excludes the husband while the author is frightened.

> One more cervical check and I'm at a 7. It is now 11:30. Baby's heart rate dipping further, so they rush us into an emergency c-section. The meds they want to push won't work fast enough, so they fully knock me out while I'm crying over how scared I am. Husband wasn't even allowed into the room because "you wouldn't be any comfort to her," as though his comfort doesn't also matter?

**[pbu08-0038]** — Reader context: Delayed first contact and a difficult second birth overturn expectations from an easy first labor.

> Baby is out within 1 minute of me being put to sleep, but is having trouble breathing. They pull baby in for monitoring oxygen levels, and he keeps failing the tests when pulled from the tubes. I wake up from the procedure unable to even look at my son because he's down a hallway the rolling bed can't fit through.
> 
> 4 hours later I finally see and touch him for the first time. He's perfect and we're both okay now. But my goodness that was terrifying. 
> 
> This is my second baby, and the first labor was pretty quick and smooth so I was expecting to handle this well. After this....I know I'll never do it again. This was such a traumatic event and I feel like I haven't even been able to process it fully.

**[pbu08-0085]** — Reader context: Normal anatomy scan reassurance is disrupted by a later kidney finding.

> I had a very uneventful pregnancy, my first baby. 20 week scan was totally normal, and I felt so much relief as I always heard after this scan, you could feel reassured everything is ok. At around 36 weeks, I had a growth ultrasound which is where we discovered one of her kidneys was massively enlarged. There was nothing seen on scans that could indicate why this was (no cysts, no obvious obstruction, no masses), and my MFM doctor and OB did not seem too concerned given she otherwise was doing great. Was recommended to get a repeat US when she was born, otherwise, nothing special. This news alone had me spiraling, but, I eventually calmed down once everyone had reassured me it was likely nothing serious and worst case, she would only have function of her other kidney but otherwise would live a normal life.

**[pbu08-0086]** — Reader context: Newborn transfer shortly after birth is remembered as the worst day.

> We were told she needed to be transferred via ambulance downtown to the level IV NICU for additional imaging, oncology and nephrology. This was probably the worst day of my life being freshly post partum and hearing my otherwise perfect baby needed to be transferred. That ambulance ride was a blur.

**[pbu08-0099]** — Reader context: Unexplained premature delivery follows an uncomplicated pregnancy.

> Our daughter was born 33w1 after an uncomplicated pregnancy; they have no idea why I delivered so early. She spent 32 days in the NICU and has been home longer than she was in the NICU.

**[pbu08-0101]** — Reader context: Mistakes leaking waters for urinary incontinence because she expected a gush.

> At 30 weeks pregnant I thought I had wet myself because I was MASSIVE already, and I thought waters breaking would be a huge gush of fluid but now I know that isn’t always the case! I called triage a few days later and they asked to see me and the test showed my waters had broken. Massive shock to me and my husband as I was expecting to go back home and told to do more pelvic floor exercises.

**[pbu08-0102]** — Reader context: Emergency section becomes relief after prolonged painful uncertainty.

> By the final time it felt like I was in labour I was still only 4cm dilated (had been for a week or so at that point) and had a big bleed - it settled again and I knew it was important to keep baby in as long as possible to protect him but the pain was constant at this point. Finally they agreed a Cat 1 c section which at that point was a huge relief after being in pain and so scared for weeks.

**[pbu08-0294]** — Reader context: Unexpected admission makes hospital packing urgent before planned preparation.

> Anyways, being only 31 weeks I had not packed a bag yet. I am having my husband pack a bag for me with some comfy pjs, my pillow, snacks, my toiletries.

**[pbu08-0295]** — Reader context: Asks which preterm-baby items and section-recovery supplies are actually needed.

> Should I have him pack me anything else? Would it even be necessary to pack any of the things I got for baby? I do have preemie clothes for him because all my babies have been so small but surely between 31-34 weeks they arent really dressing him right? I do have a consult with someone from the nicu so I will ask them these things too of course. 
> 
> I'm also wondering if i should have him stop and get me any csection recovery supplies or if its just not needed. I don't know. I'm absolutely terrified of a csection

**[pbu21-0018]** — Reader context: Prior gastric bypass complications are recognized at the 30-week appointment.

> We had our 30 appointment week this week and I was just put on high-risk due to the size of the baby & that the hospital thats delivering went "oh wait you had the gastric bypass?" To which I clarified - "I had the bypass 3 years ago and almost died. I was in a hospital in the same network. Please look up my file" they saw my complications and went "we need you to come in weekly for monitoring".

**[pbu21-0094]** — Reader context: Vacuum attempt failed before urgent surgery.

> Eventually, my water broke and was meconium stained. The team at the hospital said we’d have the NICU there at birth just in case. Then baby started having decels with contractions, but not sustained. They had to turn down the pitocin. I was able to get an epidural before it was time to push. During pushing, he continued to have decels. Then they realized he was face up. I pushed for a while but he wouldn’t descend. The doctor asked if I wanted to try vacuum assisted or c section. In the moment, I opted for vacuum assisted because I wanted to avoid surgery if possible. They tried the vacuum 3 times but that failed. Then I got rushed for urgent c section.

**[pbu21-0106]** — Reader context: Emergency preparation and consent happen within twenty minutes of arrival.

> As you can imagine there was a panic and the nurse sounds the alarm and  the doctors are called in. About 3/4 people came in and they’re talking to me while unplugging me and moving me. I’m crying, my partner is crying. We’re in our 20s and first time parents. It’s all going wrong. This is all within 20 minutes of arriving.

**[pbu21-0107]** — Reader context: Healthy baby and kind staff coexist with confusion about why birth changed.

> The baby is okay thankfully but I’m so shaken by everything. It’s 13:35pm now - the baby is sleeping, my partner is sleeping I’ve finally turned my phone on just wanted to talk to you guys ❤️ the medical team are nice and helping but mentally it’s been a lot. I don’t understand what happened or what caused it - everything was fine through pregnancy till we get there and it all went wrong.

**[pbu21-0140]** — Reader context: Surgery started before partner returned; both were frightened.

> I was rolled back for surgery early and of course my husband got caught up in construction even though we live in a small ass area where there’s never construction that holds up traffic. When they started cutting into me, I was on my own and petrified. The anesthesiologist administered a benzo because I was uncomfortably crying. Right as the baby was pulled out, he was brought in and all he saw was the dr holding my intestines up. He immediately came to my side and I asked if baby was okay and he looked over at the baby being surrounded & also at the people surrounding me and then looked back at me and just held my hand. I just saw fear in his eyes and he was shaking. Then I passed out.

**[pbu21-0145]** — Reader context: Recalls seven NICU weeks after difficult pregnancy and birth.

> I had a difficult fertility journey, a difficult pregnancy, a terrifying birth and then 7 weeks in NICU (which is its own special version of hell for parents).

> I opted for natural, all of it. only resource was gas and air. I had the option for water but said no. I laboured until about 1 pm when I asked for the epidural, I was 8cm. They said the epi would work in 30 minutes, it mever arrived. no relief, back to back labour pains. I had My partner digging his knuckles into the lower spine amongst heat packs to help.

### 14. Being believed and informed depends on clinicians, records and access systems as well as the parent's questions.

**Current findings location:** §5 (being believed), §§6, 10, 16.2, 17.4.

**Relationship:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “Care navigation includes trust and being believed.”

**What should change:** Specify withheld or unexplained findings, contradictory messages, dismissive language, poor continuity, a no-take-back policy after seeking a second opinion, pharmacy error, software recalculation, rural distance and unavailable refills. A clinician blaming diabetes on the mother or interpreting panic as nausea can change trust and help seeking. Keep callbacks, repeat tests, kind explanations and eventual validation alongside failures. Do not imply the right script removes structural barriers.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0010]** — Reader context: Questions why repeat testing must wait for delayed initial results.

> I asked to go get my blood drawn again before the initial results came back, since it's been a week with no improvement and they told me they wanted to see initial results first. Which really frustrates me because what harm will getting another blood draw cause? WHY can't they just put the order in so that we can have the results a few days sooner next time?

**[pbu08-0056]** — Reader context: Unexpected abnormal scan notes prompt searching and genetic counseling.
