It says the babies heart is dextrocardia(positioning of the heart to the right). They made me have a call with genetics to explain possibilities of why is could happen and thaknfully they had an appt that same day. No one in either mine or my husbands families know about this or that it runs in our family. I know scans werent as high tech as they are today so maybe we have this and no one knows?

**[pbu08-0057]** — Reader context: Detailed imaging brings intense relief while further testing remains pending.

> We went back yesterday for a more detailed scan with MFM. They confirmed the rest of her heart is structurally and functionally perfect. It just faces the wrong way. The relief we had was something I can’t even describe. But she did talk to me about having an amino fluid test to rule out any other genetic conditions on why this happened.   
>   
> \- quick side piece my husband and I got pregnant through IUI cuz his sperm had some issues with thickness and not moving far but we did genetic testing before getting pregnant and 2 times throughout this pregnancy and everything is normal/low risk.

**[pbu08-0213]** — Reader context: Questions whether seeking care for painful contractions is dramatic.

> 32 weeks… 33 weeks tomorrow and having contractions on and off all night. Some of the contractions have been really painful and causing severe back pain. They feel like horrible period cramps. The contractions are knocking the wind out of me at times. The intervals are a bit inconsistent and the intensity varies but it’s been happening all night long and a bit this morning, I’m calling out of work to and I’m going to visit my OB. Is this dramatic? Or the right course of action given the signs?

**[pbu08-0361]** — Reader context: Different explanations of a hematoma's size and location turn reassurance into fear.

> I am 14 weeks 4 days pregnant and about two weeks ago I started having brown spotting that eventually progressed to gushes of brown blood when I was sleeping. Last week I had an ultrasound done where the baby looked healthy (measuring 5 days ahead with a strong heartbeat.) I saw the midwife after and she said she couldn’t see the full ultrasound report yet but it likely wasn’t large and it was not near my placenta. Over the weekend, I had more significant brown bleeding and went in today. My midwife today told me that the hematoma is large and increases my risk of miscarriage, placental abruption, and preterm labor up to 5x. She said the ultrasound report didn’t measure it but indicated that it covered the anterior portion of my placenta which at this point is about the size of a grapefruit. The hopeful pieces are that it is not thick (the thickest part being 11 millimeters) and I have only had brown bleeding. Regardless, I am very concerned because she said it’s definitely large and a concern. I go back next week to measure it and see if it has changed. Looking for any other hopeful stories of large hematomas that ended up resolving.

**[pbu08-0414]** — Reader context: Had to remove medical detail to ask about staff behavior rather than viability.

> My question is about the behavior of the medical staff and whether that is typical.

**[pbu21-0166]** — Reader context: Three conflicting scan opinions cause confusion.

**[pbu21-0169]** — Reader context: Hospital worker considers quitting after requests to change physical duties receive little response.

The only response I got was "text ( other managers name)" and I asked for the other managers number, I don't have it because I've never had to reach out to her, and never got a response. It really infuriated me, I've worked there for over a year, been a full time employee and you can't at least pretend to have half a shred of compassion? After my appointment Monday I'm tempted to go in, turn my badge in and just say sorry, I'm done, my baby's health and my health is more important than this.

**[pbu21-0268]** — Reader context: Questions an appointment pathway and receives no answer about ectopic detection.

> She said: We go off of a positive home test then in two weeks you come in and we give you a pee test. Then 12 weeks from there, we will do first ultrasound. I said, well how do you rule out ectopic pregnancies waiting that long? She didn’t have an answer

**[pbu21-0274]** — Reader context: Restricted clinic hours and shortages collide with work and childcare.

> I’m already stressed the fuck out, I work, my boyfriend works, I’m with my four year old all day until I work, we’re dealing with family issues specifically with my parents. it’s just a whole damn thing, and what’s with ultrasound clinics and bloodwork clinics only open until 5pm? half of people in the world work until 4, or get off at 3, get home at 3:30. like it’s juts bullshit, the system is so fucked up it’s so overwhelming.

**[pbu21-0275]** — Reader context: Opposed interpretations of one scan leave author horrified and on bed rest.

> Is this alright for a 7 week pregnancy? The ultrasound tech told me everything looked fine except for the anechoic area, but I consulted with a doctor and she said that I have a high chance of miscarriage since the ultrasound report should have shown a heartbeat but it only says a cardiac flicker was seen. Plus she said I need to be on complete bedrest if I want to save the pregnancy. I'm genuinely horrified by this and I can't stop stressing out.

**[pbu21-0395]** — Reader context: Written radiology report contradicts reassuring verbal scan description.

> Hi all, I had a 6 week scan last week and I thought I was 6 weeks 5 days, turns out baby is 6 weeks three days. The tech told us (I know they really aren’t supposed to give diagnostic info but she could tell how scared I was) that she could see a verryyy faint heart beat measuring 119-122 bpm. We were very excited! Well, yesterday my radiology report came back and everything looked normal except it said: Cardiac Activity: Not definitely seen on current examination. The fetal pole is 0.6 cm. I’m so beyond terrified and called my OB to see if I need another ultrasound (even though I have another scheduled for next Friday) and I haven’t heard back yet. At this point I had the ultrasound about a week ago and should be measuring 7 weeks 4 days. Anyone have similar stories? I’m so afraid of what is to come. Thank you all 🫶🏻

**[pbu21-0396]** — Reader context: Follow-up scan finds a strong heartbeat after confusing report.

> Thank you so much❤️ I went today and my OB didn’t seem happy with what the radiologist put in the report, she did her own and they found bubs strong beat! 163❤️

**[pbu32-0001]** — Reader context: Nurse dismissal makes author hesitate to return for worsening symptoms.

> Now I feel dumb for even considering going in because the discharge nurse told me last time “your nurse is working on someone who is actually having complications so I’m here to discharge you”

**[pbu32-0002]** — Reader context: Partner advocacy also feels ignored.

> My partner is available and can advocate for me aswell but they just kinda take his words in one ear out the other and it really makes me scared to deliver my baby

**[pbu32-0003]** — Reader context: Follow-up after testing and reported preeclampsia diagnosis.

> I finally feel heard even though not the news I wanted but I don’t feel crazy and I feel justified for being kinda pushy with them instead of letting them bully me into thinking what I’m experiencing is normal.

**[pbu32-0021]** — Reader context: Rural counseling wait follows breakup during pregnancy.

> i have a therapy appointment set up, but they couldn’t get me in until months out. i’m from a rural county in a rural state and my primary doctor told me we are suffering from a counselor drought, that’s why my appointment was set up so far out.

**[pbu32-0025]** — Reader context: Seeks earlier NIPT after loss; current office waits until twelve-week appointment.

> I tried the Sesame app but my virtual appt was canceled since writing the NIPT lab order was “outside of their scope”, and I don’t see anyone in the OB field on Sesame licensed in Pennsylvania.

**[pbu32-0048]** — Reader context: Severe cramping persists despite reassuring fetal heartbeat and tests.

> the doctor laughed in my face and said “okay what do you think it is then” after i explained i don’t believe its growing pains and neither did my other doctor. my baby had a perfect heartbeat and is a good size but something is not right and i need answers

**[pbu32-0068]** — Reader context: Retrospective birth-injury account; current therapy discussion concerns toddler rather than newborn stage.

> My 22 month old toddler has Erb’s Palsy in her left arm from a birth injury but we didn’t get the diagnosis until she was roughly 4 months old and only because I advocated for her.

**[pbu32-0084]** — Reader context: Ferritin falls despite daily tablets, additional liquid iron and orange juice.

> The midwife seems to think I should just keep going with the tablets as I have been doing, but I'm concerned this is evident that they're not working as they should. Has anyone else faced this? Should I push back about getting an IV or similar?

**[pbu32-0312]** — Reader context: Author's account of severe insomnia and failed access to treatment; usual suggestions feel mismatched.

> I’ve had insomnia for a decade so it’s frustrating to hear suggestions like Unisom, Benadryl, melatonin. Like I do actually need to be sedated or I’m going to go into psychosis from sleep deprivation. No doctor wants the liability.

**[pbu32-0331]** — Reader context: Pregnant physician challenges Colace recommendations; efficacy claim is source testimony, not verified here.

> This is nothing new, it has been repeatedly shown that docusate is absolutely no better than placebo and yet it is still sold, still recommended, and still taken by women who just want to 💩 and are being misled.

### 15. Disclosing abuse or distress can create feared consequences or fail to address the help the parent sought.

**Current findings location:** §5 (care/partners), §6, §10 care-navigation proposal; new disclosure-consequences subsection.

**Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “A 'what your appointments should cover and what to ask at each' page answers both.”

**What should change:** Qualify 'answers': asking and disclosure do not guarantee feeling safer or receiving the wanted support. A safeguarding referral is feared as a trigger for an ex's retaliation; a historical-abuse alert changes whether a parent wants the current partner at an appointment. Another author says therapy keeps returning to crisis screening rather than her constant anxiety; another withholds an assault after prior disbelief. Describe the reported perceptions without inferring law, policy or professional misconduct beyond what is said.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0113]** — Reader context: Previous unhelpful referral makes renewed mental-health support doubtful; rejects pressure to terminate.

> I was referred by my midwife to perinatal mental health but I spoke to them during my last pregnancy already as I experienced a lot of anxiety and they weren’t helpful to me and I also had / have my own therapy already outside the NHS so I’m really unsure what they could do for me? Basically I’m experiencing a lot of gloom and doom. Any kind words or advice would be appreciated. And please no “why didn’t you terminate” comments, I’ve been told a few too many times now.

**[pbu08-0131]** — Reader context: After assault and bleeding, withholds the cause from hospital because of prior disbelief.

> My partner physically assaulted me a few weeks postpartum (dragged and punched me in the back). I later went to hospital due to heavy bleeding after this. I didn’t tell the hospital what had truly happened since they had a track record of not believing me anyways, and I just felt too scared and depressed to do anything anymore. He also refused to take his full paid parental leave and went back to work after only 2 weeks postpartum.

**[pbu08-0138]** — Reader context: Questions screening threshold and how to access help while caring for an attached baby.

> At my 6 week pp check up, they did ask but I apparently scored a 7 and I needed a 12 for it to be a concern. I feel like that test isnt accurate or maybe Im answering wrong because of how the questions/answers are worded. I dont know if I should make an appointment to talk to my OB or if I should just go into therapy but then I dont know how to go about that when my son cannot be away from me for a very long time.

**[pbu08-0182]** — Reader context: Unreturned calls and perceived disbelief make the experience feel lonely.

> I don't know what to do. I will keep trying to get in touch with my doctor or get a new provider. But this ish is hard : '( I've never felt so unseen and unheard in all my life & I've never had to walk a path this lonely before. Oh yeah my man especially ain't gettin none when I glow the f up.

**[pbu08-0205]** — Reader context: Wants informed chronic-pain care without being presumed to have substance-use problems.

> I am seeking an MFM in Atlanta area that knows more about this than "offering methadone" I'm not in recovery or have substance abuse issues. The ob I saw KEPT insinuating I do, which was rude and not helpful. I barely know her and I've been managing my meds with a lot of drug testing and supervision for years. I am open to tapering/getting off, but I want a kind, compassionate ob who has experience with this and knows how withdrawal/untreated pain/opioid rx could affect myself and unborn baby.

**[pbu21-0142]** — Reader context: Asks what support exists when disability prevents infant care.

> If I don't recover and am unfit to care for the baby due to limited mobility and disability, what happens? Does CPS intervene? Can I ask a hospital for resources? Is there some sort of govt assistance navigating this scenario? Would I be forced to give baby up for adoption? I very much want her, but it's getting to the point where it's impossible to meet her needs.

**[pbu21-0250]** — Reader context: Therapy’s crisis screening does not answer pervasive pregnancy worry.

> i never imagined my pregnancy would be so freaking depressing! i’m stressed out 24/7. i’m always worried about my baby. always crying or having panic attacks. literally can’t even function correctly or comfortably. folding baby clothes? breakdown. can’t find my cat? breakdown. bf just existing? breakdown. feeling guilty for eating something unhealthy? stressed thinking i ruined something. forgot to take prenatals? impending doom. like DAMN I JUST WANT TO BREATHE. i’m just so anxious. i’ve tried therapy and it’s always the same exact thing. “do you want to harm yourself” “call 988” LIKE NO. i just want a peace of mind. im going on 33 weeks and my mind had been on high alert since 20 weeks. im just so worried about my baby being okay & being a good mother. it’s all just too much on me 😩 and i feel so guilty for complaining.

**[pbu21-0303]** — Reader context: Psychiatrist’s medication restrictions conflict with perceived support from pregnancy care team.

> My Psychiatrist also discontinued my ADHD medication and said she will not be prescribing me anything more for anxiety until I'm done breastfeeding the baby I'm expecting which I'm unsure if I'll even be able to due to my first pregnancy I couldn't breastfeed past two months due to my production. I have chronic anxiety and major depression and it seems like she just keeps trying to make me feel bad for needing medication during my pregnancy even though my care team was prepared for that, except for her I suppose.

**[pbu21-0338]** — Reader context: Safeguarding referral raises fear of retaliation after asking midwife for support.

> After the appointment, she called me to state she would be speaking to her safeguarding team on Thursday to see whether a referral needed to be made to social services. I understand they have a duty of care, and they have pathways to follow. But this has stressed me out even further, if they make a referral to social services about him he will be made aware that I’ve sought advice from them and he will assume I’m trying to take his son off him, but he also has a daughter from a previous relationship and will assume I’ve tried to implicate that situation too, no doubt bringing more verbal abuse to me at a time I really don’t need it.

**[pbu21-0339]** — Reader context: Fears disclosing distress created more risk and administrative pressure.

> I don’t know what to do, I told the midwife how I felt that I just needed support and advice and not another thing to stress over, I don’t want to worry about social services involvement, I don’t want to have to worry about more back lash from him and I feel like I did the wrong thing telling them how much of a difficult time I’ve been having now. Am I just being a hormonal, overthinking mess?

**[pbu32-0087]** — Reader context: Badger Notes safeguarding flag follows disclosure of past abuse, not current relationship.

> Due to this I’m quite confused and a bit upset since I’m not under any threat and don’t want any referral to any teams. I’ve got my second midwife appointment this week and my partner also really wants to come but I don’t want him to because of this now.

**[pbu32-0157]** — Reader context: Coworker's account of examinations and instruments triggers fear before first gynecological exam.

> I’m 14 weeks pregnant right now, never had a pap smear or anything down there due to an extensive history of sexual trauma.

**[pbu32-0164]** — Reader context: Reports physical and sexual violence during pregnancy and threats about newborn removal.

> he’s also threatened to take the kid out of the hospital after I give birth and go to Mexico. I’m honestly venting and asking for advice on how I can keep my child safe after the baby is born because I don’t want my baby to be taken and in an unsafe place, and I also don’t want him coming after me and hurting me or my family.

**[pbu32-0199]** — Reader context: Considers shelter after family conflict; earlier reply fears child removal if using one.

> I did think of going to a women’s shelter. I can’t imagine it being worse than being stuck in a room for days and crying. I will contemplate a little more and then make a decision.

**[pbu32-0205]** — Reader context: Partner offers abortion pills but refuses accompanying author to doctor.

> I also live in a small city with fairly conservative attitudes, and I'm worried about being judged by doctors because I'm unmarried. I'm trying to find a gynecologist online but don't know whom to trust.

**Further supporting or qualifying IDs:** [pbu08-0337] [pbu08-0338] [pbu21-0226] [pbu21-0306] [pbu32-0311] [pbu32-0312]

Full wording for these IDs is either elsewhere above or in the evidence appendix below. Primary grouping is for retrieval; it does not make the quotes independent.

### 16. Medication and substance changes can remove ways to function, and both taking treatment and feeling relief can provoke fear.

**Current findings location:** §§2, 4 (attempts), 5 (care), 6, 9, 10.

**Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “The broken week-by-week script includes persistent pain, exhaustion, vomiting and incontinence, repeated remedies and disrupted sleep.”

**What should change:** Add loss of effective ADHD, anxiety, sleep, allergy or pain management; cravings and withdrawal after stopping substances; uncertainty about prescribed medicines; exposures before knowing about pregnancy; and fear of future dependence on a helpful treatment. Some people describe benefit, failure or adverse effects. The last ur08 account links a meal after Zofran and reduced nausea to fear of having killed the baby. Preserve claims, fears and speculation as attributed accounts rather than prescribing or verifying efficacy, teratogenicity or causal links.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0002]** — Reader context: Knows the stated benefit of treatment but feels guilty before the first scan.

> I’m feeling very guilty about all of these antibiotics. I know they are safe for pregnancy and that the risk of an untreated infection is worse, etc. but I’ve never been on this many in my life and of course it’s during this super delicate time. I haven’t even had my first ultrasound yet, that will be in another 2 weeks on April 13th. Not sure when I’ll be able to meet with an OB either.

**[pbu08-0017]** — Reader context: Agrees to a scan for her own safety, then feels guilty when no clot is found.

> I have a four year old daughter and even though I was terrified, I knew I had to do this for my safety and for her sake. She deserves her mom. That’s all I kept telling myself. 
> 
> I got the CT scan and my lungs are healthy. My heart is healthy. I know hindsight is 20/20, but really, the CT scan is was for nothing because I don’t have any clots or a PE. I feel so guilty. I exposed my unborn baby to radiation. 
> 
> They said that the radiation would not be over my stomach or uterus. Any exposure would be extremely minimal. I still feel horrible. 
> 
> I want my baby to be okay. I want her to be healthy and grow into a healthy adult. I made a decision based on the information I had at the time, and I know that, but I still feel guilty. I can’t stop crying.

**[pbu08-0018]** — Reader context: Later reply reports reassuring follow-up and a healthy active baby.

> But my OB wasn’t concerned at all about the CT scan and said it was the right choice. I’m 37 weeks now and baby is very happy and healthy. She regularly passes her NSTs within minutes of being on and is very active. I totally understand the guilt but you are okay and your baby will be okay.

**[pbu08-0021]** — Reader context: Wants the pregnancy despite partner pressure and fears harm from substances used before recognizing it.

> He wants me to have an abortion and I do have an appointment but it would fucking BREAK ME break me worse than I already am . I want this baby so bad. More than life it’s self but I’m so scared I did something so terrible already by doing drugs and alcohol (unknown I was pregnant) what are the chances the baby isn’t ok? What do I do. I cannot get this abortion. I’m lost and scared and beyond confused. I went on 3 benders in the past 3 weeks 2 days straight of drinking and coke use both times

**[pbu08-0022]** — Reader context: Partner reports that vague clinical reassurance worsened guilt about drinking before pregnancy recognition.

> Now she’s spiraling with guilt and fear that she somehow permanently harmed the baby before we even knew. We’ve read so many reassuring stories and seen nurses/people say early exposure before implantation is very low risk, but then at Planned Parenthood the answers felt extremely vague and neutral like “some pregnancies turn out fine, some don’t,” which honestly made her anxiety worse.

**[pbu08-0023]** — Reader context: Asks for experience and ways to cope with uncertainty without judgment.

> Has anyone gone through something similar and had a healthy pregnancy/baby afterward? Or how did you mentally cope with not knowing for sure? We’re not looking for judgment — just real experiences or perspective because this has been emotionally exhausting.

**[pbu08-0071]** — Reader context: Contradictory advice arrives while the author is waiting for an insured OB referral.

> He told me I need to stop taking my trazodone- which I asked about during appt and he initially said was fine and then I get a message from the office this morning, telling me to discontinue. And to ask my OBGYN what they recommend. My office knows I’ve been waiting for a referral to go through to an OB that covers my insurance. So they know I don’t currently have an OB, but essentially don’t care.

**[pbu08-0072]** — Reader context: Working six days a week, anticipates insomnia after stopping medication.

> What can I take to sleep?  
> I work 6 days a week and I’m not going to survive this. I’ve been on the edge of a full blown panic attack all day. I’m dreading going to sleep because I know I’m going to be awake all night.

**[pbu08-0415]** — Reader context: Nausea relief allows a meal, then feeling better creates uncertainty.

> I took a zofran last night bc my nausea had me constantly gagging all week and I couldn’t eat anything or drink so I took one and was finally able to eat a full meal. When I woke up I don’t feel nauseous as usual and feel a little better but I do feel kinda off like I don’t know how to describe it.

**[pbu08-0416]** — Reader context: Fears medication killed the baby while awaiting a first scan and Medicaid.

> Basically I’m worried if I killed my baby or not. I’m only 7 weeks and haven’t had an ultrasound yet and I’m still waiting to get Medicaid. It’s my first time ever being pregnant and I keep hearing these horror stories of miscarriages.

**[pbu21-0025]** — Reader context: Changing supplement brands caused an unnoticed fivefold dose increase.

> Im 18 weeks pregnant and i just realized that throughout my pregnancy I’ve been taking 5x more vitamin d3 than what I thought. I bought a different brand of vit d3, one pill is 5000iu. my previous brand. each pill was 1000iu, I used to take 4 of them. so I’ve stupidly been taking 20k iu of vitamin d3 per day.

**[pbu21-0026]** — Reader context: New job, long commute, pregnancy and quitting cannabis converge.

> But today was my first day on the job. It was mostly logistics and introductions, obviously, but a pretty far travel (3.5 hours; until we move, thatll be my commute unfortunately) and lots of first impressions. I am now on the train back and I feel like I've already reached some limit. I am so tired, I feel extremely moody, and my head is both buzzing and dead paralyzed at the same time. I also have combined ADHD (mostly hyperactive) and I'm noticing my symptoms are also going haywire and the meds aren't helping as much as usual. It also sucks feeling this bad that I wont be "rewarding" myself with a smoke when I get home, when the thought of "rewarding" myself with 🍃 hadnt crossed my mind since we found out.

**[pbu21-0028]** — Reader context: Asks for combined experience while waiting months for therapy.

> I just feel like im stuck between 500 rocks and a hard place. I am on a waitlist for a therapy programme, and the estimate is december...until then I am paired with a counselor that I can call in the meantime, and we have a talk scheduled soon (she doesnt know about the pregnancy yet), but I was wondering if anyone has ANY experiences or words of advice on any of these areas. Even better if you have experience with at least 2 of those things combined hah (adhd, pregnancy, starting a phd and withdrawing from weed).

**[pbu21-0098]** — Reader context: Reports medication constraints around changing blood volume.

> I do take lamotrigine as my mood stabilizer. It really was life changing for my mood and has helped keep me stable. I’m actually only on that and gabapentin for anxiety, but for some reason this anxiety is just too much. My psychiatrist doesn’t want to increase the doses right now since the blood levels will drastically rise after giving birth, so I’m kind of out of options at the moment 😕 I already had to  
> Increase it once 2nd trimester. I’m really hoping that maybe it is just the medications being so diluted by the increased blood volume that is causing me to feel this way.

**[pbu21-0141]** — Reader context: Feels treated as a drug addict after newborn symptoms attributed to prescribed medication.

> It was just a horrifying experience that has really shaken us both up. Our baby is doing alright as far as the delivery goes but is now experiencing issues from withdrawal from the Lexapro & Trazadone I’ve been taking throughout pregnancy. He is having some tremors, irritability, and issues with eating. I expressed my concerns pretty early on to multiple people but they didn’t really say anything until this morning when it was addressed. Since then, one of the nurses who was nice to begin with has been acting very short with me and I feel like holding back pain medications. Then just a little bit ago she comes in and asks if they can run a drug test on my urine & then says it’s hospital policy. I find that hard to believe because they test for drugs throughout pregnancy without asking, but now she’s asking for consent? I agreed because I have nothing to hide but it feels like I’m being treated like a drug addict.

**[pbu21-0176]** — Reader context: Family-safe marketing did not prevent fear about a household spray exposure.

> I’ve accidentally been using a bed and linen spray that has neem oil in it. I naively have been using it as it’s advertised as pet and family safe and didn’t do my diligence to check until now and I’m 11 weeks pregnant and freaking out!! I’ve change the sheets and aired out the bedroom but I’ve used this weekly since I’ve been pregnant and now I’m terrified I’ve harms my baby or will have a miscarriage. Not sure what I’m after, maybe some advice if anyone knows anything about neem oil or accidentally done something similar. I’m filled with dread, fear and guilt about it

**[pbu21-0247]** — Reader context: Comfort product becomes a source of fear after ingredient searching.

> I’m currently sick with the flu or something and I thought it would be a great idea to use the Vicks vaposhower tablet I used it like an hour ago and then I found out it has camphor in it which apparently causes miscarriages? Has anyone else used these during pregnancy and been fine

**[pbu21-0271]** — Reader context: Medication and apparently reassuring investigations do not prevent another loss.

> In my second pregnancy, I took baby aspirin and vaginal progesterone. I don’t have any of the major recurrent pregnancy loss factors. My husband’s semen analysis came back nearly perfect except for elevated white blood cells in the sample. I feel like we did everything right this time and we still had the same outcome.

**[pbu21-0328]** — Reader context: Alcohol-free labeling prompts panic after discovering 0.5% content.

> I drank 3 champagne glasses of nozeco today as it's alcohol free. But just found out it has 0.5% so now I'm spiralling? How can they call it alcohol free - I drank it in total good faith. I'm only 5 weeks - do you think this will have impacted anything? I had a miscarriage in my last pregnancy so am so so anxious.

**[pbu21-0364]** — Reader context: Early blood-pressure treatment causes symptoms that intensify medication anxiety.

> Hi! Im 13 almost 14 weeks pregnant and got prescribed Procardia xl 30mg, I took my first dose last night and feel absolutely awful all day today (headaches on and off and just feeling tired and like crap). Is this a normal side effect of the drug or should I be worried (as a person with medication anxiety it’s not helping with me feeling this way). Oh also my Bp has been running mid to high 130s/80 and not bottoming out or sky rocketing so not sure why I feel this way!

**[pbu32-0027]** — Reader context: Author asks about alcohol exposure using a metabolism-based rationale.

> would having 3 glasses of wine over the course of 8-10 hours be harmful to the baby?? Considering it takes about an hour or two to process one standard drink.. so if you have 3 over a huge period of time does it hurt anything?

**[pbu32-0052]** — Reader context: Looking back at medication interruption; restarting after weaning later helped.

> Fast forward, I stopped taking adderall while pregnant and while breastfeeding. My Dr. was personally uncomfortable with it during pregnancy and I didn't want to risk anything. But postpartum has REALLY humbled me. Btwn Prozac, sleep depreviation, hormones, breastfeeding every 2 hours, etc..I could barely function for months.

**[pbu32-0088]** — Reader context: Reports stopping vaping immediately; source's causal beliefs are unverified.

### 17. Symptoms can make eating, sleeping, walking and working impossible while familiar remedies fail or conflict.

**Current findings location:** §§2 (symptoms), 3, 4, 6 (body), 9.

**Relationship:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “The case is not for more weekly facts”

**What should change:** The issue includes basic function and pre-existing conditions, not only a broken promise of the second-trimester glow. Record severe nausea/HG, itching, cramps, pain, headaches, insomnia, mobility loss and remedies tried without relief. Some surgery or treatment restores eating and pain control. Everyday advice to exercise, eat well or simply sleep can be unusable; a remedy may conflict with another medicine or the parent's reported epilepsy. These selected passages cannot establish which remedies work.

**[pbu08-0034]** — Reader context: Repeated hospital checks end in discharge without explaining persistent contractions and vomiting.

They check on baby because he wasn’t moving much again. He’s totally okay. I’m sent home again. I finally try to sleep after 48 hours and wake up to uncontrollable vomiting.
> 
> What is going on? Has anyone else been through this? I’m desperate for answers. I was just told that it’s either early or practice labor and to try to rest. My body won’t let me fucking rest because I either wake up in pain or throwing up!!

I thought AMAZING, I can finally feel somewhat normal and BAM Tuesday I see aura and have a mild headache after. Wednesday morning BAM again with aura but no real pain after, just PURE PANIC which made me throw up lol. I have history of migraine with aura but I haven’t had one in 7 YEARS!

**[pbu08-0238]** — Reader context: Severe cramping persists despite reassuring early testing.

**[pbu08-0255]** — Reader context: Second-trimester nausea worsens despite multiple medicines and restricted tolerable foods.

B6/unisom and zofran are unhelpful. I can’t eat ANYTHING. My appetite is totally gone and the nausea is so severe I can’t stomach anything. I do the best I can eating through the nausea and trying to keep my blood sugar up. I’ve given up on trying to be balanced/nutritious and am eating mainly fruit, crackers and cheese, and ice cream/yogurt/smoothies when I can. Brushing my teeth and drinking water increase my nausea. I feel like I’ve had a 3 week long stomach bug.

It's not life threatening, thankfully, and theyre hopeful it will go away after giving birth. Im 32 weeks now, and my SVTs were the worst weeks 17-22.

I am a teacher and have had off work from weeks 9 on, which I feel was lucky as the nausea and vomiting had been awful up until the past few days. However, even though the past few days have been better I still have done nothing but lay around. I am feeling a lot of guilt about this and am wondering if this is normal? I’m just so tired and I know in a few weeks when I go back to school my life is going to be exhausting again so I almost feel like I’m trying to soak it up while I can. Is this awful for the baby? ### 18. Screening is a decision about uncertainty, information, future choices and cost, not a universal reassurance purchase.

**Current findings location:** §§4 (choice), 5, 6, 8, 9, 10; new prenatal-screening subsection.

**Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “Women explicitly ask what procedures involve and how to accept or refuse them;”

**What should change:** Include screening and diagnostic testing alongside birth procedures. Parents weigh uncertain results, invasive-test fears, when information will arrive, whether it would change continuing the pregnancy, and bills. Some seek fetal sex or information to remember a possible loss. A low-risk result can coexist with later alarming findings. One parent declines an invasive test because they would continue regardless; another worries about making a decision very late. Their understandings of test performance and risk require attribution.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0062]** — Reader context: New uncertain scan finding recalls a previous termination for fetal abnormality; wants honest outcomes.

> I know there are so many posts regarding this but looking for some more reassurance that everything is going to be okay. This is my first pregnancy after a TFMR due to acrania/anencephaly so of course I feel like everything is going to go wrong in this pregnancy too. I am 35 and will be 36 when I hopefully deliver. Today I had my 12 week NT ultrasound (which is ironically the same ultrasound where they found out our last baby had acrania) where they discovered our baby has no nasal bone. We previously had a NIPT test with fetal fraction of 11.7% and everything was low risk for baby girl. Our MFM didn’t really explain what it could be other than a possible chromosomal abnormality. We opted to not do the CVS today and wait for our 16 week early anatomy scan on October 7th where they could hopefully see the bone then and if they can’t then we opted to do an amniocentesis at that point. I did opt to get the Vistara test today through Natera to see if that would pick up any abnormalities in the meantime. Our MFM did do 2 ultrasounds to take a look for herself and she said she MIGHTVE seen a bone but also admitted she was just being hopeful so not sure if she actually saw one or not. Just seeing if anyone has had this experience and were in a similar situation as us and had good news (or tbh bad news too). I just feel like I’m spiraling out of control waiting for bad news. Thanks!

**[pbu08-0069]** — Reader context: Repeatedly checks for screening results, then buys baby supplies after reassurance.

> At twelve weeks, I had my NIPT test done. Eleven vials of blood were taken that day to check for any possible issues and to find out the gender. I held my breath, for what seemed like eternity. Checking the portal multiple times a day for the results. Two weeks later, the results came in at 4:30 in the morning. I woke my husband up so we could open them together. I couldn’t wait. I had thought about doing a gender reveal, but by then I was already too tired to plan another party or gathering. So, there we were, half awake, opening the results together. It was a boy! More than anything, I felt relief. The test showed no issues, and for the first time in weeks, I felt like I could breathe. I went on to purchasing pacifiers, onsies, blankets, teddy bear stuffies and more, all in colors ranging from green to blue.

**[pbu08-0385]** — Reader context: High screening result prompts distress; demands to stay positive feel empty.

> Every second since that phone call has felt like hell on earth. Based on what I’ve read online, 6.4 is like craaazzyyy high and the base is like 2.5 or 2.8. Everyone in my circle keeps telling me to be positive but that’s such a load of shit to me honestly. How am I supposed to be positive after receiving that kind of information?

**[pbu08-0386]** — Reader context: Seeks outcomes and self-compassion from people who have faced similar results.

> Has anybody received these results with numbers as high as mine and come out the other side of it with a healthy baby? 6.4 is like 2.5 times the normal amount. I haven’t stopped crying. I just need to hear from people who have been through something similar, what the outcome was, and how you gave yourself grace through it. I’m having a really hard time processing everything and it is hitting me like a freight train now that my toddler is napping and I am finally alone.

**[pbu08-0394]** — Reader context: Reports the unexpected bill for prenatal screening.

> Just got my $3,399 NIPT Bill

**[pbu08-0395]** — Reader context: Unexpected out-of-network bill threatens debt repayment and savings before birth.

> I plan to talk to Natera (provider who did the test) to see about financial assistance and/or self pay discounts. But we had a plan to pay off all our debts and put more into savings before my due date, and if I have to pay this in full it will derail that plan.

**[pbu08-0396]** — Reader context: Says testing was purchased mainly for reassurance after missed miscarriage.

> The test was basically unnecessary for us, as we aren't high risk for anything. My last pregnancy was a MMC so this was mainly done for reassurance.

**[pbu21-0007]** — Reader context: Disagrees with wife about early sex testing and how loss would feel.

> She's also really anxious about knowing the gender and wants to pay for an at home sneak peek gender test because she's scared that, if we lose this pregnancy, she at least wants to know what gender they would've been (I tell her that this pregnancy will be fine and there's nothing to worry about, and even if something did happen, I think knowing the gender would only make it harder, mainly because my wife wants a girl really badly, and if we were to find out it's a girl and miscarry, that would be way harder for my wife than if we didn't know at all. Another part of it is that I've heard these sneak peeks can be inaccurate and easily contaminated especially if there are males in the household; overall it seems better to way for NIPT from what I've seen considering how much my wife wants a girl; on top of this I don't want to pay for extra testing that may be inaccurate because we're trying to save money which has always been my strength but not my wife's).

**[pbu21-0010]** — Reader context: Agreed to a reveal and now carries other people’s expectations.

> This time I gave into pressure, thinking it would be helpful for shopping and that I'd feel fine. I made the plan to do a blood draw while visiting my US family and let them do a reveal so they could feel involved in this pregnancy even though we live abroad now. I had my SneakPeek clinical draw a few days ago and my mom got the results last night. She's doing a reveal for us this evening. I was kind of excited before but today I'm just sad. I would have been equally happy with either gender but my whole family, husband included, is talking about how much they hope for it to be a girl. So I'm carrying anxiety over everyone else's potential disappointment and mourning the surprise I really wanted.

**[pbu21-0029]** — Reader context: Weighs diagnostic certainty against the procedure’s reported risk.

> I have a meeting with a genetic counselor and he said I could do an Amniocentesis procedure to find out 100% but that has its own risk (low risk of pregnancy loss with it). I don't want to do the amniocentesis for no reason if there is any risk of pregnancy loss and if he's healthy. Really don't know what to do.

**[pbu21-0159]** — Reader context: Antenatal screening brought procedures, uncertain genetic findings and termination discussions.

> When we were pregnant one of the ultra sounds popped a positive for elevated NT score. Then came specialists, more procedures, talks about downs, cardiac issues, and terminating. Finally animostenstiths test came back with a genetic mutation. Brace for the worst. Doc literally said this mutation has zero scientific study and everything fine.

**[pbu21-0165]** — Reader context: Searches for meaningful interpretation of a genetic screening result.

> The test said that she is at risk for something called DiGeorge syndrome aka 22q11.2 deletion syndrome. Wanted to see if anyone else here has heard of this and what it means for my little girl. I've done some research on Google and it says that it's a very varied syndrome with some people having certain symptoms and others not so much. Hoping just to get some more insight as to help my daughter best! Thank you all!!

**[pbu21-0170]** — Reader context: Soft marker and incomplete anatomy scan unsettle prior low-risk screening.

> Thursday I got a call from my doctor to inform my that on Monday they flagged echogenic bowel on the ultrasound, the rest of the measurements were normal, but they are still missing the face and heart measurements so we are hoping they are able to take those tomorrow and they are normal. I’m so scared and sad, I had two early losses before this pregnancy and I’m a FTM so I’m terrified of something bad happening. I would like to add that my NIPT was low risk for everything and NT was perfect.

**[pbu21-0216]** — Reader context: Worsening findings turn reassurance into frantic searching and uncertainty.

> Fast forward today and our life completely came to a halt. The follow-up scan now showed measurements of 14.9mm and \~15mm. Needless to say we were shocked and the specialist now is recommending we have the baby in the NICU and is warning us the baby will have a disability just don’t know how severe. The crying car ride home and the frantic googling has probably not helped but I’m in a bit of a downward spiral. I’m reaching out to see if anyone can share experiences with Ventriculomegaly. I’m hoping some of these stories may help my wife deal with emotions I can’t even imagine. Just trying to help my wife in anyway possible… I’m an emotional wreck and I don’t even feel the baby moving and kicking every moment like she’s does.

**[pbu21-0261]** — Reader context: Fertility history shapes expectation of being considered high risk.

> Am I just in infertility mind and am so stressed that I’m jumping to conclusions. When I spoke with my obgyn the doctor said there is no reason to assume I am high risk or am having a high risk pregnancy at this point which really surprised me.

**[pbu21-0357]** — Reader context: Severe diagnosis makes birth intervention and possible loss immediate realities.

> My baby has been diagnosed with a serious genetic condition which will make his life incredibly hard. Without huge medical intervention at birth he won't make it. Why must we experience losses of pregnancies or babies? Please can someone explain why this happens.

**[pbu21-0370]** — Reader context: Suspected late diabetes creates competing fears about vaginal birth and future sections.

> So now I’m worried about possible undiagnosed late-onset GD and possible harm caused to my baby, and on top of that, terrified of vaginal delivery. I’ve read AC over 35cm increases risk for shoulder dystocia and birth injuries, and generally if abdomen/shoulder size is proportionally bigger than head. And he already has AC of 35cm at 37 weeks… I’m seriously considering a planned C-section, but I’m also scared of the risks for future pregnancies.

**[pbu32-0006]** — Reader context: Questions test accuracy after an earlier loss.

> I just got my NIPT test results back and am wondering if I can truly trust the report for gender? Has anyone had an incorrect gender?

**[pbu32-0009]** — Reader context: Asks about a pregnancy complication following previous procedures.

> Hi— did anyone have cervical insufficiency after one or two D&C’s in the first trimester? I’m reading that it’s a low risk.

**[pbu32-0029]** — Reader context: Reading scan measurements creates worry despite clinician reassurance.

> Also on the 20 week report I noticed the Nuchal fold is 5.0 mm which is below 6.0 mm but not by much. He didn't bring that up. I just saw it on the report. I'm worried because of my age, 42, but OB doesn't seem that worried.

**[pbu32-0037]** — Reader context: After uneventful amniocentesis, uncertainty changes into self-blame about choosing the test.

> Given that we were not going to terminate the pregnancy, I feel like I have been a terrible mom and took a risk that was unreasonable. Given that my husband only agreed to it because I wanted to do it, it makes me feel even shittier.

**[pbu32-0077]** — Reader context: Considers paying privately for NIPT in addition to NHS screening.

> After two miscarriages, I'm just looking to gain as much information about this current pregnancy as I can so that I can give it the best chance possible.

> I feel like it's a lot of money to justify but of course it's worth it for peace of mind.

**[pbu32-0079]** — Reader context: Compares low-risk screening result to other women's lower figures.

> I realise my risk is low, but I see some women get 1/10000 results for example. 1/5000 feels quite high given the birth rate per year.

**[pbu32-0090]** — Reader context: Seeks NIPT for fetal sex; this rationale was absent from existing quote.

> The chromosomal stuff doesn't bother me. If baby is going to have down's syndrome, me knowing earlier doesn't change anything. We'd still keep baby and love them the same.

**[pbu32-0335]** — Reader context: Positive prior Ultrasound Direct experience informs intended NIPT booking; no new purchase claimed.

### 19. Recovery can prevent basic self-care or baby care, and interpreting serious symptoms as normal can delay returning for help.

**Current findings location:** §2 (recovery), §5 (care), §6, §10 For 5.

**Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “Recovery is not just a supplies checklist;”

**What should change:** Describe the physical tasks that fail, including getting out of bed, walking, using the toilet, sex, feeding and holding the newborn. Add readmission and recovery taking place in hospital while someone else cares for the baby. Accounts of a CSF leak, postpartum cardiomyopathy, retained products, clots and severe infection show why a supplies list and generic reassurance are incomplete. Keep relief after returning for care. A new pregnancy can begin before recovery feels complete.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0133]** — Reader context: Repeated tears and partially failed epidurals combine with grief about formula feeding.

> I’ve been struggling big time during postpartum recovery this time around. Everything has been making me extremely emotional and sad. I don’t know what to do with my emotions, I’ve been crying so much about everything. Like one minute I’m okay and the next I’m crying again over stuff that I thought I was past.   
> I’ve been experiencing grief over my birth trauma, not being able to breastfeed, not being able to get the baby to sleep without being held.   
> I have birth trauma over partially failed epidurals both deliveries, ended up with 3rd degree tears both times so recovery has been hell.   
> And I’m choosing to exclusively formula this time around because of the toll breastfeeding took on my mental health with my first, my breast milk is starting to dry up and it’s making me very sad and feel like a failure.

**[pbu08-0134]** — Reader context: Wants more children but not another pregnancy, birth or newborn period.

> I’ve also been feeling grief about knowing I probably will never have anymore kids, which is the weirdest feeling because I don’t want to go through pregnancy, childbirth or newborn stage again. I would love to have more kids but after the trauma I went through, I don’t think I will.

**[pbu08-0135]** — Reader context: Seeks a postpartum section-recovery workout program where apps mainly offer prenatal workouts.

> I’ve noticed lots of apps have prenatal workout series but not much postpartum. I had a c-section 13 weeks ago and I’ve done a grand total of one workout since lol. Not that I was crazy active before but I’d love a good postpartum c section program to follow to help heal as well as possible!

**[pbu08-0142]** — Reader context: Medical clearance after tearing has not removed fear of sex, pain or infection.

> I’m 8 weeks postpartum and my husband has been so patiently waiting for us to have sex again. I’ve been wanting this level of intimacy back in our marriage but I’m so scared!
> 
> I had a 2nd degree tear and inner tearing as well, and was cleared at my 6 week appointment. I did start to bleed a little for a few days after my appointment, so I wanted to hold off a few more weeks. 
> 
> I’m terrified of the pain everyone talks about and I don’t want get an infection or something. My mind is going to worst case scenario. I haven’t had sex since I was 34 weeks pregnant, and it was painful then. 
> 
> Give it to me straight here.. what should I expect? I know everyone’s experience is different but I’m low key wondering if this is too soon?

**[pbu08-0143]** — Reader context: Reports failed epidurals and procedures without explanation.

> Let me just say my birth experience was a nightmare for many different reasons. The anesthesiologist who was doing my epidural was seemingly in a rush, didn’t educate me at all as to what was going on, and didn’t really talk to me at all either. The first attempt failed. He hit a nerve making my right leg jump, which in turn, made the puncture hole for the epidural worse, causing more CSF fluid to leak. Following this, he ended up doing a blood patch immediately, but did not even tell me what was going on*.* Well the second attempt failed as well, and I was only numbed at my legs, causing me to basically have an unmedicated birth. The entire epidural process was pointless.

**[pbu08-0144]** — Reader context: Repeated reassurance and ineffective medication leave severe headache disabling newborn care.

> The nurses kept telling me “oh the spinal headache goes away on its own in around three days” and would give me an Ibuprofen 600 which absolutely did *nothing* for me. I suffer from migraines, so I’m no stranger to bad headaches. I held out hope that they were right and pushed through it. The day of my discharge, the headache continuously got worse, which I didn’t even think was possible at this point. Again, I was reassured it would go away. At this point I was starting to get irritated because I’m freshly postpartum, bleeding, with a newborn baby, and in catastrophic pain from this CSF leak. How am I supposed to be a mother?

**[pbu08-0145]** — Reader context: Reports major relief after returning to emergency care.

> Immediately after that blood patch I had the most relief I’d ever felt. Forget giving birth, CSF leaks are no joke.

**[pbu08-0387]** — Reader context: Attributes serious symptoms to anxiety or normal recovery and feels guilty leaving baby to seek hospital care.

> After being discharged from the hospital I was really struggling with shortness of breath and I started having more swelling throughout my body. I have struggled with anxiety and panic attacks throughout my life so I attributing  my racing heart and shortness of breath to that. Three days after being released I couldn’t deal anymore and went to the emergency room where I was diagnosed with postpartum cardiomyopathy. I’ve been in the hospital now for 5 days being treated for congestive heart failure caused by pregnancy. By the time I sought treatment my efv was only 20% which I’ve learned is quite low. I thought what I was feeling was normal postpartum symptoms and I was just a wimp  unable to handle them. I felt so guilty going to the hospital and leaving my husband and baby but I’m glad I pushed through that.

**[pbu21-0075]** — Reader context: Unexpected surgery, month-long pain and difficult breastfeeding recovery.

> In July I had my baby. It wasn't an emergency C-Section, but an unexpected one. My first was vaginal, and I just wasn't expecting it at all. He came out perfect, but I was dealing with a recovery that was a bit harder (imo) than a vaginal birth. I struggled with so much pain the first month of his life. I was determined to breast feed him for at least 6 weeks, but I made it to 3 months before I switched him to formula. I started experiencing what I can only figure was D-MER and formula was a sanity saver.

**[pbu21-0095]** — Reader context: Readmission for severe complications separates parent and newborn.

> Finally, we were home. One day after, I started to have pain in my right upper abdomen. The pain continued to worsen, spread down to my right lower abdomen. It got to the point where I couldn’t get out of bed without help and could hardly walk. Two days after, I ended up in the ER. I was diagnosed with a blood clot in my ovarian vein which became infected, leading to something called septic pelvic thrombophlebitis, and pre-eclampsia with severe features. I was admitted to the ICU for magnesium drip, antibiotics and pain control. I was there for 5 days, missing initial bonding time with my baby.

**[pbu21-0096]** — Reader context: Feels blamed by her body and medication despite outwardly good outcomes.

> I feel like a failure. I couldn’t deliver him, and he ended up with so many complications. A medication I was taking caused him harm. Then we finally get home and I had to leave him again. I feel like shit, and I don’t know if the guilt will ever go away. And everyone around me seems to just have pushed forward, because I’m doing fine and he’s doing fine, but I feel like complete shit. Now I can’t even breastfeed because I’m on blood thinners for months so there’s another thing. I just can’t handle it.

**[pbu21-0100]** — Reader context: Describes unexpected hair loss after miscarriage surgery.

> We recently had an oops that ended in miscarriage and I opted for a d&c surgery under general anesthesia and HOLY HELL the hair loss was real. It was a very early miscarriage but no doubt triggered a major hormonal shift, but then I heard about a link between broadly any surgery/anesthesia and hair loss.

**[pbu21-0129]** — Reader context: Delayed discovery of retained tissue after bleeding since birth.

> So I just found out today I have retained products left from pregnancy/birth and i have to go in for a D&C today!! I am almost 12 weeks post partum. Just posting this as heads up for others to keep an eye on your symptoms and tell your dr! I've been bleeding off and on since, thought I got my period 2 times, and had what I thought was BV. Turns out it is not! I've had pain in my uterus and a foul odor. My dr sent me for an ultrasound just to rule out retained products and sure enough... there are some. Please watch for symptoms and don't be afraid to call the dr and be annoying!! Wish me luck❤️😬

**[pbu21-0134]** — Reader context: Describes catheterization, incontinence and wheelchair use after birth.

> The worse thing is the incontinence.  After birth pee fell right out of me and I ended up having a catheter for a day.  Recovery was very painful and I had to get a wheelchair after baby appt because I was in so much pain.

**[pbu21-0135]** — Reader context: Prior injury drives questions about a future section.

> Would a c section for a 2nd birth be a little easier?  Also with the damage I had, idk if a 2nd birth could make the issues worse....and that is super sad

**[pbu21-0142]** — Reader context: Asks what support exists when disability prevents infant care.

> If I don't recover and am unfit to care for the baby due to limited mobility and disability, what happens? Does CPS intervene? Can I ask a hospital for resources? Is there some sort of govt assistance navigating this scenario? Would I be forced to give baby up for adoption? I very much want her, but it's getting to the point where it's impossible to meet her needs.

**[pbu21-0221]** — Reader context: Asks for practical hygiene preparation for surgery at a larger body size.

> My question is when it comes to the recovery side of things (specifically using the bathroom) how did you plus size girlies do it? The reach around to wipe I'm sure isn't easy... I do have a peri bottle but something about not wiping just makes me feel dirty. Has anyone else struggled with this or these thoughts? How did you get through it? Any tips for my upcoming recovery bathroom trips? I would love any helpful suggestions. Thank you in advance!!!

**[pbu21-0409]** — Reader context: Physical recovery remains incomplete when another pregnancy starts at four months postpartum.

> Anyways here’s the dilemma. My partner has left it up to me what we do. He says we want 2 and he’s happy if we keep the baby, but it’s also my body & I should do what’s best for me.  
> Sounds simple, but I’ve never wanted to have an abortion. Trust me I’m pro-choice, but I never thought I’d be in this situation. At the same time, I’m not healed at all. I’ve heard it takes 18m. I had preeclampsia, then 2 weeks after birth I was in and out of hospital. I just started feeling better physically like 2 weeks ago & even that’s not completely better. The past 2 days I’ve been feeling depleted, which makes sense now. But even if I can mentally do two under one, I don’t think I physically can. I think ik what I have to do, but I’m having trouble coming to terms with it.

### 20. Feeding decisions involve pain, supply, medication, sleep, trauma and protecting mental health, as well as technique.

**Current findings location:** §§2 (feeding/recovery), 3, 5, 6, 10 For 5.

**Relationship:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “The first weeks include incapacity, feeding pressure and delayed connection.”

**What should change:** Keep guilt and grief even when formula is a self-directed or helpful choice. Supply questions can need a specific lactation service rather than more latch advice; a demanding pump/feed routine can coexist with low supply. Tandem feeding, aversion during pregnancy and fear of weaning affect later pregnancies. Include newborn reflux, weight monitoring and failed attempts at relief. Authors' explanations linking drugs, diet or products to milk and infant outcomes are testimony.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0100]** — Reader context: From the first night home, tries feeding and bassinet strategies including a Snoo.

> But it’s been so difficult. We hold her up after eating for 45 min at least but as soon as she goes on her back, she freaks out. Sometimes we’ll get 2 hours in the snoo but that’s few and far between. (We tried traditional bassinet and she will not go down in it. The lactation consultant we met with doesn’t like the snoo but we’re desperate.)

**[pbu08-0112]** — Reader context: Weaning and toddler tantrums exhaust a parent expecting twins again.

> They’re still breastfeeding and I’ve been desperate to wean them but it leads to so many tantrums but the sensation of breastfeeding while pregnant is awful and makes my skin crawl. One is also potty trained since 16 months while the other one still isn’t fully potty trained, it’s a lot. Basically we are in the terrible 2s, potty training, trying to wean off the boob. A lot on our plate.  
> I don’t know how I’ll get through this whole pregnancy while chasing my girls all day and dealing with huge tantrums. I know I have to stay firm but I’ve already given in to whatever they asked me a couple of times now as I was just too exhausted to deal with 2h of moaning and screaming and just staying at the playground felt easier even though that meant cancelling my own plans with a friend.

**[pbu08-0124]** — Reader context: Reply adds collapse, transfusion and unexpectedly tiny baby clothing to an otherwise positive birth narrative.

> After I gave birth I was so desperate to poo I stood up and passed out due to sheer exhaustion and ended up needing a blood transfusion. My baby was born so teeny tiny (2nd percentile), that non of his clothes fit and my husband had to do a mad dash to m&s and buy their 'Tiny Baby' range. He was so small he fell asleep every time I breastfed him because he had absolutely no energy 💔. He's 20 weeks old today, and he's the absolute best thing that ever happened to me, after 4 recurrent miscarriages ❤️

**[pbu08-0133]** — Reader context: Repeated tears and partially failed epidurals combine with grief about formula feeding.

> I’ve been struggling big time during postpartum recovery this time around. Everything has been making me extremely emotional and sad. I don’t know what to do with my emotions, I’ve been crying so much about everything. Like one minute I’m okay and the next I’m crying again over stuff that I thought I was past.   
> I’ve been experiencing grief over my birth trauma, not being able to breastfeed, not being able to get the baby to sleep without being held.   
> I have birth trauma over partially failed epidurals both deliveries, ended up with 3rd degree tears both times so recovery has been hell.   
> And I’m choosing to exclusively formula this time around because of the toll breastfeeding took on my mental health with my first, my breast milk is starting to dry up and it’s making me very sad and feel like a failure.

**[pbu08-0141]** — Reader context: Wants supplement options for recovery and breastfeeding beyond the OB's prenatal recommendation.

I’m totally cool with this but I was hoping to get some ideas/input/experiences from yall when it comes to other options for supplements? I have a few days left of my prenatal and trying to decide what to purchase!   
> My priorities would be recovery and breastfeeding support however I’m really interested in any thoughts you have!  
> TIA!!

**[pbu08-0146]** — Reader context: At five days, latch problems and fear of insufficient intake lead to formula top-ups.

> Feeling really overwhelmed with breastfeeding and like I have no idea what I’m doing. I struggled to harvest colostrum, baby struggles to latch, and I’ve been scared she isn’t getting enough so we started topping up with formula. She’s only 5 days old. Did I jump the gun on formula??

**[pbu08-0147]** — Reader context: Wonders whether a section has delayed milk while only expressing droplets.

> The longest she’s really stayed on the boob is maybe 5–6 minutes max on one side, and even then I don’t think the positioning or latch is right. She gets really fussy at the boob and still seems hungry and cries after ages of trying both sides. I had a c-section so I’m wondering if my milk is delayed . I’m on day 5 and can still only hand express tiny droplets at a time.

**[pbu08-0150]** — Reader context: Multiple gas remedies fail; upright holding helps only sometimes.

> At first, we thought it was gas/colic, but colic exercises do not help. Infacol did not help. Putting him in colic positions made him even angrier. Then we noticed he needing burping a lot (which apparently is uncommon for breastfed babies). He can do two large burps after one breast feed. We also find his crying is alleviated 50% of the time by being held upright. We haven’t seen excessive reflux/vomiting but occasionally small amounts of bubbling milk comes out his mouth after a feed.

**[pbu08-0151]** — Reader context: Questions why increasing a prescribed feed thickener would help after no visible improvement.

> All this led us to conclude he has silent reflux, GP agreed and has prescribed Gaviscon. He’s having 1 sachet twice a day, one morning one night in a bottle, but so far it has not made any difference. I can increase these but I feel like it isn’t likely to do much, since my understanding of Gaviscon infant is that it isn’t a drug that “builds up” in the system and changes the stomach physiology (i.e. like omeprazole does for the pH) but instead works on a per-feed basis at thickening up that feed and making it harder for it to reflux. If he has still cried intensely after each feed I’ve used Gaviscon, I don’t see how using it on more feeds will make any difference?

**[pbu08-0186]** — Reader context: Fear of falling asleep while feeding makes nursing-pillow usefulness, price and multipurpose value urgent.

> I tend to have a sort of narcolepsy response where I involuntarily fall asleep during important tasks if I don't get good sleep, so this has been my biggest worry. Doctors haven't been much help on this concern, they just tell me to get better sleep and *somehow* that hasn't been a watertight solution, so I rely on myself to manage it in different ways. The narcolepsy-type response I have you'd think would give me a no-brainer "oh yes, I should probably buy this nursing pillow so I don't worry about immediate dangers if I fall asleep while holding my baby", but I also see some moms who come out saying "literally you can use a regular pillow and it works fine" so I get a bit stuck there. There's also the question of what I would possibly use the nursing pillow for when I don't need it, do I just...sell it? That seems like a waste of money, which we are tight on. So I'd really appreciate any personal opinions about nursing pillows; whether you think they're necessary, whether you'd definitely or totally recommend them for my situation or a pillow would do the exact same thing I'm looking for (comfort and baby's safety), AND I'd especially appreciate if there are any recommendations for nursing pillows that have uses outside of being a nursing pillow, as the good quality ones I tend to see everywhere..only have that one use, I'd much rather have something multipurpose.

**[pbu21-0075]** — Reader context: Unexpected surgery, month-long pain and difficult breastfeeding recovery.

> In July I had my baby. It wasn't an emergency C-Section, but an unexpected one. My first was vaginal, and I just wasn't expecting it at all. He came out perfect, but I was dealing with a recovery that was a bit harder (imo) than a vaginal birth. I struggled with so much pain the first month of his life. I was determined to breast feed him for at least 6 weeks, but I made it to 3 months before I switched him to formula. I started experiencing what I can only figure was D-MER and formula was a sanity saver.

**[pbu21-0077]** — Reader context: A ten-minute post-birth visit centered photos and breastfeeding judgment.

We allowed her to come and visit a couple weeks after the baby was born. She literally came in, held the baby, had my husband take pictures of her, told me I need to be breastfeeding and then left. Literally was there for 10 minutes. Then proceeded to post all over social media that she was soo in love with “her baby”

**[pbu21-0096]** — Reader context: Feels blamed by her body and medication despite outwardly good outcomes.

I feel like shit, and I don’t know if the guilt will ever go away. And everyone around me seems to just have pushed forward, because I’m doing fine and he’s doing fine, but I feel like complete shit. Now I can’t even breastfeed because I’m on blood thinners for months so there’s another thing. I just can’t handle it.

**[pbu21-0144]** — Reader context: Recalls frightening behavior and severe symptoms after returning home.

I was sleep deprived, desperately trying to breastfeed a baby with CMPA and reflux (issues now resolved……somewhat), hallucinating, and struggling massively with my OCD mixing with PPA. Every day was a battle. And his temper got worse. There were times that were completely unacceptable; he’d always had a bit of a leashed temper (usually game related and wasn’t a problem in our relationship) but then the baby crying got to him. There were instances of him screaming at the dog while holding the baby, kicking our dog while holding the baby, and aggressively sitting the baby across his knee to rip off a shirt when she spit up on it. I was appalled and furious.

### 21. Another pregnancy changes capacity and older-child care, and pregnancy number cannot stand in for the actual family history.

**Current findings location:** §§2, 9, 10 For 3 and 8, 16.5, 17.4.

**Relationship:** Extends and qualifies an assumption.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “Second-time content must assume she has a child to mind”

**What should change:** Replace 'assume' with asking about living children and their needs. A second or sixth pregnancy may follow losses rather than include the expected number of children. Existing children can have disabilities, therapy schedules or strong attachment needs; the parent may be widowed, still breastfeeding, or recovering. Include first parenthood through surrogacy and different pregnancy histories between partners. Labor-day childcare is only part of this capacity problem.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0001]** — Reader context: Early pregnancy, repeated antibiotics and caring for a toddler while the husband works away.

> My body is so run down and sick from all of the antibiotics and also just being pregnant. I have a 3 year old and I struggle everyday to be the mom I want to be. My husband works away from home and is often gone for weeks at a time.

**[pbu08-0073]** — Reader context: Poor health reduces perceived capacity for the hoped-for third child.

> I have honestly been lowkey begging my husband to let me have one more child for a few years (we have a 6 and a 4 year old), but the older my existing kids get, and the shittier I feel, the less capable I feel of adding a third child. And that does kind of break my heart. I love my children, but I do already struggle to be a consistent parent for them, and that causes me a lot of distress, so I absolutely worry about the strain of having another child.

**[pbu08-0074]** — Reader context: Eight months pregnant, runs through the building searching for a toddler.

Still haven’t found her. Fully panicking now.

**[pbu08-0088]** — Reader context: Recalls being left caring for five children, including newborn twins, after hospital discharge.

I feel like I am a single mom.

**[pbu08-0094]** — Reader context: Pregnancy exhaustion increases screen use for a nine-month-old and guilt despite knowing recommendations.

### 22. Useful preparation must fit actual needs and remain usable during the event; purchase alone does not create readiness or support.

**Current findings location:** §§4 (classes/doulas/products), 6 (requests), 8, 10, 11, 15, 17.4.

**Relationship:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “The commercial complaint is specific.”

**What should change:** Add service access rules: one parent repeatedly checks the doula packet to know whether she is allowed to text during induction, then says the paid support never arrived. Buying organizers does not make the home ready when a partner forbids bringing them in. Parents explicitly ask for tailored programs, resources, packing advice, reliable products and specific specialists; these are varied requests rather than one demonstrated demand for the proposed guide. Clothing's value can depend on postpartum reuse, and a nursing pillow's value on multiple uses. No Mamivio purchase or price acceptance is established here.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0055]** — Reader context: Seeks vetted, digestible resources for a first-time single mother leaving an abusive relationship.

I’m legit worried for her and her baby.. does anyone have any suggestions for resources that are in general GOOD for first time parents, and also anything for single mothers of sons? Anything to know about post partum depression? Anything about surviving narcissistic abuse during or after pregnancy? Anything about lifespan development and milestones… podcasts/audiobooks/youtubers/youtube videos???
