> I’ve noticed lots of apps have prenatal workout series but not much postpartum. I had a c-section 13 weeks ago and I’ve done a grand total of one workout since lol. Not that I was crazy active before but I’d love a good postpartum c section program to follow to help heal as well as possible!

**[pbu08-0141]** — Reader context: Wants supplement options for recovery and breastfeeding beyond the OB's prenatal recommendation.

> I’m almost a month postpartum and EBF my little boy. I’ve asked my OB for recommendations on supplements and he really only directed me to continue my prenatal. I’m totally cool with this but I was hoping to get some ideas/input/experiences from yall when it comes to other options for supplements? I have a few days left of my prenatal and trying to decide what to purchase!   
> My priorities would be recovery and breastfeeding support however I’m really interested in any thoughts you have!  
> TIA!!

**[pbu08-0178]** — Reader context: Targeted maternity-clothing ads offer expensive items after earlier clothes were given away.

> This is likely my last pregnancy (number 3) And I gave away the majority of my maternity clothes thinking we were done at 2! I keep getting targeted for Soon Maternity clothes, where they advertise shorts and pants you can wear pregnant and postpartum with their elastic waist band. They’re on the pricey side, at $115-150 a pair.

**[pbu08-0179]** — Reader context: Willing to pay for maternity clothes if they remain useful after weight loss.

> Has anyone bought from them? Do you recommend it? Can you really wear them postpartum? I’m willing to spend the money on a single pair of shorts and pants if it’s something I can truly wear even after I’ve lost the baby weight. Curious of your thoughts!

**[pbu08-0186]** — Reader context: Fear of falling asleep while feeding makes nursing-pillow usefulness, price and multipurpose value urgent.

> I tend to have a sort of narcolepsy response where I involuntarily fall asleep during important tasks if I don't get good sleep, so this has been my biggest worry. Doctors haven't been much help on this concern, they just tell me to get better sleep and *somehow* that hasn't been a watertight solution, so I rely on myself to manage it in different ways. The narcolepsy-type response I have you'd think would give me a no-brainer "oh yes, I should probably buy this nursing pillow so I don't worry about immediate dangers if I fall asleep while holding my baby", but I also see some moms who come out saying "literally you can use a regular pillow and it works fine" so I get a bit stuck there. There's also the question of what I would possibly use the nursing pillow for when I don't need it, do I just...sell it? That seems like a waste of money, which we are tight on. So I'd really appreciate any personal opinions about nursing pillows; whether you think they're necessary, whether you'd definitely or totally recommend them for my situation or a pillow would do the exact same thing I'm looking for (comfort and baby's safety), AND I'd especially appreciate if there are any recommendations for nursing pillows that have uses outside of being a nursing pillow, as the good quality ones I tend to see everywhere..only have that one use, I'd much rather have something multipurpose.

**[pbu08-0187]** — Reader context: Wants to prioritize useful preparation over unnecessary nesting tasks.

> But, I wanna make sure I'm doing the *right* things to prepare rather than kill a bunch of time doing unnecessary stuff.

**[pbu08-0249]** — Reader context: Pays for prenatal paternity testing but partner still requests a post-birth test.

> I’m (32/F) currently 34wks pregnant and I wanted to know if anyone had done a paternity test before the baby was born and what did your results say. Did the results come out accurate once you had the baby? I paid for one and the father still wants to get his own once the baby is born. I don’t like that but it’s up to him.  
>  Can anyone let me know what their results said?

**[pbu08-0336]** — Reader context: Stores feeding equipment and recovery supplies in a hot car or storage unit at the partner's insistence.

> So far, I have kept everything baby needs either in my car (in the middle of summer heat) or in a storage unit. Just today, I decided to just bring in the bottle sterilizer, my pumps, baby bottles, some creams, & postpartum supplies and got lots of negative remarks and feedback on that. He told me to put the stuff back in my car. I don’t want the things to get damaged and it’s exhausting running back and forth to a storage unit alone putting baby items back and forth.

**[pbu08-0350]** — Reader context: Wants updated products and advice for combining school-aged children with a newborn.

> For anyone who has three, I'm here for your advice (apart from obvious things like don't announce yet, don't get too excited until you reach a certain point, etc.). How do you juggle school aged children with an infant? Any new products I don't know about that are life savers (the last time I did this was 2020)?

**[pbu21-0054]** — Reader context: Feels guilty for using conception vitamins before discovering pregnancy on holiday.

> I am currently on holidays and I only took with me the pre natal vitamins (proceive conception). On holidays I discovered that I was pregnant and I kept taking the proceive conception just to have the adequate amount of folic acid. I haven’t gone to the doctor yet but I know it’s been roughly 20 days after conception. The thing now is that Im feeling extremely guilty for not being prepared and not taking the first trimester vitamins for the last three weeks or so, and also for not having them with me. But again, I had no clue I was pregnant. I am flying back home tomorrow and on Monday will buy the first trimester medicine.

**[pbu21-0102]** — Reader context: Wants basic care steps and preparation after poorly explained miscarriage care.

> I literally have no idea on anything and i’m relying on google searches for everything. I had little to none support post miscarriage even in a&e they wasn’t clear on what was going on so it was quite hard for me. I don’t really even know what steps to take now in terms of doctor appointments etc so any advice at all will help a lot. Also advice on prenatals etc would be great as i have never taken them and haven’t started yet so also slightly worried about that. I just want to prepare myself for this journey ahead as much as i can especially with what to expect for first trimester in these upcoming weeks. Thank you 🩷

**[pbu21-0221]** — Reader context: Asks for practical hygiene preparation for surgery at a larger body size.

> My question is when it comes to the recovery side of things (specifically using the bathroom) how did you plus size girlies do it? The reach around to wipe I'm sure isn't easy... I do have a peri bottle but something about not wiping just makes me feel dirty. Has anyone else struggled with this or these thoughts? How did you get through it? Any tips for my upcoming recovery bathroom trips? I would love any helpful suggestions. Thank you in advance!!!

**[pbu21-0273]** — Reader context: Paid $100 for private reassurance while awaiting first care appointment.

> Also because doctors are so backed up here I had to wait so long for my appointment I’m literally like 13 weeks?! so a few weeks ago I paid 100$ to get a private ultrasound because I had a miscarriage at 11 weeks back in January, I brought the photos apparently that was completely useless to them.

**[pbu21-0307]** — Reader context: Bereavement reduces concentration for registry decisions and newborn preparation.

> I am trying to get the essentials where the baby sleeps and lays to be greenguard gold certified and as nontoxic as possible.   
> I’m worried about buying things that I don’t need as we have a small home and live minimally. With her loss, it’s just been really hard to concentrate on getting this registry together. I have only had time to work and just trying to keep myself healthy and read about preparing for labor and how to actually care for the newborn when they come.

**[pbu21-0309]** — Reader context: Prior stillbirth delays readiness and car-seat purchase even after prior loss milestone.

> I just really need to vent. I am currently 35 weeks pregnant with my second baby boy after a 3rd trimester stillbirth in 2022. I honestly thought that after I made it past the point in which I lost his big brother that I would begin to feel more excited for this baby, but I have honestly felt anything but. It’s been so bad that I literally JUST purchased his car seat & have been dragging my foot packing his hospital bag.

**[pbu21-0347]** — Reader context: Anticipated maternity clothing needs prompt considering buy-now-pay-later spending.

> All this was triggered by me having an absolute crash out that nothing in my closet fits comfortably and I'm going to have to buy now pay later a capsule wardrobe of maternity cloths. I'm 16 weeks, no sense in siving up when ill just have to do it again in a month.

**[pbu21-0355]** — Reader context: Shopping for maternity clothes was an investment in feeling good during a last pregnancy.

> In early August, I found out I was expecting my 4th baby (3rd pregnancy, I had twins first time). It was unexpected and I was so excited to be having another baby. Since it was my 3rd and probably last pregnancy, I decided to spoil myself and went on a shopping spree (mostly secondhand but some brand new) since most of my maternity clothes I didn’t like and I just really wanted to feel good this pregnancy.

**[pbu21-0362]** — Reader context: Seventeen-year-old names $110 pill cost and asks for cheaper termination options.

> We live in a state where abortions are illegal, and my mom is heavily against abortion (believes it’s murder and yada yada). Neither my boyfriend or I make enough money to move out and support a baby right now. I’m guessing I’m about a month pregnant as I got my period in the beginning of July. I was considering ordering abortion pills from a neighboring state as I don’t need parental consent to buy them, and they’re about 110 dollars. However I’m unsure of how far along I am and feel insane guilt aborting the baby. Is there any advice anyone could offer me on how to feel better about my decision? Is there a cheaper way to terminate the pregnancy?

**[pbu21-0369]** — Reader context: Wants skin-product experiences and clarity about safety.

> Guys please tell me what u used to for the unprecedented skin problems, because im losing my mind and cant figure out whats safe and whats not.

**[pbu32-0008]** — Reader context: Nine weeks pregnant after missed miscarriage; soreness creates purchase hesitation.

> I very rarely wear bras if I’m honest but obviously as I’m getting more pregnant they need a bit more support but feel like it’s wild to buy anything maternity yet especially after last time so do people just size up?

**[pbu32-0031]** — Reader context: Toddler's crib escape prompts monitor replacement before second baby.

> I’m also not interested in spending hundreds of dollars on something that is going to monitor my baby’s vitals or make my family feel like we’re living in a surveillance state (mostly because I am perfectly capable of doing both of those things myself) - I simply want to improve our system and ensure its more reliable that the Vtech WiFi Monitor we were gifted before our first before the next little one arrives.

**[pbu32-0036]** — Reader context: Unconsulted trip conflicts with baby preparations and need to move.

> No baby preparations have begun yet but I’ve been making moves to work towards starting because we need to *literally move* out of our current living space*…* we have SO MANY other things that need to be purchased and done. He dropped thousands on a trip that’s going to last 2 days and decided to bring everyone whom he will have to pay everything for.

**[pbu32-0039]** — Reader context: Infant-care job requires sitting on floor; jeans squeeze at thirteen weeks.

> I don't want maternity jeans, I don't care for leggings, and I don't like sweatpants that are tight at the bottom. I'm also a tall girl at 5'10, and need pants that would fit my entire legs lol. They don't have to be ultra professional because our dress code is pretty chill. The cheaper the better 🙏🏼 lol.

**[pbu32-0049]** — Reader context: Sixteen weeks pregnant; organizing baby shower before March due date.

**[pbu32-0108]** — Reader context: New passage: doula's induction contact rules constrained requesting support.

> And I kept checking the packet to see if I could text her. It was terrible.

Full wording for these IDs is either elsewhere above or in the evidence appendix below. Primary grouping is for retrieval; it does not make the quotes independent.

### 23. Work, leave, care bills and housing constrain what parents can do, including whether and where to remain pregnant or give birth.

**Current findings location:** §8, §§9–10 (work/leave), 16.3, 16.5, 17.2, 17.5.

**Relationship:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “The money stress is mostly bills, daycare and leave”

**What should change:** Retain the practical limit on a guide and add the specific costs and access barriers in the Money inventory below. Reassurance has an unexpected $3,399 bill; medication may depend on insurance; therapy may be unaffordable; hospital transfer may be required to afford care; parents budget abortion pills, clothing, pet surgery and lost earnings alongside baby needs. Include working during possible miscarriage to preserve leave, disclosure-related job fears, involuntary job loss, and a planned benefit that supports continuing. Dollar amounts and stated policies remain each author's account.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0013]** — Reader context: Unplanned pregnancy, variable earnings and job losses undermine stable housing.

> I’m 37 weeks tomorrow and my partner and I have been struggling nearly this entire pregnancy establishing financial stability. this was unplanned and at the time of finding out about the pregnancy I lived in a chaotic home with both of my parents who drink and fight. the dogs are always barking their heads off and it is a mess literally. We moved in together in my first trimester and it was the most peaceful and self healing I have ever encountered. we’re both quiet and just simple partners who really enjoy our peace. I found my depression nearly vanished, my anxiety was fully manageable and I felt… alive more so than I ever had. fast forward to my third trimester now and our financial stability has become rocky again. he does sales so it’s always just a guessing game if he makes quota and can support us and the jobs I’ve been able to find hardly pay me enough for groceries. then I lose my job twice. now Im too far along to work.

**[pbu08-0014]** — Reader context: At term, caregiving for a grandmother overwhelms the author and disrupts bonding.

> we chose that option as the best for our daughter but I realized only a week in how bad of a decision that was because of how rapidly her memory declines and trying to manage my own mental health has now plummeted. Her dog pees all over, yaps constantly, and now it’s all on me. I’m crying all the time, finding myself having to go on car rides to disappear for three hours and just feel so anxious and stressed I cant even bond with my belly anymore. I’m due in two weeks and I am terrified of how much I’ve taken on. I’m more so just upset I didn’t work harder, save more money, or just overall push myself before pregnancy to become stable. If anyone has any mental health advice or  stories similar at all please share. we’re stuck in a 1 year lease now with her and financially couldnt do it ourselves so at the time this seemed like the best option.

**[pbu08-0033]** — Reader context: Miscarriage nightmares and anxiety during a wait for insurance coverage.

> hey everyone i’m 8 weeks and 2 days pregnant and keep having scary dreams of miscarrying. i’m still waiting for my insurance to kick in here in the next week so my anxiety has been on 10.

**[pbu08-0052]** — Reader context: Insurance calls compete with feeding, pumping, changing and eating.

> I thought I was doing fine with it until today when I found out my insurance is all messed up. So I am having to call insurance while changing diapers and pumping. All the while I have my husband reminding me to eat. Which yes I get I need to eat but where do people find the time?? She's 2 weeks old today and up every 2ish hours to eat, change diapers, burp ect. And then I pump. That basically takes up all my time and then people also tell me to sleep.

**[pbu08-0061]** — Reader context: Single parent wants another baby but housing and finances constrain the decision.

> My daughter’s father isn’t involved in her life due to a history of domestic violence and legal issues, so I’ve been raising her on my own. If I continue this pregnancy, I’d eventually need to move out of my current home, figure out finances/housing, and introduce my daughter to someone I’ve only known for a short time.
> 
> If housing and money weren’t concerns, I honestly think want to keep this baby. Any advice is appreciated

**[pbu08-0097]** — Reader context: Previously effective ADHD medication seems ineffective after pregnancy; cost limits switching.

> So, before getting pregnant I was on straterra for my adhd. It did wonders for me and I could actually function like a normal human being. Well then I got pregnant and obviously had to stop taking them. I started taking them again when I could no longer breast feed my daughter and they just haven’t been the same ever since and it’s been months already. 40mg was great for me, now I’m on 80mg and legit still have such bad adhd symptoms. Yes I have talked to my psychiatrist about switching meds but omg adhd meds are expensive if you don’t have insurance (I lost my insurance as I quit my job and I’ve been trying to get Medicaid ever since) so I just feel like I’m stuck at the moment. Moms with adhd, did you go through the same thing after pregnancy and birth? Do you just change completely after pregnancy I don’t get. Can my body not metabolize the same as it once did? I’m struggling so bad at the moment 😭

**[pbu08-0139]** — Reader context: Partner's traumatic recollection changes plans for another birth and prompts adoption or surrogacy discussion.

> I feel like I have gained an understanding for what happened now. We would love to have another baby,I want to experience pregnancy again ,give my baby another sibling and grow as a family. I feel I would be selfish of me to want to do that you know? My husband had made it clear yes to another baby but that was extremely traumatic for him. I understand like I said I don’t remember any of that but I respect that. We’d rather have me here than not. So we talked about adoption and or having a surrogate . Adoption seems easiest route financially and we have lots of love to give. Having a surrogate could be up to $200k based off others experiences who were able to do that route. Has anyone ever gone through this or something similar? What are your thoughts?

**[pbu08-0153]** — Reader context: Seeks affordable ways to support his wife's grief on Mother's Day.

> What are some ideas to help my wife grieve while still celebrating her Mother’s Day with our 2 kids? Money is a barrier for us but we can still afford to go out if that’s the best course of action. I don’t really care if we do celebrate, I just want what’s best for her at this moment. I am so lost at this point and could use the ideas. Thank you all

**[pbu08-0396]** — Reader context: Says testing was purchased mainly for reassurance after missed miscarriage.

> The test was basically unnecessary for us, as we aren't high risk for anything. My last pregnancy was a MMC so this was mainly done for reassurance.

**[pbu08-0416]** — Reader context: Fears medication killed the baby while awaiting a first scan and Medicaid.

> Basically I’m worried if I killed my baby or not. I’m only 7 weeks and haven’t had an ultrasound yet and I’m still waiting to get Medicaid. It’s my first time ever being pregnant and I keep hearing these horror stories of miscarriages.

**[pbu21-0051]** — Reader context: Supportive partner and parents cannot remove depression or the cost barrier to outsourcing.

> I’m (30F) 24w pregnant with my 2nd and struggling so much with my mental health this pregnancy. I’ve always struggled with anxiety/depression/adhd but it seems to be significantly worse this pregnancy. I’m working full time & parenting a high energy 3 year old this time around but I can’t seem to get past my intrusive thoughts and debilitating fatigue. I truly have no desire to do anything but sleep right now. I feel like I’m drowning in responsibilities and can’t keep up with life. My husband is extremely supportive and keeps telling me to give myself grace right now but I feel so much frustration because I truly can’t keep up with life right now. Laundry, house work, getting baby’s room ready are all hanging over my head and I can’t seem to muster up any motivation to do anything. I feel horrible because my husband also works a pretty demanding, full time job and has picked up a lot of my slack. Outsourcing isn’t super feasible right now (like laundry or cleaning) because we’re pinching Pennie’s in order to pay off some debt before baby is here. Our parents help out as much as they can too but I’m just feeling so depressed and no desire to do anything.

**[pbu21-0058]** — Reader context: Work stress blocks eating and sleep; sick leave feels like failure.

> This week has been the most stressful of my career and it's not even the end of Wednesday. A horrible project is falling apart, and despite my best efforts and warning everyone on time, inept management is putting all the blame on me. I am a 20w pregnant woman who hasn't been able to eat anything today until 3 PM, due to stress. I haven't been able to sleep. I am seriously considering taking a sick leave but I would feel like I failed and chickened out..

**[pbu21-0125]** — Reader context: Public announcement claims future leave changes; not a parent experience or verified entitlement.

> So we’re changing the law. From April, women and their partners will be entitled to two weeks off after any pregnancy loss. And we’re making wider changes, with new rights to time off when you lose someone you love.

**[pbu21-0131]** — Reader context: Fears recurrent depression with partner refusing a better job and shorter hours.

> My fiance only got a week off work after baby was born and even though he got a better job offer with better pay and way more reasonable hours ($5/hr more and steady 10hr days instead of 12hr days) he still refuses to leave his current job and signed a contract for another several months after I begged him not to. It’s like he wanted to have a baby just to be able to say he’s a dad and now she and I are both just accessories to him and the novelty of parenthood has worn off.

**[pbu21-0132]** — Reader context: Recalls paid leave treated as vacation and no care on returning home.

> The way he treated me postpartum was so awful that I cannot go one day without thinking about it and I have told him that. When I told him that, he got offended that I would say such a thing. He watched me have two natural births, he watched me in excruciating pain two separate times. And even after seeing me like that, he still wouldn't even change a diaper in the hospital. Hell? He wouldn't even go into Walmart to get me my mommy diapers!  He wouldn't do anything, literally not one single thing to help me when we got home also. He wouldn't keep wood in the fire, he wouldn't even bring wood in for me to add myself, he wouldn't hold the baby so I could shower. He got paid leave that he called a "free vacation."  He expected dinner the night we got home, didn't do a single chore to help out. He never went to a single pregnancy or baby appointment. Never made dinner, never went shopping, I can't think of one thing he did besides go to work sometimes and pay the bills, the easiest part if you ask me.

**[pbu21-0136]** — Reader context: Older child’s emerging needs and unaffordable daycare complicate current pregnancy.

> I’m also pregnant again and really having a hard time being excited about it. We can barely afford one kid in daycare, we definitely can’t afford two. I’m going to have to take a long leave from my dream job until one of them is old enough for preschool; or we move to Florida and move in with my family-in-law.

**[pbu21-0143]** — Reader context: Only-income partner cannot provide full-time care.

> Her father works. We live together. He's helpful when he's around but he's our only income, so he can't just commit to caring for the baby full time.

**[pbu21-0155]** — Reader context: Names pay, savings and graduate-school fears.

> First off, I’m worried about my finances. I work as a waiter that pays decently well(500-800 on average). It doesn’t come with any benefits but it has allowed me to have a decent saving (upwards of 30k). I just don’t know if it’ll be enough. I’m worried about whether or not I’ll be able to go to grad school. I’m worried that I won’t have any time for myself as selfish as it sounds. This all just came out of left field.

**[pbu21-0400]** — Reader context: Home-birth midwife cost and fear of being judged block planning.

> My birth plan is cause for concern also. There is pretty much no plan and I haven’t taken any courses or anything even though i’m this far along. The baby is perfectly healthy and I have another checkup tomorrow. My boyfriend wants me to have a natural home birth, and I also want that, it’s just that a midwife won’t be covered by my insurance, and it will cost about $4\~5k. Of course many of them offer payment plans, which helps a lot, but it’s stressful thinking about how I will need to pay for rent, food, gas, insurance, all the other baby necessities, etc. soon as well. While his mom will help a little, I’ll be paying for most of everything as I’m the one with a full time job(again, praying I don’t get fired). Back to the midwife problem, aside from the cost, i’m also very worried I won’t be able to find one I feel very comfortable with. I feel like i’ll feel judged by most of them as I have some slightly unconventional requests(nothing really weird but also not completely sorted out just yet) for my birth.

**[pbu21-0401]** — Reader context: Expects to miss FMLA eligibility and worries about time with newborn.

I also won’t qualify for FMLA until a few months after I give birth, and even then that only gets me a few unpaid weeks off. Lol, i’m very conflicted on how i’ll be treated at work once I disclose my pregnancy! I feel like they’ll either be very supporting or i’ll be fired, no in between🥹

> I feel like it's a lot of money to justify but of course it's worth it for peace of mind.

**[pbu32-0118]** — Reader context: Recently had second child; says fathers receive little mental-health attention.

**[pbu32-0135]** — Reader context: Unconfirmed pregnancy; lack of parental financial support shapes fear.

**[pbu32-0149]** — Reader context: Fourteen weeks; wants planned birth timing early to avoid losing prenatal leave.

> I would just like to know because I am pretty sure the 4 weeks paid stops the second your baby is born, so if I tell them my due date is any later than planned c-section is, I might loose some leave time.

**[pbu32-0234]** — Reader context: Nineteen-year-old fears asking wealthy grandparents for help and disappointing family.

> Getting an abortion is not an option for me but I’m scared that because I do not have a lot of money I won’t be able to give my child the best life possible.

**[pbu32-0337]** — Reader context: Author's stated benefits and childcare option underpin desire to continue pregnancy despite boyfriend's opposition.

> I work in a daycare, meaning I could bring the baby to work with me and pay discounted childcare fees. I’ll also qualify for full paid parental leave (around 28k over 26 weeks).

**[pbu32-0338]** — Reader context: Defers dog's imaging to avoid duplicate cost before surgery and newborn arrival; account is about household preparation costs.

> We didn't get the X-rays as planned since it would be about $1200 JUST for that but after the physical exam the vet is pretty positive its a CCL ligament tear (like an ACL tear in humans) and they dont do the surgery in house.

### 24. Personalisation must include identity, body, geography, family structure and previous care, rather than a mother-and-male-helper default.

**Current findings location:** §§1, 5, 9, 10, 15, 17.3.

**Relationship:** Extends and corrects corpus omissions.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “Personalisation by pregnancy number alone is a thin selector.”

**What should change:** Add actual teenage pregnancies, a pregnant trans teenager who wants to be recognized as dad, two-mother families, a Black parent weighing hospital diversity against an unsafe-feeling family home, and a newborn parent who did not gestate their first child. Include disability, autism, chronic pain, eating-disorder recovery, migration, rural distance and differing treatment systems. The old statement that no minors were quoted reflects selection, not irrelevant experience or established buyer status. Texas/Florida emergency-care concerns influence trying or travel; preserve them as perceived constraints, without turning them into verified legal claims.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0212]** — Reader context: Pregnant trans teenager fears family invalidation and being called mother rather than father.

> I think I actually want to keep the baby, but I’m terrified of being invalidated for being trans AND pregnant. I come from a really transphobic family and I already know they’d call me “mom” instead of dad, which honestly hurts just thinking about it.

**[pbu08-0217]** — Reader context: Teen feels a disappointment to parents despite their attempts to support the pregnancy.

> Hey guys. I just told both my parents I’m pregnant and I’m truly in shambles. They are trying to hard to be supportive but I can see their broken hearts and I feel like the biggest disappointment. I’m 18 for context and this obviously isint what they wanted for me but I couldn’t do an abortion. I genuinely feel like my heart is broken and our relationship will never be the same. Has anyone else been through this and came out on the flip side okay?

**[pbu08-0325]** — Reader context: With pre-existing nerve issues, feels failed by care that labels pain abnormal without answers.

> I have nerve issues. I have a mild case of cerebral palsy in my legs. Before I got pregnant, I was having more nerve issues and also nerve issues in my head and having really bad migraines. I’ve noticed since I found out it was pregnant the nerves in my legs, back, and head have increased. So I have a feeling it has something to do with that. I have been trying to advocate for myself and it just seems like healthcare is failing me. They’re definitely telling me it’s not normal, but I’m not getting many answers. I have also have had bad chills at night and a few fevers. The one night I cried myself to sleep because my entire body hurt like my back to my ankles.
> 
> Has anyone experienced this so early on? And when I tell you, this is not just pregnancy back pain. It is way more than that. I can barely walk or bend over. I would say the pain is about an eight or nine. I’ve been just so conflicted and in so much pain that I don’t even know what to do. Everything I read online puts me in the high risk category for me but baby should be okay. I just can’t do normal day tasks.

**[pbu08-0329]** — Reader context: Others attribute pregnancy exhaustion to weight despite reported good test results.

> I’m overweight (250lbs) so people are constantly just pointing the finger at my weight as to why I feel the way I do which I’m sure may have something to do with it but it isn’t the WHOLE reason. I eat very healthy and was in the middle of my weight loss journey when I got pregnant! AND my vitals and bloodwork always look amazing when I go in.

**[pbu08-0363]** — Reader context: Sensory difficulties and pregnancy distress include suicidal thoughts despite ongoing therapy.

> I am autistic and already have sensory issues and this shit is putting me over the edge. I fantasize about jumping off my balcony and weird fucked up shit like that. And before you ask. Yes I go to therapy because yes I struggle enough on my own without being pregnant .

**[pbu08-0410]** — Reader context: Long travel, packing and family conflict interrupt appointments during a prenatal-care transfer.

> While transferring prenatal care, I've been making the grueling 8hr trek back and forth, packing moving boxes during heatwaves to bring down there, taking in the mental load of planning out the move, etc. I've started slowing down...the traveling is too much on my body, I've stopped packing, started cancelling appointments, and stopped picking up the phone as much when my mom calls. I love my mom, she's like a best friend to me but this started negatively effecting my relationship with her. The stress of everything is so bad, I've had horrible chest pains and my obgyn refered me to cardiology for EKG.

**[pbu08-0411]** — Reader context: Wants a more diverse hospital and safer community but fears the family housing environment.

> I do not feel comfortable giving birth to my child in the town I live in...black women are 3-4x more likely die in child birth, my husband promised he'd advocate for me if we decide to birth in this town but I just am so scared. (There's a city an hour away if it came down to it, I would birth there cause there's more diversity..) We hate this town, it doesn't align with us, politically or morally and not somewhere we feel safe as a interracial couple with a baby..we just want out of here. 
> 
> The hospital where my mom lives is one of the best in California, and I feel reassured by their medical expertise and diversity..and I could be around famiy and support..But....the thought living with my mom and in that environment is not something I want to handle right now being pregnant. It's too much.

**[pbu21-0015]** — Reader context: Sixteen-year-old fears pregnancy denial by an ex who blocked contact.

> Okay so yesterday I found out I'm pregnant and the guy im pregnant with is my ex who cheated on me and I don't know what to do we broke up a month ago and im scared he'll start saying its not his but he's the only one I've ever been with and like im going to call him today off my bsfs phone cus he blocked me everywhere but what do I do if he says it's not his BTW im 16 he's 18

**[pbu21-0087]** — Reader context: A routine 35-week appointment changed date, team and hospital without warning.

> The long story is this: my partner (40F) and I (40F) live approximately 4-5 hours away from our family.  We both have full time jobs, friend groups, and have purchased a home where we currently live.  We went in for a routine ultrasound and NST at 35 weeks.  We anticipated that l would have a scheduled c section at 37 weeks due to a previous uterine surgery. However, big shocker, I was told I had a chorioamniotic separation during the visit and that I needed to have my son that day (or the next day), not with my original OB team and not at the hospital we planned to deliver due to how early it was and the potential need for the NICU (our original hospital had a lower level of care and no room).  C section is had, baby boy is born with some breathing trouble (all resolved), and a little trouble feeding.  I’m discharged after 4 days, baby discharged from NICU and sent home after 5 days.

**[pbu21-0088]** — Reader context: Newborn hospitalizations prompt reconsideration of job and distance from family.

> All of this is to say: we’ve had a rough fee days of motherhood. While my partner is very much with me in all respects, I’m realizing now that I need the additional support of family.  However, our families are largely unable to travel to us - my parents are still working, my extended family is elderly and/or disabled.  This has made me consider finding a new job and moving back home.

**[pbu21-0142]** — Reader context: Asks what support exists when disability prevents infant care.

> If I don't recover and am unfit to care for the baby due to limited mobility and disability, what happens? Does CPS intervene? Can I ask a hospital for resources? Is there some sort of govt assistance navigating this scenario? Would I be forced to give baby up for adoption? I very much want her, but it's getting to the point where it's impossible to meet her needs.

**[pbu21-0178]** — Reader context: Partner departure, opposed family and job loss frame pregnancy.

> My whole pregnancy has been a giant shitshow tbh, my partner of 3 years straight up leaving after I didn’t get an abortion, family who wouldn’t have even let me get an abortion, losing my job etc etc. but the most irritating thing is the racist remarks…..

**[pbu21-0179]** — Reader context: Reports racist assumptions about an absent father.

> From strangers all I hear is ‘I bet the dad is black’ Just bc he left. And it’s always these typical white guys who see themselves as better than others just bc of their skin colour that say this. It’s a disgusting stereotype first of all

**[pbu21-0180]** — Reader context: Family’s racial jokes drown out the author’s own answers.

> I’ve told them to stop, they don’t. Embarrassing me constantly. When we’re outside and see somebody we know, they will obviously ask me about the pregnancy, if I have a name yet, etc. But before I can say anything the ‘Yes he’s Chinese, his name is lingling’ starts and then everybody is laughing and talking over me while I try to explain the truth and just wanted to give an answer about MY son.

**[pbu21-0219]** — Reader context: Sixteen-year-old describes family and religious pressure to continue pregnancy.

> I don’t know what to do, in my state you need parental consent to get an abortion and my parents are Jehovahs Witnesses and our religion abortions are strictly forbidden. im panicking , I can’t raise a kid and my boyfriend can’t either. all of my family agrees with my dad I dont know what to do! i want to go to college and have a career I can’t raise a kid

**[pbu21-0401]** — Reader context: Expects to miss FMLA eligibility and worries about time with newborn.

> The only thing I think I forgot to mention until now is how i’m also worried that I won’t have much time to spend with my child, especially after giving birth because, like I said, I work overtime most weeks. I also won’t qualify for FMLA until a few months after I give birth, and even then that only gets me a few unpaid weeks off. Lol, i’m very conflicted on how i’ll be treated at work once I disclose my pregnancy! I feel like they’ll either be very supporting or i’ll be fired, no in between🥹

**[pbu21-0402]** — Reader context: Partner questions parenting capacity because of chronic illness.

> 10. My partner brought up how my chronic pain and fatigue from my chronic illnesses sometimes limits my productivity and physical ability, and that it makes him have a lack of confidence in my ability to take care of a baby. This ableist attitude expressed by him was crushingly disappointing and hurtful. It ripped the rug out from under me and made me question why he’s even with me if he feels that I am so deficient. Like does he see me as a viable partner to raise a family with or has he just been keeping me around bc he thinks I’m nice to look at? This ableism from him made me briefly feel like I should terminate everything— the pregnancy, the trip, and the relationship, and just be a recluse. But he apologized after seeing how he broke me, and has resumed being the nurturing supportive partner he normally is, so I forgave him and chose to reinstate my trust in him.

**[pbu32-0010]** — Reader context: Pistol course requires shooting at around 31 weeks; plans to ask doctor.

> Is this something that is okay or is the noise of 5 rounds going to be a problem for baby that I need to postpone or talk to the instructor about options?

**[pbu32-0021]** — Reader context: Rural counseling wait follows breakup during pregnancy.

> i have a therapy appointment set up, but they couldn’t get me in until months out. i’m from a rural county in a rural state and my primary doctor told me we are suffering from a counselor drought, that’s why my appointment was set up so far out.

**[pbu32-0028]** — Reader context: Currently pregnant after a stillbirth; pregnancy number differs from living-child count.

> When people ask how many kids I have I always want to say “6” but then that requires an explanation when they ask about genders and names and all that. I don’t want my son to be forgotten or overlooked but I also don’t always want to talk about the situation.

**[pbu32-0039]** — Reader context: Infant-care job requires sitting on floor; jeans squeeze at thirteen weeks.

> I don't want maternity jeans, I don't care for leggings, and I don't like sweatpants that are tight at the bottom. I'm also a tall girl at 5'10, and need pants that would fit my entire legs lol. They don't have to be ultra professional because our dress code is pretty chill. The cheaper the better 🙏🏼 lol.

**[pbu32-0044]** — Reader context: Reflects on postpartum eating disorder while planning feeding for second baby.

> I had a really difficult time mentally postpartum. I was unhappy with my body to begin with, and two weeks after giving birth, I started eating 1,200 calories a day because I desperately wanted to lose weight. Over the next year, I lost 115 lbs and became dangerously anorexic.

**[pbu32-0082]** — Reader context: Fourth pregnancy; new GD restrictions collide with already limited diet.

> It’s taking all of the fun out of food, I’m constantly worrying about baby when my readings come back above 7.8 as I’m newly diagnosed and trying to see what works for me and what doesn’t. I’m struggling with this as I have suspected autism and I have a very limited diet anyway.

**[pbu32-0087]** — Reader context: Badger Notes safeguarding flag follows disclosure of past abuse, not current relationship.

> Due to this I’m quite confused and a bit upset since I’m not under any threat and don’t want any referral to any teams. I’ve got my second midwife appointment this week and my partner also really wants to come but I don’t want him to because of this now.

**[pbu32-0090]** — Reader context: Seeks NIPT for fetal sex; this rationale was absent from existing quote.

> The chromosomal stuff doesn't bother me. If baby is going to have down's syndrome, me knowing earlier doesn't change anything. We'd still keep baby and love them the same.

**[pbu32-0337]** — Reader context: Author's stated benefits and childcare option underpin desire to continue pregnancy despite boyfriend's opposition.

> I work in a daycare, meaning I could bring the baby to work with me and pay discounted childcare fees. I’ll also qualify for full paid parental leave (around 28k over 26 weeks).

**[pbu32-0339]** — Reader context: Egg-freezing consultation prompts fear about future pregnancy and birth; risk and birth-route beliefs are author's, with no current pregnancy reported.

> It was just such a shock to find out I have both of these rare malformations. I've done some research and it sounds like I'll have to get surgery to have the septum removed before I can have sex (comfortably), and the didelphic uterus, while not affecting egg freezing, comes with higher risk of miscarriages and require a C-section.

### 25. Body changes, intimacy, touch, pets and daily routines can alter identity and coping during pregnancy and recovery.

**Current findings location:** §6 (identity/body), §2 (symptoms/recovery), §5 (relationships); some material needs new subsections.

**Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion (verbatim from findings):** “Identity and the body.”

**What should change:** Include sexual safety and bodily autonomy in consensual intimacy as well as violence; fear after tearing or prior loss; aversion to a loving partner's touch; body shame and eating-disorder history; pet disgust, animal illness and sensory routines. Keep the partner's self-described breeding kink without inferring coercion from it. Household and animal-care demands can affect both money and postpartum planning. Unusual hair changes, dreams, superstition and activities such as a pistol course are parts of what parents actually ask, not reasons to exclude their experience.

**Quotes behind the pattern:**

**[pbu08-0060]** — Reader context: Difficult pregnancy and family conflict leave regret about not cherishing a last pregnancy.

> I feel so sad, this is my last baby and I don’t think I cherished being pregnant enough. I’ll never feel these kicks again, or be his only home, the only one to hold him. I’m holding onto these last couple of days before my induction and trying not to beat myself up too much.

**[pbu08-0090]** — Reader context: Worries about bonding with a son and the influence of misogyny.

> I do have some fears about raising a son, I think men’s and women’s brains just work in very different ways and I worry that I won’t be able to connect as strongly I guess? And I’m worried about the state of society, about how widespread misogyny and violence is, and that even if I do everything right society and peers will be a negative influence.

**[pbu08-0091]** — Reader context: Social-media feed supplies pregnancy-ending content at odds with her own wishes.

> To make matters worse, my algorithm is full of “girl or abortion” type posts, which my personal morals don’t agree with. I am pro-choice and extremely pro-women but I wouldn’t make that choice for myself.   
> I guess what I’m looking for is supportive stories from other moms, ones that have experienced the same emotions, and/or ones that have boys and have amazing and sweet stories to share about them!

**[pbu08-0109]** — Reader context: Animal waste exposure prompts contamination fears despite cleaning precautions.

> In between this, because we have a dog with us (not mine, I can't stand them) that pooed, the person with the dog tried to clean up the remainder of the dog poo with a wet wipe and his bare hands 🤢 I did give him some antibac spray but I was already spiralling about toxoplasmosis or something and now I'm panicking completely myself and my son and this baby are now exposed to something and it's going to cause a miscarriage. 
> 
> I have followed all the common sense guidelines, washing hands/antibac wipes and I've actually chucked the clothes away because I read it's harmful to breathe in dry bird poo spores. I can't avoid unfortunately touching intimate areas of myself because I am on progesterone pessaries until 16 weeks so I've just done my nighttime one.

**[pbu08-0115]** — Reader context: Pregnancy brings unfamiliar disgust toward household dogs and fear it will persist.

> I'm heavily pregnant and throughout the entire pregnancy, my dogs literally make me feel sick. I didn't have morning sickness, so it isn't that, and I never felt this way before pregnancy, so just wanted some advice or someone to tell me they also felt this way so I know it's a phase and not permanent, which is worrying me. Most behaviours that are bothering me are hygiene related. Also, any advice on how to correct any of these behaviours may help. Here are the main things that bother me during my pregnancy:

**[pbu08-0116]** — Reader context: Fear of dogs hurting the baby changes behavior at home.

> 4) Jumping up. This isn't really an issue anymore, as I've been training them not to during the pregnancy and they rarely do it now, but I feel as though it is relevant to my issues. I got sooo angry when this happened. I never shouted or hurt the dogs, but I'd spend the day in another room away from them. I didn't want to be around them. I had an irrational fear during the time that my dogs were going to hurt my baby by jumping up.

**[pbu08-0126]** — Reader context: Prior losses led to abstinence throughout one pregnancy and fear of resuming intimacy.

> I’ve had multiple miscarriages in the past and one successful pregnancy where I didn’t have sex at all for the whole pregnancy as I was scared that there would be implications as a result of sex or an orgasm.
> 
> I am pregnant again and have just gone into my second trimester, and would really like to be intimate with my husband again but am finding it hard to look past my worries around sex and orgasms causing issues which could result in a loss of the baby.

**[pbu08-0142]** — Reader context: Medical clearance after tearing has not removed fear of sex, pain or infection.

> I’m 8 weeks postpartum and my husband has been so patiently waiting for us to have sex again. I’ve been wanting this level of intimacy back in our marriage but I’m so scared!
> 
> I had a 2nd degree tear and inner tearing as well, and was cleared at my 6 week appointment. I did start to bleed a little for a few days after my appointment, so I wanted to hold off a few more weeks. 
> 
> I’m terrified of the pain everyone talks about and I don’t want get an infection or something. My mind is going to worst case scenario. I haven’t had sex since I was 34 weeks pregnant, and it was painful then. 
> 
> Give it to me straight here.. what should I expect? I know everyone’s experience is different but I’m low key wondering if this is too soon?

**[pbu08-0383]** — Reader context: After chemical pregnancy, wonders whether a brief dizzy episode predicts conception.

> Anyway, I woke up at 6 am 7dpo and literally could not open my eyes without feeling like I had the spins. I ate plenty of food the day before and I was hydrated. My husband had to watch me shower (that’s all I wanted to feel better apparently) to make sure I didn’t pass out. I thought I might be coming down with the flu or something since that’s going around but I felt somewhat better midday, just sleepy then the next day I was totally fine albeit a little fatigued & today I feel great!

**[pbu08-0405]** — Reader context: Ambivalence about an unplanned first pregnancy clashes with partner's wish for several more children.

> I'm 15 weeks pregnant and I still don't know how I feel about being pregnant.It's like sometimes I'm excited but other times I'm like I would have been fine with my animals and husband.I didn't need to have a baby but I'm having one and it's only because it wasn't really planned I mean maybe some years down the line but rn doesn't seem like the best time.My husband wants like three more after this but like I would stop at one or just have one more it's like I'm sacrificing everything.

**[pbu21-0001]** — Reader context: Second pregnancy brings guilt about wanting a girl.

> I just feel really guilty about even feeling disappointed about not having a girl. I never thought I would be someone that would experience gender disappointment  but I kinda am. I want a healthy baby again and a smooth pregnancy but I guess I would be more excited if it was a girl.

**[pbu21-0009]** — Reader context: Keeping sex a surprise helped during a premature delivery.

> I'm 14 weeks with my second baby. I waited until birth to find out the gender of my first much to the annoyance of basically everyone around me. I loved everything about that experience except constantly having to justify it. It was especially nice because I had a very premature delivery and I got to focus on how excited I was to have a son and what that meant, and not on the trauma that was actively happening to us both.

**[pbu21-0010]** — Reader context: Agreed to a reveal and now carries other people’s expectations.

> This time I gave into pressure, thinking it would be helpful for shopping and that I'd feel fine. I made the plan to do a blood draw while visiting my US family and let them do a reveal so they could feel involved in this pregnancy even though we live abroad now. I had my SneakPeek clinical draw a few days ago and my mom got the results last night. She's doing a reveal for us this evening. I was kind of excited before but today I'm just sad. I would have been equally happy with either gender but my whole family, husband included, is talking about how much they hope for it to be a girl. So I'm carrying anxiety over everyone else's potential disappointment and mourning the surprise I really wanted.

**[pbu21-0050]** — Reader context: Balances body shame against fear of announcing before another loss.

> I am about 5 weeks pregnant right now. I am a very small female, so I am already showing. It is pretty hard to hide even with loose shirts. I have had two miscarriages prior to this. I can't decide whether to tell friends and family because I am kind of embarrassed just letting them think I've gotten "fat." But at the same time, it might be awkward if I miscarry again. I'm just very self-conscious of my round belly.

**[pbu21-0100]** — Reader context: Describes unexpected hair loss after miscarriage surgery.

> We recently had an oops that ended in miscarriage and I opted for a d&c surgery under general anesthesia and HOLY HELL the hair loss was real. It was a very early miscarriage but no doubt triggered a major hormonal shift, but then I heard about a link between broadly any surgery/anesthesia and hair loss.

**[pbu21-0126]** — Reader context: Fears vigorous escape from a locked bathroom harmed the baby.

> I’m 15 weeks pregnant and I accidentally got locked in the bathroom today. I completely panicked because I felt trapped and couldn’t get the door open.    
> I ended up kicking the door really hard and shaking/pulling on it pretty aggressively trying to get out. I was using a LOT of force because I was freaking out. 😭    
> I didn’t fall, and I didn’t hit my stomach. But now that I’ve calmed down, I’m worried that all the kicking, pulling and physical exertion could somehow have hurt the baby.    
> Has anyone experienced something similar? Is this kind of intense exertion something I should be worried about at 15 weeks?

**[pbu21-0176]** — Reader context: Family-safe marketing did not prevent fear about a household spray exposure.

> I’ve accidentally been using a bed and linen spray that has neem oil in it. I naively have been using it as it’s advertised as pet and family safe and didn’t do my diligence to check until now and I’m 11 weeks pregnant and freaking out!! I’ve change the sheets and aired out the bedroom but I’ve used this weekly since I’ve been pregnant and now I’m terrified I’ve harms my baby or will have a miscarriage. Not sure what I’m after, maybe some advice if anyone knows anything about neem oil or accidentally done something similar. I’m filled with dread, fear and guilt about it

**[pbu21-0181]** — Reader context: Body distress changes clothing, bathing and social plans.

> None of my clothes fit, I feel like a house, my belly is getting bigger everyday and I cry every single day and night because I cannot stand my body and I can’t stand getting dressed and I hate myself more than I ever thought possible. None of this feels magical or anything of the sort I just feel an insane pit of self loathing and whenever I tell people in my life that they say “oh well you’re pregnant!” Or that I’m “making the baby feel bad” with my negative self talk, Yipee thanks for the guilt trip on top of it aughhhh. I’ve even decided no baby shower because the idea of people seeing me like this quite literally gave me hives. I do not want anyone to see me like this.

**[pbu21-0398]** — Reader context: Links bleeding with stress after discovering partner’s betrayal.

> A few days after, at 13 weeks, I had a huge gush of blood, so much so that I thought I had lost the baby. I rushed to the ER where they saw the baby was okay, heartbeat was present and everything seemed “fine”. My OB a few weeks later then diagnosed an hematoma and I spotted from 13 weeks until around 17 weeks ish. I really think the stress of the situation might have caused the bleeding, it’s too close in between not to make a connection but anyhow, now hopefully the worst is behind and everything will go well from now on!

**[pbu21-0410]** — Reader context: Worries crying and stress changed the baby’s movements.

> I’m almost 37 weeks pregnant, and I’ve been crying nonstop for the past 2–3 hours because of something personal. I haven’t really been paying attention to my baby’s movements during that time, and now I’m worried because I can’t feel him moving.  
> I did feel him move earlier today, and I also have an anterior placenta, so I’m not sure if I just haven’t noticed his movements because I’ve been so upset.  
> Has anyone experienced something similar? Can crying or emotional stress affect the baby’s movements?

**[pbu32-0010]** — Reader context: Pistol course requires shooting at around 31 weeks; plans to ask doctor.

> Is this something that is okay or is the noise of 5 rounds going to be a problem for baby that I need to postpone or talk to the instructor about options?

**[pbu32-0027]** — Reader context: Author asks about alcohol exposure using a metabolism-based rationale.

> would having 3 glasses of wine over the course of 8-10 hours be harmful to the baby?? Considering it takes about an hour or two to process one standard drink.. so if you have 3 over a huge period of time does it hurt anything?

**[pbu32-0039]** — Reader context: Infant-care job requires sitting on floor; jeans squeeze at thirteen weeks.

> I don't want maternity jeans, I don't care for leggings, and I don't like sweatpants that are tight at the bottom. I'm also a tall girl at 5'10, and need pants that would fit my entire legs lol. They don't have to be ultra professional because our dress code is pretty chill. The cheaper the better 🙏🏼 lol.

**[pbu32-0041]** — Reader context: Vivid dream of feeding baby follows two previous miscarriages.

> I'm starting to kind of dread sleeping because the emotional aftermath upon waking up is crazy! None of my other dreams have been like THIS one!

**[pbu32-0042]** — Reader context: Unexpected hair lightening differs from family's pregnancy hair changes.

> My hair has grown over an inch in the last 3 months, I'm 14 weeks. We did our gender reveal /Pregnancy announcement photos, and that inch of new growth is clearly much lighter and I look like I'm balding.

**[pbu32-0101]** — Reader context: Seventeen months postpartum after difficult emergency section; two pelvic-floor courses.

> my issue is that sex has never felt the same and i've been feeling very down because i worry that it will never feel "normal" again.

**[pbu32-0102]** — Reader context: Postpartum weight-loss compliments conceal severe pregnancy sickness.

> And people would be like "wow you look good for just having a baby". Please I was so fucking sick and it actually traumatized me, stop 😭.

**[pbu32-0128]** — Reader context: Mother-in-law expects possible attendance at large shower three days after due date.

> Maybe the baby will come a bit early and you'll be home and feeling great already LOL. Plus we can make it a group effort and all go together.

**[pbu32-0338]** — Reader context: Defers dog's imaging to avoid duplicate cost before surgery and newborn arrival; account is about household preparation costs.

> We didn't get the X-rays as planned since it would be about $1200 JUST for that but after the physical exam the vet is pretty positive its a CCL ligament tear (like an ACL tear in humans) and they dont do the surgery in house.

**[pbu32-0339]** — Reader context: Egg-freezing consultation prompts fear about future pregnancy and birth; risk and birth-route beliefs are author's, with no current pregnancy reported.

> It was just such a shock to find out I have both of these rare malformations. I've done some research and it sounds like I'll have to get surgery to have the septum removed before I can have sex (comfortably), and the didelphic uterus, while not affecting egg freezing, comes with higher risk of miscarriages and require a C-section.
