Full wording for these IDs is either elsewhere above or in the evidence appendix below. Primary grouping is for retrieval; it does not make the quotes independent.

## Outliers and counter-examples

These qualify the interpretations above. They do not establish how frequently any outcome occurs.

### Counter-example 1: Good care and persistence can restore trust, including repeat tests and a corrected scan report.

**[pbu08-0012]** — Reader context: A second call reaches a different nurse and gets repeat testing arranged.

> i called again today and got a new nurse! she is putting in a new lab draw or whatever so i can do that today and start ursodiol in hopes that it will help! 
> 
> i'm sorry no one would listen to you! it's so frustrating. we know when something is wrong! i am confident that what i'm feeling is not right and i will be so shocked if my results come back negative the second time. i don't want to have it but at the same time, i want relief

**[pbu08-0103]** — Reader context: Praises staff who made time for questions and emotional support under pressure.

> The midwives, consultants, NICU nurses were the most amazing people I have ever met. To be so busy but to give you the time to listen, answer questions, emotionally support you at your most vulnerable times was immense.

**[pbu08-0288]** — Reader context: A covering clinician answers questions respectfully, but returning to the usual doctor is dreaded.

> This past week, that doctor was on vacation so I was scheduled to see a different doctor who didn’t make me feel like shit at all. She answered all of my questions, recommended my OB put me on a short acting insulin for meals, and made me feel like I was an actual human being. Not someone who was “k\*\*ling” their unborn baby. This week, I have to see the original doctor again, and I’m dreading it. I know I can ask for a different doctor, but I’m scared it’s just going to be the same thing all over again (most likely just my anxiety). The fill in I saw was just that. Just a fill in and not someone I can continuously see.

**[pbu21-0394]** — Reader context: Risk discussion feels frightening but also useful for recognizing emergencies.

> they scared me a bit when they started talking about ectopic and miscarriages, but i feel a bit more informed knowing what i need to keep a eye out for in case of emergency. they said baby is a healthy length - 1 inch - and healthy heart rate!!

**[pbu21-0396]** — Reader context: Follow-up scan finds a strong heartbeat after confusing report.

> Thank you so much❤️ I went today and my OB didn’t seem happy with what the radiologist put in the report, she did her own and they found bubs strong beat! 163❤️

**[pbu32-0003]** — Reader context: Follow-up after testing and reported preeclampsia diagnosis.

> I finally feel heard even though not the news I wanted but I don’t feel crazy and I feel justified for being kinda pushy with them instead of letting them bully me into thinking what I’m experiencing is normal.

**[pbu32-0147]** — Reader context: Follow-up: another nurse arranges repeat test before initial results return.

> i just called another nurse and she agreed to put in a lab draw so that i can get that done and then start the ursodiol this weekend and see if that helps.

### Counter-example 2: A section or surgery can bring relief, manageable recovery and gratitude rather than define a traumatic outcome.

**[pbu08-0102]** — Reader context: Emergency section becomes relief after prolonged painful uncertainty.

> By the final time it felt like I was in labour I was still only 4cm dilated (had been for a week or so at that point) and had a big bleed - it settled again and I knew it was important to keep baby in as long as possible to protect him but the pain was constant at this point. Finally they agreed a Cat 1 c section which at that point was a huge relief after being in pain and so scared for weeks.

**[pbu08-0121]** — Reader context: Despite missing the birth under general anesthesia, reunion feels worthwhile and pain manageable.

> unfortunately i had to be put under general anaesthetic so missed the birth and my partner couldn't be in the theatre but once we were all reunited and i got to meet my baby the whole ordeal was worth it for sure, am in a bit of pain due to the c section but its managable and am looking forward to going home as we are still in hospital at the moment

**[pbu08-0145]** — Reader context: Reports major relief after returning to emergency care.

> Immediately after that blood patch I had the most relief I’d ever felt. Forget giving birth, CSF leaks are no joke.

**[pbu21-0172]** — Reader context: Reports relief and restored eating after surgery during pregnancy.

> Now, 8 days later and 16 weeks pregnant today, I can honestly say it was worth it. Recovery wasn’t easy, especially with limited pain relief options during pregnancy, but I am finally free from the constant nausea, pain, and heartburn. I can eat again. I can enjoy my cravings. I feel like myself.

**[pbu21-0173]** — Reader context: After surgery, bought a support belt and found short walks helpful.

> I was just on paracetamol pain relief wise, position Id say would be best elevated on your back, foods I kept bland and small portions but often. Movement is the best healer and having a walk, even of it’s around the house (which was my case) helped me loads. I had a horrible pain around belly button area, I bought a belt that supports lower belly only which helped me to stay on my feet longer :)

**[pbu32-0236]** — Reader context: Gallbladder removal at twenty-four weeks relieved pain despite difficult recovery and temporary emotional distress.

> Surgery recovery is never easy and especially pregnant but it was one hundred percent worth it with having no pain now!! :) I hope this helps.

### Counter-example 3: Prenatal rejection or panic can change into attachment, but the change is not universal or guaranteed.

**[pbu21-0334]** — Reader context: Severe symptoms produced resentment and thoughts of not caring about miscarriage.

> Moving on, as I mentioned, reality set in and I wasn't sure if this was truly what I wanted. I cried and told him I was considering an abortion (looking back I'm glad I didn't do it), and I was depressed for awhile. 6 weeks and ALL of the symptoms hit me at once and I was done for. I was throwing up, nauseous all the time and super cranky. That also didn't help. I began to hate and resent our baby, and I even got so depressed to a point that I told my boyfriend I wouldn't even care if I miscarried. I wouldn't even refer to it as a baby, I just kept calling it "fetus" and "parasite."

**[pbu21-0335]** — Reader context: Seeing baby move at ultrasound shifts feelings toward love.

> Once 8 weeks hit and I got to do my ultrasound, everything changed. There my baby was, on the screen, wiggling it's bottom. It looked like a little bean, but it was oh so cute. After that, I got this overwhelming sense of love for my baby. Ever since, I've loved being able to nurture and cherish this little thing. I've never wanted to meet my baby so bad.

**[pbu21-0260]** — Reader context: Pregnancy intensifies affection and gratitude for partner.

> Everything he does seems endearing. I keep thinking about how lucky I am to have him, how excited I am to raise a child with him, and how grateful I am that he’s my partner. It’s almost overwhelming.

**[pbu08-0015]** — Reader context: Recalls immediate regret, anxiety and delayed connection after the first birth.

> I was so excited to be a mom even though I had no experience with babies. Unfortunately when I finally had him, I was instantly regretting it. I was so anxious when he would cry, and not sleeping was really getting to me. I felt no connection for a long time. I just didn’t feel confident when he was a new born and was scared.   
> That did go away and I love my son more than anything; he’s great.

**[pbu32-0107]** — Reader context: Follow-up accepts clinician's PPD/PPA assessment and disability extension.

> Now a few days out and talking more with my husband I’m really glad she did say something and I’m getting some more support.

### Counter-example 4: A strong partner or wider support system can be present while distress remains severe.

**[pbu08-0075]** — Reader context: Therapy and regular family help did not prevent repeated severe postpartum distress.

> I have two kids and my mental health was terrible after both (not that it’s amazing generally either, but on another level). With my second I was prepared, was already seeing a therapist who knew what was coming, slept over at in laws house almost every week, etc yet I almost feel traumatized now by how horrible I felt. I saw a newborn baby the other week and immediately my stomach turned and I felt the classical FIGHT OR FLIGHT turn on inside.

**[pbu08-0136]** — Reader context: Links distress to pregnancy losses and postpartum recurrence; seeks relief after multiple medications and talk therapy.

> Fortunately never had anxiety/depression until I had back to back pregnancy losses in late 2021. After that my brain went haywire : Suicidal pure OCD, intrusive thoughts, fear of losing control, etc. I was able to somewhat "manage" it with time, but I am now 6 months post partum with my 2nd and these thoughts are out of control and somewhat turning into "wow I do not know if I will make it out of this". I am scared, horrified for my life. I have failed all SSRIS thus far, lamictal, clomipramine, caplyta, abilify. I am so lucky I have an incredible support system who are helping me, but I need to help myself and these thoughts need to ease. I need relief desperately. I am looking in to two extreme options: Ketamine or Zurzuvae. \*I have also tried talk therapy with no relief\*. I am also more than willing to do any therapy to help myself, but I desperately need even 20 percent relief in the interim in order to do so. Anyone have relief with either?

**[pbu08-0154]** — Reader context: Practical support and preparation coexist with deep paternal regret and limited emotional help.

> We are six months along now, I'm still really struggling. Most days I feel pretty alright. I try to think of the positives, but some days I just feel like I've completely fucked up my life because I couldn't just man up and break up. So far, the pregnancy has been wretched, she has been sick the entire time, probably with hyperemesis. I have been very supportive of course, I have picked up all the house work, I've been going to all appointments, engaged in the future planning and trying to fix up the house to be ready for when the baby arrives. I haven't been wallowing in self pity, but I still struggle deeply internally. I have a psychologist, but it doesn't help much, and most of my male friends are classic emotionally insensitive guys or just a bit unhelpful, so it's hard to talk to anyone. I am also just embarrassed by all this. everyone is so happy when we tell them, and I'm here thinking "haha yeah how lovely I've destroyed my life".

**[pbu08-0207]** — Reader context: Nursery, letters, daily reading and excellent support do not prevent fear of failing or not connecting.

> I have done everything “right” so far, I have made her a beautiful nursery, I’ve written her letters and read her books and spoken to her every day. But suddenly I am so nervous, I’m nervous I won’t connect with my baby when I meet her, I’m nervous I won’t be good at this at all, nervous for all the ways my life will never be the same, nervous for all the mistakes I’ll inevitably make. I have dreamt of this baby forever, I have an incredible support system and the greatest possible husband but some how I suddenly feel like a failure and I haven’t even done it yet. Help?

**[pbu21-0051]** — Reader context: Supportive partner and parents cannot remove depression or the cost barrier to outsourcing.

> I’m (30F) 24w pregnant with my 2nd and struggling so much with my mental health this pregnancy. I’ve always struggled with anxiety/depression/adhd but it seems to be significantly worse this pregnancy. I’m working full time & parenting a high energy 3 year old this time around but I can’t seem to get past my intrusive thoughts and debilitating fatigue. I truly have no desire to do anything but sleep right now. I feel like I’m drowning in responsibilities and can’t keep up with life. My husband is extremely supportive and keeps telling me to give myself grace right now but I feel so much frustration because I truly can’t keep up with life right now. Laundry, house work, getting baby’s room ready are all hanging over my head and I can’t seem to muster up any motivation to do anything. I feel horrible because my husband also works a pretty demanding, full time job and has picked up a lot of my slack. Outsourcing isn’t super feasible right now (like laundry or cleaning) because we’re pinching Pennie’s in order to pay off some debt before baby is here. Our parents help out as much as they can too but I’m just feeling so depressed and no desire to do anything.

**[pbu21-0353]** — Reader context: Supportive marriage does not resolve uncertainty over wanting children.

> I’m happily married, and my partner is incredibly supportive. He is willing to work through all of this with me and has been there for me emotionally. So this isn’t about not having a supportive partner or having doubts about our relationship. If anything, having his support makes me realize how much of this is something I need to work through within myself.

**[pbu32-0182]** — Reader context: Supportive partner does not remove distress or perceived bodily inequity.

> He’s doing everything he can to share the mental load, has been so helpful in doing things around the house, but it’s not the same and I resent that he can’t understand where I’m at.

**[pbu32-0232]** — Reader context: Unexpected pregnancy after ten years of infertility and son born via surrogate.

> I really struggled with the newborn stage and first three months of my son’s life and I wasn’t even postpartum! I have a really good support system and partner but it was still so hard for me and I cried a lot.

### Counter-example 5: Abortion can be described without regret, while other records describe regret or trauma; no single trajectory stands for the others.

**[pbu32-0241]** — Reader context: Does not regret prior abortion but fears missing future chance of motherhood at thirty-seven.

> Has anyone else found the right partner later in life and gone on to have a healthy pregnancy in their late 30s or even 40s? Or has anyone else struggled with these feelings after a medical abortion?

**[pbu32-0332]** — Reader context: Prior abortion without regret coexists with current desire for future motherhood.

> It was the right decision for where we were in life, and I don't regret making the best choice we could at the time.

**[pbu21-0352]** — Reader context: Wishes for more time and mental health support before abortion.

> I know logically that having another pregnancy may be possible, but emotionally I’m struggling with the fact that I *was* pregnant, and I made the decision to end it. There are moments where I desperately wish I could go back and give myself more time, get better mental health support, and figure out what I actually wanted before making such a permanent decision.

**[pbu21-0353]** — Reader context: Supportive marriage does not resolve uncertainty over wanting children.

> I’m happily married, and my partner is incredibly supportive. He is willing to work through all of this with me and has been there for me emotionally. So this isn’t about not having a supportive partner or having doubts about our relationship. If anything, having his support makes me realize how much of this is something I need to work through within myself.

**[pbu21-0407]** — Reader context: Repeated pregnancy follows traumatic abortion with competing fears about both choices.

> My first abortion was probably the most traumatizing experience of my entire life. I don’t think I’ve even begun to properly process it. I’ve been drinking every single day since, and mentally, I’ve been having an extremely difficult time.  
> Part of me feels like having another abortion would completely destroy me emotionally, but another part of me knows that having a baby with this man would mean being permanently tied to someone who has repeatedly abused, cheated on, and disrespected me.  
> I’m also terrified of potentially raising a child alone, especially after seeing how little effort he puts into being a father to the child he already has.  
> I feel completely stuck between two decisions that both seem impossible to live with.

**[pbu08-0129]** — Reader context: Difficult prior pregnancies and an uncertain future shape a grieving termination decision.

> I just ordered the pills off of aid access and i feel so heavy and devastated. this is not what i wanted. i know it's the right choice though. i have 2 under two and this was a major slip up on my husband. pregnancy was so awful for me both times i'd never be able to go through it for a third time. we are getting out of military next year so who knows what the future holds especially with two kids. i feel so selfish doing this and this awful heavy feeling in my chest. my poor innocent baby. it hits so much harder since i have two.  before i had kids i probably wouldn't have been this worked up about it, but growing life two times and going through pregnancy- it's changed me. this is my first and last abortion and i am just so devastated. what kind of personality would my baby have? would they look like me? would they look just like their brother and sister? i can't believe I'm taking this life away but i know deep down it's the best decision. my punishment will be having this live with this choice for the rest of my life. sorry i just needed to get this off my chest.

### Counter-example 6: Some explicitly want company without hope, while others want optimism, blunt facts or practical action.

**[pbu08-0301]** — Reader context: Wants someone to talk with rather than optimistic reassurance about a likely loss.

> Not really looking for hope because I know what this means, but anyone with similar stories just to talk through it with someone.

**[pbu08-0159]** — Reader context: Wants plain facts rather than softened or spiritual reassurance.

> I do remember her saying the nurse was very forward and direct, which I appreciated at the time. In times like this, nothing will soften the blow. If anything, those beating around the bush or softening their language would really grind my gears. I just want facts. No bullshit. No god shit. No ‘she will be with you forever in your hearts’ shit. Just facts.

**[pbu21-0030]** — Reader context: Wants hopeful stories while preparing for a D&C.

> When I look on other subs I see a lot of sad stories and they help me not feeling alone, but right now I feel like I need an injection of optimism so that I can look ahead with hope. Can you tell me about your success story after loss?

**[pbu21-0065]** — Reader context: Explicitly asks for practical actions rather than positive thinking.

> Is there any advice how to get through this? Will this worry and anxiety ever go away? I don't need "think positive" advice, I need practical "do this" advice... did anyone push for early testing? Push for weekly check ups this early on?

**[pbu32-0105]** — Reader context: Physically symptom-free pregnancy includes insomnia and new anxiety.

> Does anyone have some positive stories to share about struggling with mental health during pregnancy, but feeling better after the baby was here? Of course if your story doesn't fit that narrative it's ok to share, but mainly looking for stories of hope.

**[pbu32-0328]** — Reader context: Membranes ruptured at twenty-two weeks four days; hoping to remain pregnant.

> Please share your miracle stories with me, I really need it. All I’m being told right now is she may not survive or if she does she will probably have some sort of disability. Please help me.

### Counter-example 7: Early announcements can secure support or make the parent feel more alone.

**[pbu21-0208]** — Reader context: Early disclosure is valued as access to support if loss occurs.

> Furthermore, the risk of a miscarriage in an otherwise healthy body is very low…people make it sound like it’s more likely than not to occur, and it’s absolutely not true. And even IF that happened, what’s the point of robbing yourself of any sort of emotional support in your grieving? It’s in no way something I would feel embarrassed to tell anyone. I would want people to be there for me.

**[pbu21-0288]** — Reader context: Friends and family guard their hopes, leaving author feeling more alone.

> So basically my issue is, I told the same people this time and the reaction this time is…not what I expected. One of my friends said she wasn’t getting her hopes up. The other one said she didn’t want to get too happy…my brother seemed excited but I could tell there was some hesitation. My mom seemed excited at first but she changes the subject anytime I mention it now. Before it was “when is the due date?!” And “I can’t wait for the baby shower!” But now it’s crickets. I don’t even want to talk to anyone now :(   
> I actually felt alone after my miscarriage but now I feel really alone and I actually regret telling anyone this early for the second time. I just thought it would be good news to tell them that everything feels different this pregnancy. My intuition isn’t blaring like the last time, I know so many things can go wrong still but I actually feel better this time. Has anyone else dealt with this? What do I do? I just want to crawl in a hole the next 8 months and come out with a healthy baby.

**[pbu32-0150]** — Reader context: Told family early to get support during sickness; responses instead increase anxiety.

> All they keep saying is “you told us too early” and “i’m not gonna be excited until the second trimester or later.” It makes me feel as if i did something wrong.

**[pbu32-0189]** — Reader context: Partner's travel creates need to disclose early despite ectopic and chemical pregnancy history.

> I’m only 7 and a half weeks and I want to tell my parents I’m pregnant so I’m not alone while he is away.

**[pbu32-0206]** — Reader context: Challenges secrecy rule as withholding support during sickness or loss.

> And also, if I were to get pregnant, and have a miscarriage, I would want a support system to lean on when something that life changing and sad happens, so that would make me more inclined to tell people pretty early on

### Counter-example 8: Testing and tracking are not uniformly rejected: some seek an early warning, fetal sex regardless of diagnosis, or a reliable monitor without surveillance.

**[pbu32-0031]** — Reader context: Toddler's crib escape prompts monitor replacement before second baby.

> I’m also not interested in spending hundreds of dollars on something that is going to monitor my baby’s vitals or make my family feel like we’re living in a surveillance state (mostly because I am perfectly capable of doing both of those things myself) - I simply want to improve our system and ensure its more reliable that the Vtech WiFi Monitor we were gifted before our first before the next little one arrives.

**[pbu32-0090]** — Reader context: Seeks NIPT for fetal sex; this rationale was absent from existing quote.

> The chromosomal stuff doesn't bother me. If baby is going to have down's syndrome, me knowing earlier doesn't change anything. We'd still keep baby and love them the same.

**[pbu32-0190]** — Reader context: Pushes for more HCG tests to gain advance warning, not simply repeated reassurance.

> Part of me wants the levels bc my last miscarriage I was having my levels checked just randomly and that’s how we found out that my pregnancy was not viable. I appreciated knowing way before I started bleeding and it wasn’t just unexpected.

**[pbu21-0007]** — Reader context: Disagrees with wife about early sex testing and how loss would feel.

> She's also really anxious about knowing the gender and wants to pay for an at home sneak peek gender test because she's scared that, if we lose this pregnancy, she at least wants to know what gender they would've been (I tell her that this pregnancy will be fine and there's nothing to worry about, and even if something did happen, I think knowing the gender would only make it harder, mainly because my wife wants a girl really badly, and if we were to find out it's a girl and miscarry, that would be way harder for my wife than if we didn't know at all. Another part of it is that I've heard these sneak peeks can be inaccurate and easily contaminated especially if there are males in the household; overall it seems better to way for NIPT from what I've seen considering how much my wife wants a girl; on top of this I don't want to pay for extra testing that may be inaccurate because we're trying to save money which has always been my strength but not my wife's).

### Counter-example 9: Valued contact and keepsakes can coexist with death; one partner can value a memory box that the other cannot open.

**[pbu08-0158]** — Reader context: Values compassionate, direct explanations and control over timing.

> To their credit, the nurses were very compassionate and straightforward with their outlining of the process. They maintained that we are in charge of the time frames and we can go as fast or as slow as we want.

**[pbu08-0159]** — Reader context: Wants plain facts rather than softened or spiritual reassurance.

> I do remember her saying the nurse was very forward and direct, which I appreciated at the time. In times like this, nothing will soften the blow. If anything, those beating around the bush or softening their language would really grind my gears. I just want facts. No bullshit. No god shit. No ‘she will be with you forever in your hearts’ shit. Just facts.

**[pbu08-0160]** — Reader context: Memory box comforts his wife while he cannot face looking inside it.

> Don’t get me wrong, I could see how this could be beneficial to others and how it can make someone feel. My Wifey liked it. That is what mattered to me more than my own disdain and we agreed to this box. To this day, I have not looked inside the box. Maybe 1 day. For now, I can’t. I won’t. I can’t fathom seeing a ‘certificate of life’ with her name on it. She was never alive. She never got to meet her Daddy.

**[pbu08-0161]** — Reader context: Many bought going-home outfits become the choice of a stillborn baby's only outfit.

> The nurses ask what clothes to put her in. Earlier in the week of the due date, I was going to the likes of Next and Zara to find the perfect ‘going home’ outfit. I couldn’t decide so bought so many different outfits. My Wifey asks me which one they should put her in and I choose the outfit. Tears stream down my face as I deliberate over which one. I choose such a cute floral one. 100% pure cotton. The only outfit she will ever wear in her life.

**[pbu21-0241]** — Reader context: Values natural birth, brief family contact and keepsakes despite loss.

> I'm trying to concentrate on the positives. I gave birth to him naturally like I wanted to. We got time with him before he passed. He got to hear his family love him, he got to feel our kisses. And he got to reach out and touch us. It was beautiful. Truly. We got him cremated and the hospital/funeral home were so generous with things for him and us! We got so many beautiful keepsakes!

**[pbu21-0351]** — Reader context: Holding the fetus after loss provides recognition and a chance to say goodbye.

> Something inside me told me to reach in and hold it, so I scooped this clot out of the blood-stained toilet and just held it in my hand for a little bit. I looked closer, and it seemed like there was something inside of it. That inside voice told me to open it up, so I cut it open, and clear as day, my tiny, blueberry baby was in the palm of my hand. Maybe this is an insane thing to say, but there was such an intense feeling of gratitude that I felt in that moment. To know that what I was experiencing was all real, and that I was truly growing a tiny life inside of me. It’s helped me to feel like I can actually say goodbye.

### Counter-example 10: The pregnant author who reports hitting her partner is a counterexample to an account that only records aggression by the other partner.

**[pbu08-0312]** — Reader context: Author reports hitting her partner during an argument and feeling ashamed.

> our fights have gotten worse since i’ve been pregnant. i’ve been overwhelmed and emotional, and one time i hit him during an argument. i’m ashamed of that, and i know it was wrong. i take responsibility for it.

### Counter-example 11: The author explicitly says the experience is not depression; keep that distinction instead of diagnosing from the topic tag.

**[pbu32-0307]** — Reader context: Follow-up explicitly rejects depression interpretation; preserves author's distinction.

> But what I am feeling isn't depression. I am a psychologist and I do not meet the criteria for being depressed.

### Counter-example 12: Reassuring a parent does not always mean giving less medical information: one says emergency-warning explanations helped them feel informed.

**[pbu21-0394]** — Reader context: Risk discussion feels frightening but also useful for recognizing emergencies.

> they scared me a bit when they started talking about ectopic and miscarriages, but i feel a bit more informed knowing what i need to keep a eye out for in case of emergency. they said baby is a healthy length - 1 inch - and healthy heart rate!!

## What fits nowhere

The current findings have related broad headings, but no developed treatment of these situations. Each needs a named subsection or a change to the reader model. The unusual and professional voices are retained with their role visible.

### Missing treatment 1: Stillbirth and neonatal-bereavement logistics need a developed subsection: labor after death, pain relief, time with the baby, clothing, autopsy, registration, organ donation, funeral and the empty journey home. Related material is currently scattered across birth/recovery and the selection-gap notes.

**[pbu08-0157]** — Reader context: Learns that confirmed fetal death is followed by labor and birth.

> After the phone calls, we get briefly explained the process from the nurses and I get the shock of my life. They mention key words and phrases like labour and ‘c section is more dangerous’. It dawns on me that my Wifey was going to have to give birth to our dead baby.

**[pbu08-0159]** — Reader context: Wants plain facts rather than softened or spiritual reassurance.

> I do remember her saying the nurse was very forward and direct, which I appreciated at the time. In times like this, nothing will soften the blow. If anything, those beating around the bush or softening their language would really grind my gears. I just want facts. No bullshit. No god shit. No ‘she will be with you forever in your hearts’ shit. Just facts.

**[pbu08-0160]** — Reader context: Memory box comforts his wife while he cannot face looking inside it.

> Don’t get me wrong, I could see how this could be beneficial to others and how it can make someone feel. My Wifey liked it. That is what mattered to me more than my own disdain and we agreed to this box. To this day, I have not looked inside the box. Maybe 1 day. For now, I can’t. I won’t. I can’t fathom seeing a ‘certificate of life’ with her name on it. She was never alive. She never got to meet her Daddy.

**[pbu08-0161]** — Reader context: Many bought going-home outfits become the choice of a stillborn baby's only outfit.

> The nurses ask what clothes to put her in. Earlier in the week of the due date, I was going to the likes of Next and Zara to find the perfect ‘going home’ outfit. I couldn’t decide so bought so many different outfits. My Wifey asks me which one they should put her in and I choose the outfit. Tears stream down my face as I deliberate over which one. I choose such a cute floral one. 100% pure cotton. The only outfit she will ever wear in her life.

**[pbu08-0162]** — Reader context: After birth, must make immediate decisions about death registration, donation and funeral.

> We also have to discuss registering her death, organ donations and the funeral, amongst other heavy shit. Cremation or burial. No parent should ever have to even entertain this kind of conversation. Even in a ‘hypothetical’ sense. We must entertain it because we have to action it.

**[pbu08-0163]** — Reader context: Contrasts going home after the first birth with leaving after stillbirth.

> I left with my baby boy in his car seat when he was born. I left this time as I came. Just with my Wifey. Nobody spoke a word on the way back to the house.

**[pbu21-0063]** — Reader context: Birth choices, autopsy and death paperwork arrive while grieving and medicated.

> Throughout the evening, many doctors have come visited us explaining different options of birthing and what to do after he’s born. We decided that if they have no idea how he died then we would send him to autopsy. Then the options of a funeral and whether we want him cremated or not. As they were talking options (while high from pain meds) they silently handed my partner a form. After discussing some things and they left, he started sobbing. The paper they gave him was his death certificate. Now, I understand that they need this information, but we could not fathom how cruel and twisted this became in an instant.

**[pbu21-0064]** — Reader context: Reports ineffective epidural top-ups and dismissed pain during stillbirth delivery.

> Unfortunately it was a terrible induction. It took two days. I had two top offs but by the time it was getting close I had feeling back in my lower body. By that point I had a different anesthesiologist and very reluctantly gave me a tiny bit more. It did nothing and the pain tenfolded. She claimed that I needed to feel pressure and a little bit of pain but it did nothing. I had labor pains the rest of the time. I pushed for 3 1/2 hrs with basically no epidural. considering his size, it makes sense because his shoulders were stuck for the longest time. In the end though it was worth it seeing him. He came into this world October 18 2026. Trying not to bring fear into you guys, I’m pretty sure I was a special case cause most epidurals I’ve heard have been fine. The first one was way more kind. The second one had shitty bedside manners and gaslit me saying that I should feel pain and disregarded my request to have more. I don’t wish that pain on anyone.

**[pbu21-0137]** — Reader context: Stillbirth delivery included long induction, surgery, infection and hemorrhage.

> I had a C-section almost 5 weeks ago after induction and a very long labor. We found out our son had passed which is what necessitated the induction in the first place, and then he was a pretty large baby (I was full term) and I labored for 36 hours before pushing for 2 and developing an infection. The C-section went mostly ok, I did hemorrhage a bit and had a cervical extension but it could have been worse.

**[pbu21-0138]** — Reader context: Postpartum bleeding after activity must wait for a weekday reply.

> I had the normal actual bleeding for the first week, then lochia serosa for probably 10 days and after that it seemed to have progressed to lochia alba but it's really mucusy. A couple of times in the past week and a half, there's been bloody streaks in it after a little bit of activity (vacuuming last week, running a bunch of errands this week). I assume this is normal but this is my first time being postpartum. I did message my doctor but it's the weekend so I won't hear back for a couple of days. Nothing is serious enough for me to consider going into the hospital either, I don't feel unwell or anything like that, just like I overdid it a little.

**[pbu21-0241]** — Reader context: Values natural birth, brief family contact and keepsakes despite loss.

> I'm trying to concentrate on the positives. I gave birth to him naturally like I wanted to. We got time with him before he passed. He got to hear his family love him, he got to feel our kisses. And he got to reach out and touch us. It was beautiful. Truly. We got him cremated and the hospital/funeral home were so generous with things for him and us! We got so many beautiful keepsakes!

**[pbu21-0325]** — Reader context: First encounter with bereavement is stillbirth; explanation may take months.

> Trigger warning: stillbirth.   
> My daughter was stillborn on the 1st of August 2026. I was 40 weeks and 3 days, she was my first baby. I got pregnant at 19, healthy pregnancy, although I developed anemia over the last few weeks . Post mortem will take months and I’m completely at a loss. I’ve never felt anything like this, I feel like I’m trapped in a nightmare. I don’t know how I can ever recover from this, and all I can think is if I went to the hospital for reduced movements maybe they could have saved her. I can’t comprehend that my baby is dead. I am so lost, I’ve never even been to a funeral before or dealt with any grief. My grandparents are still alive yet I had to burry my daughter on Wednesday. I’ve never felt so hopeless, I can’t fathom what the next step is…

### Missing treatment 2: Decision support before a commitment to continuing the pregnancy needs its own place, including competing fears, abortion or adoption, wanted motherhood at a different time, and divergent reactions afterward.

**[pbu08-0004]** — Reader context: Late discovery and travel requirements shape the decision to continue.

> I’m 31 weeks pregnant. I’m very small and I found out extremely late. I found out so late that my first instinct was an abortion. My husband and I have been married for a year and with everything going on in the world I didn’t think it was the best time. I had an abortion early on when we first wed. I found out and realized that I was so far along that I couldn’t get it done in my state and would have to travel so we decided to keep it. (He wanted to keep this and the last one).

**[pbu08-0078]** — Reader context: Partner shifts from wanting a child to preferring termination over career timing.

> I’m only about 5 weeks, and even though logically I know how this happened 😅 it still feels shocking. What makes it harder is that now my husband seems unsure. He says he supports whatever I choose, but leans more toward abortion because of the timing with us starting our careers.

**[pbu08-0079]** — Reader context: Makes and cancels appointments while feeling frozen by either permanent choice.

> I keep making appointments and then backing out at the last minute because I feel frozen. I am stressed trying to make a decision that feels so permanent either way.

**[pbu08-0129]** — Reader context: Difficult prior pregnancies and an uncertain future shape a grieving termination decision.

> I just ordered the pills off of aid access and i feel so heavy and devastated. this is not what i wanted. i know it's the right choice though. i have 2 under two and this was a major slip up on my husband. pregnancy was so awful for me both times i'd never be able to go through it for a third time. we are getting out of military next year so who knows what the future holds especially with two kids. i feel so selfish doing this and this awful heavy feeling in my chest. my poor innocent baby. it hits so much harder since i have two.  before i had kids i probably wouldn't have been this worked up about it, but growing life two times and going through pregnancy- it's changed me. this is my first and last abortion and i am just so devastated. what kind of personality would my baby have? would they look like me? would they look just like their brother and sister? i can't believe I'm taking this life away but i know deep down it's the best decision. my punishment will be having this live with this choice for the rest of my life. sorry i just needed to get this off my chest.

**[pbu08-0226]** — Reader context: Agrees to adoption to preserve the relationship and satisfy the partner's parents.

> We ended up deciding to go the adoption route because he said it was what would make his parents happiest and how we could keep our relationship alive.  
> I never wanted it but I agreed because at the time I guess it did make sense.

**[pbu08-0227]** — Reader context: Reconsiders adoption after abandonment and imagines raising the child herself.

> Im also scared because I’m actually planning ok not following through with adoption and have already envisioned being a single mother because I realized i was only making that decision so that his family could keep dishing him conditional love.

**[pbu08-0334]** — Reader context: At term, regrets continuing an unwanted pregnancy and worries about pausing education.

> He told me he admits that we might’ve not been financially in the best shape to have a baby.   
> I admitted I felt this was at 12 weeks but kept the baby anyway.   
> I’m 40 weeks + 3 days and this feeling is awful.   
> I feel like I never enjoyed this pregnancy, because it was unwanted. I don’t want this baby. But I love my partner. I feel weak for not sticking true to myself or listening to my gut feeling, and getting the abortion. I feel defeated thought it’s no one’s fault but my own.

**[pbu08-0391]** — Reader context: Feels caught between readiness for parenthood now and uncertainty about another chance later.

> My biggest fear about having an abortion is not necessarily that I don’t want to be a mother, I do, but that I might later want a baby and be unable to conceive. As I am overweight. I am also afraid I would feel guilty or grieve the baby I didn’t choose to have.  
> My biggest fear about continuing the pregnancy is that I may not be ready or capable of giving the child the life and stability I want for them, and that they could eventually suffer or resent me.  
> Ultimately, I feel caught between two futures I could both imagine wanting: becoming a mother now, which feels frightening and earlier than planned, or ending this pregnancy and trying for a baby later, which feels closer to the life I originally envisioned but carries the fear and uncertainty of letting this pregnancy go.

**[pbu21-0352]** — Reader context: Wishes for more time and mental health support before abortion.

> I know logically that having another pregnancy may be possible, but emotionally I’m struggling with the fact that I *was* pregnant, and I made the decision to end it. There are moments where I desperately wish I could go back and give myself more time, get better mental health support, and figure out what I actually wanted before making such a permanent decision.

**[pbu21-0353]** — Reader context: Supportive marriage does not resolve uncertainty over wanting children.

> I’m happily married, and my partner is incredibly supportive. He is willing to work through all of this with me and has been there for me emotionally. So this isn’t about not having a supportive partner or having doubts about our relationship. If anything, having his support makes me realize how much of this is something I need to work through within myself.

**[pbu21-0362]** — Reader context: Seventeen-year-old names $110 pill cost and asks for cheaper termination options.

> We live in a state where abortions are illegal, and my mom is heavily against abortion (believes it’s murder and yada yada). Neither my boyfriend or I make enough money to move out and support a baby right now. I’m guessing I’m about a month pregnant as I got my period in the beginning of July. I was considering ordering abortion pills from a neighboring state as I don’t need parental consent to buy them, and they’re about 110 dollars. However I’m unsure of how far along I am and feel insane guilt aborting the baby. Is there any advice anyone could offer me on how to feel better about my decision? Is there a cheaper way to terminate the pregnancy?

**[pbu21-0409]** — Reader context: Physical recovery remains incomplete when another pregnancy starts at four months postpartum.

> Anyways here’s the dilemma. My partner has left it up to me what we do. He says we want 2 and he’s happy if we keep the baby, but it’s also my body & I should do what’s best for me.  
> Sounds simple, but I’ve never wanted to have an abortion. Trust me I’m pro-choice, but I never thought I’d be in this situation. At the same time, I’m not healed at all. I’ve heard it takes 18m. I had preeclampsia, then 2 weeks after birth I was in and out of hospital. I just started feeling better physically like 2 weeks ago & even that’s not completely better. The past 2 days I’ve been feeling depleted, which makes sense now. But even if I can mentally do two under one, I don’t think I physically can. I think ik what I have to do, but I’m having trouble coming to terms with it.

**[pbu32-0162]** — Reader context: Continued pregnancy after canceling abortion decision; partner expects happiness about baby girl.

> How can I be excited when I almost ended this pregnancy? I can’t pick out names and be happy when this baby almost didn’t exist and I feel so guilty.

**[pbu32-0172]** — Reader context: Nineteen-year-old surprised by grief after unwanted pregnancy and reported miscarriage.

> Did anyone else experience this after a miscarriage they didn't want? Why does it hurt emotionally even when the pregnancy wasn't planned or wanted?

**[pbu32-0214]** — Reader context: Two men urge abortion; author considers adoption if unable to parent.

> I just know that I need to make the choice that I can live with not the choice other people want me to make.

**[pbu32-0243]** — Reader context: May be pregnant despite implant; debt and new job shape decision to terminate.

> I feel so preemptively guilty about this, I’ve always wanted to be a mum and it feels like both things can’t be true.

**[pbu32-0292]** — Reader context: Casual worker with job-seeking boyfriend requests honest accounts of both decisions.

> If you terminated, did you regret it? If so, did that feeling change over time?  
> If you continued the pregnancy, did you initially feel unprepared or wish you’d terminated, and how do you feel about your decision now?

**[pbu32-0332]** — Reader context: Prior abortion without regret coexists with current desire for future motherhood.

> It was the right decision for where we were in life, and I don't regret making the best choice we could at the time.

### Missing treatment 3: Reproductive coercion and newborn safety need explicit treatment outside the partner-task model: forced sex, ignored refusal, financial inducement, threats to cause termination, threats to leave for either decision and threats of newborn removal.

**[pbu08-0266]** — Reader context: Partner shifts from support to a monetary offer and threat of disappearance if pregnancy continues.

> When I told him I was pregnant, he initially said he’d support me either way. Now that I’m leaning toward keeping it, he’s completely changed—he says he can’t handle another child, feels overwhelmed, and is now strongly pressuring me to abort. It’s escalated to offering me money ($20K) to terminate and saying he’ll disappear if I continue.

**[pbu08-0335]** — Reader context: Bought organizers but partner prevents preparing the home for the baby.

> I’m currently 37 weeks and have nothing ready for baby boy, and it’s not by choice. I have repeatedly tried to bring things in the home to get things ready, purchased shelves, organizing baskets, bottle organizers, etc. just to prepare and nest the way I’d like. However, that’s not seeming to work with my partner. He does not want the baby items cluttering the home and feels we have to make more space before bringing things in which I have took the initiative to do on several occasions, exhausted and all. He’s very busy so the things he says he will do, often does not get done especially considering we are at a crunch for time.

**[pbu08-0336]** — Reader context: Stores feeding equipment and recovery supplies in a hot car or storage unit at the partner's insistence.

> So far, I have kept everything baby needs either in my car (in the middle of summer heat) or in a storage unit. Just today, I decided to just bring in the bottle sterilizer, my pumps, baby bottles, some creams, & postpartum supplies and got lots of negative remarks and feedback on that. He told me to put the stuff back in my car. I don’t want the things to get damaged and it’s exhausting running back and forth to a storage unit alone putting baby items back and forth.

**[pbu21-0022]** — Reader context: Describes forced sex and repeated infections.

> He gets mad when I refuse sex. But he doesn’t use a condom with anybody. And I am tired of getting stds from him forcing me.

**[pbu21-0219]** — Reader context: Sixteen-year-old describes family and religious pressure to continue pregnancy.

> I don’t know what to do, in my state you need parental consent to get an abortion and my parents are Jehovahs Witnesses and our religion abortions are strictly forbidden. im panicking , I can’t raise a kid and my boyfriend can’t either. all of my family agrees with my dad I dont know what to do! i want to go to college and have a career I can’t raise a kid

**[pbu21-0311]** — Reader context: Describes refusal ignored during sex and subsequent pregnancy.

> During sex he asked if he could leave it in, I told him no, and i pushed him out. He done did, and you could tell he did and kept going for a minute. I felt so disgusted and gross inside and he told me he didn’t feel himself doing it. I sat there staring at the ceiling, thinking “what if i get pregnant” and he looks and me and tells me i won’t be pregnant it’s fine. Well two weeks later a positive test right in front of my eyes, i feel betrayed, upset, and worried about what this means for me and my career.

**[pbu32-0142]** — Reader context: Financial-security worries emerge during pregnancy after two losses and husband's hospitalization.

> He wanted a child badly and told me it's wrong to use birth control.I didn't want children.

**[pbu32-0164]** — Reader context: Reports physical and sexual violence during pregnancy and threats about newborn removal.

> he’s also threatened to take the kid out of the hospital after I give birth and go to Mexico. I’m honestly venting and asking for advice on how I can keep my child safe after the baby is born because I don’t want my baby to be taken and in an unsafe place, and I also don’t want him coming after me and hurting me or my family.

**[pbu32-0235]** — Reader context: Five months pregnant after infant death; excludes threatening partner from delivery room.

> Sometimes he gets mad and tell me to abort the baby or he will do it for me, then he’d say how much he loves the baby, and i don’t know if that’s him grieving or if it’s just to hurt me?

**[pbu32-0263]** — Reader context: Considers excluding husband from labor room due to aggression during pregnancy.

> My husband has been physically aggressive and emotionally abusive to me throughout my pregnancy. I don’t feel safe to have him in that room. I’m due end of October. Will he resent me for this?

**[pbu32-0303]** — Reader context: Threat of abandonment constrains discussion of pregnancy soon after section.

> My fiancé refuses to discuss with me about options. He has said that he will leave me if I have an abortion. He also says anybody could have had his baby and that I'm lazy and bring no value to his life.

**[pbu32-0330]** — Reader context: Reports sexual violence during pregnancy despite bleeding-related refusal.

> he keeps wanting to have sex and I always tell him no because I have hemorrhages and we ended up having sex eight times yesterday. Only two of those times I consent it the other times were forced.

### Missing treatment 4: Consequences feared after disclosure need a place beyond knowing who to call: retaliation after safeguarding, historical-abuse alerts, worries about CPS or adoption, and crisis screening experienced as missing the wanted support.

**[pbu08-0131]** — Reader context: After assault and bleeding, withholds the cause from hospital because of prior disbelief.

> My partner physically assaulted me a few weeks postpartum (dragged and punched me in the back). I later went to hospital due to heavy bleeding after this. I didn’t tell the hospital what had truly happened since they had a track record of not believing me anyways, and I just felt too scared and depressed to do anything anymore. He also refused to take his full paid parental leave and went back to work after only 2 weeks postpartum.

**[pbu21-0142]** — Reader context: Asks what support exists when disability prevents infant care.

> If I don't recover and am unfit to care for the baby due to limited mobility and disability, what happens? Does CPS intervene? Can I ask a hospital for resources? Is there some sort of govt assistance navigating this scenario? Would I be forced to give baby up for adoption? I very much want her, but it's getting to the point where it's impossible to meet her needs.

**[pbu21-0250]** — Reader context: Therapy’s crisis screening does not answer pervasive pregnancy worry.

> i never imagined my pregnancy would be so freaking depressing! i’m stressed out 24/7. i’m always worried about my baby. always crying or having panic attacks. literally can’t even function correctly or comfortably. folding baby clothes? breakdown. can’t find my cat? breakdown. bf just existing? breakdown. feeling guilty for eating something unhealthy? stressed thinking i ruined something. forgot to take prenatals? impending doom. like DAMN I JUST WANT TO BREATHE. i’m just so anxious. i’ve tried therapy and it’s always the same exact thing. “do you want to harm yourself” “call 988” LIKE NO. i just want a peace of mind. im going on 33 weeks and my mind had been on high alert since 20 weeks. im just so worried about my baby being okay & being a good mother. it’s all just too much on me 😩 and i feel so guilty for complaining.

**[pbu21-0338]** — Reader context: Safeguarding referral raises fear of retaliation after asking midwife for support.

> After the appointment, she called me to state she would be speaking to her safeguarding team on Thursday to see whether a referral needed to be made to social services. I understand they have a duty of care, and they have pathways to follow. But this has stressed me out even further, if they make a referral to social services about him he will be made aware that I’ve sought advice from them and he will assume I’m trying to take his son off him, but he also has a daughter from a previous relationship and will assume I’ve tried to implicate that situation too, no doubt bringing more verbal abuse to me at a time I really don’t need it.

**[pbu21-0339]** — Reader context: Fears disclosing distress created more risk and administrative pressure.

> I don’t know what to do, I told the midwife how I felt that I just needed support and advice and not another thing to stress over, I don’t want to worry about social services involvement, I don’t want to have to worry about more back lash from him and I feel like I did the wrong thing telling them how much of a difficult time I’ve been having now. Am I just being a hormonal, overthinking mess?

**[pbu32-0087]** — Reader context: Badger Notes safeguarding flag follows disclosure of past abuse, not current relationship.

> Due to this I’m quite confused and a bit upset since I’m not under any threat and don’t want any referral to any teams. I’ve got my second midwife appointment this week and my partner also really wants to come but I don’t want him to because of this now.

**[pbu32-0199]** — Reader context: Considers shelter after family conflict; earlier reply fears child removal if using one.

> I did think of going to a women’s shelter. I can’t imagine it being worse than being stuck in a room for days and crying. I will contemplate a little more and then make a decision.

### Missing treatment 5: Listener identity needs direct inclusion: a pregnant trans teenager who wants to be a dad; actual teenage pregnancy and parental-consent constraints; a pregnant wife supporting her grieving wife; a Black parent choosing between perceived racial safety in care and family stress.

**[pbu08-0212]** — Reader context: Pregnant trans teenager fears family invalidation and being called mother rather than father.

> I think I actually want to keep the baby, but I’m terrified of being invalidated for being trans AND pregnant. I come from a really transphobic family and I already know they’d call me “mom” instead of dad, which honestly hurts just thinking about it.

**[pbu08-0217]** — Reader context: Teen feels a disappointment to parents despite their attempts to support the pregnancy.

> Hey guys. I just told both my parents I’m pregnant and I’m truly in shambles. They are trying to hard to be supportive but I can see their broken hearts and I feel like the biggest disappointment. I’m 18 for context and this obviously isint what they wanted for me but I couldn’t do an abortion. I genuinely feel like my heart is broken and our relationship will never be the same. Has anyone else been through this and came out on the flip side okay?

**[pbu08-0411]** — Reader context: Wants a more diverse hospital and safer community but fears the family housing environment.

> I do not feel comfortable giving birth to my child in the town I live in...black women are 3-4x more likely die in child birth, my husband promised he'd advocate for me if we decide to birth in this town but I just am so scared. (There's a city an hour away if it came down to it, I would birth there cause there's more diversity..) We hate this town, it doesn't align with us, politically or morally and not somewhere we feel safe as a interracial couple with a baby..we just want out of here. 
> 
> The hospital where my mom lives is one of the best in California, and I feel reassured by their medical expertise and diversity..and I could be around famiy and support..But....the thought living with my mom and in that environment is not something I want to handle right now being pregnant. It's too much.

**[pbu21-0015]** — Reader context: Sixteen-year-old fears pregnancy denial by an ex who blocked contact.

> Okay so yesterday I found out I'm pregnant and the guy im pregnant with is my ex who cheated on me and I don't know what to do we broke up a month ago and im scared he'll start saying its not his but he's the only one I've ever been with and like im going to call him today off my bsfs phone cus he blocked me everywhere but what do I do if he says it's not his BTW im 16 he's 18

**[pbu21-0219]** — Reader context: Sixteen-year-old describes family and religious pressure to continue pregnancy.

> I don’t know what to do, in my state you need parental consent to get an abortion and my parents are Jehovahs Witnesses and our religion abortions are strictly forbidden. im panicking , I can’t raise a kid and my boyfriend can’t either. all of my family agrees with my dad I dont know what to do! i want to go to college and have a career I can’t raise a kid

**[pbu21-0266]** — Reader context: Pregnant wife wants space for her own grief alongside her spouse’s.

> At the same time, I’m 28 weeks pregnant, taking care of our 2-year-old, trying to support my wife through the loss of her mom, and honestly struggling myself.  
> My wife and I have had some ups and downs recently. During one conversation, she told me she feels like she has to choose between grieving her mom and saving our marriage. Hearing that broke my heart because I never wanted her to feel that way. At the same time, I feel like whenever I try to express my own struggles, it gets interpreted as me criticizing her ability to grieve or somehow making it about me.  
> The truth is, I’m grieving too. I’m scared too.  
> I’m exhausted. I don’t feel like myself. My mental health has been suffering. I feel lonely most days. On top of everything else, there are things happening at work that make me unsure if I’ll even have a job by the end of the year.  
> I feel guilty even writing this because I know my wife is going through one of the hardest things a person can experience. But I also feel like I’m drowning and don’t know where to put my own feelings. I spend so much time trying to be strong for everyone else that I don’t know what to do when I need someone to be there for me.  
> I don’t want my wife to grieve less. I just wish there was room for my pain too.

**[pbu21-0362]** — Reader context: Seventeen-year-old names $110 pill cost and asks for cheaper termination options.

> We live in a state where abortions are illegal, and my mom is heavily against abortion (believes it’s murder and yada yada). Neither my boyfriend or I make enough money to move out and support a baby right now. I’m guessing I’m about a month pregnant as I got my period in the beginning of July. I was considering ordering abortion pills from a neighboring state as I don’t need parental consent to buy them, and they’re about 110 dollars. However I’m unsure of how far along I am and feel insane guilt aborting the baby. Is there any advice anyone could offer me on how to feel better about my decision? Is there a cheaper way to terminate the pregnancy?

### Missing treatment 6: Pregnancy count, living-child count and the gestating-parent role require separate fields. Previous surrogacy, a stillbirth, repeated losses and mismatched pregnancy numbers between partners break a simple first/second/third ladder.

**[pbu08-0058]** — Reader context: Nausea eases just as recurrent migraine symptoms trigger panic.

> Currently 11w5d. This is my overall third pregnancy but one one living child so far. I’ve had horrible nausea/vomiting/dizziness/motion sickness from 6 weeks until about 11 weeks. I thought AMAZING, I can finally feel somewhat normal and BAM Tuesday I see aura and have a mild headache after. Wednesday morning BAM again with aura but no real pain after, just PURE PANIC which made me throw up lol. I have history of migraine with aura but I haven’t had one in 7 YEARS!

**[pbu08-0108]** — Reader context: Prior losses make travel more than half an hour from home feel unsafe.

> Hi all, I'm currently 11 weeks with my rainbow baby. Fourth pregnancy and I currently have only had one successful pregnancy and have one living child age 2. My anxiety during pregnancy is always awful anyway, especially after two miscarriages. I don't like being away from home when pregnant, as in not further than half hour away at any time because I'm paranoid something is more likely to go wrong with baby and me if I'm away. (I know there's no actual logic to this)

**[pbu21-0031]** — Reader context: Repeated early losses changed what a positive test means.

> The second time around, we tried for a while. I was pregnant about 8 times in the past year and a half and it always ended in an early loss around week 6-9. I decided not to take any pregnancy ‘seriously‘ before week 12 to ‘guard my heart’. I could only scoff at movie scenes where women get excited over a positive pregnancy test. A positive pregnancy test meant nothing to me, I had one in almost every cycle. I think the whole experience left me a bit cynical.

**[pbu21-0292]** — Reader context: Unexpectedly high early hormone result raises concern despite knowing one result is limited.

> This is my 5th pregnancy and will be my 3rd living child if all goes well (had a miscarriage after each of my kids). Today I am 13DPO (3w5d) with a textbook cycle. HCG today is 398, progesterone is 39.4. Obviously I know a single level can’t say much and I am getting a repeat Monday, but this just caught me SO off guard because it seems SO high? Any input would be greatly appreciated!

**[pbu21-0341]** — Reader context: Pregnancy number differs between partners, changing the shared sense of first experiences.

> I’m grieving the fact that I see, day in and day out, that this isn’t my partner’s first baby.  
> I imagined tummy kisses. I imagined him talking to our baby. I imagined him being fascinated by every little thing I was experiencing and asking me lots of questions about what it felt like.  
> Instead, sometimes I feel like I’m experiencing this enormous, life-changing thing almost by myself.  
> And the hardest part is that my partner is someone who is so emotionally in tune. He understands feelings, communicates well and shows me love in many ways.

**[pbu32-0028]** — Reader context: Currently pregnant after a stillbirth; pregnancy number differs from living-child count.

> When people ask how many kids I have I always want to say “6” but then that requires an explanation when they ask about genders and names and all that. I don’t want my son to be forgotten or overlooked but I also don’t always want to talk about the situation.

**[pbu32-0232]** — Reader context: Unexpected pregnancy after ten years of infertility and son born via surrogate.

> I really struggled with the newborn stage and first three months of my son’s life and I wasn’t even postpartum! I have a really good support system and partner but it was still so hard for me and I cried a lot.

**[pbu32-0239]** — Reader context: Bleeding and declining fetal heart rate; compares OB assessment with hopeful peer stories.

> This is my 5th pregnancy, including a stillbirth, in 13 months.    I realize these mostly heal on their own but would love to hear if anyone has been in a similar boat or is currently experiencing this.  My OB is not hopeful for this pregnancy but I hear so many success stories …

### Missing treatment 8: Care affected by autism, disability, chronic pain, eating-disorder history, rural distance or wildfire smoke needs specific material rather than a generic personalization promise.

**[pbu08-0186]** — Reader context: Fear of falling asleep while feeding makes nursing-pillow usefulness, price and multipurpose value urgent.

> I tend to have a sort of narcolepsy response where I involuntarily fall asleep during important tasks if I don't get good sleep, so this has been my biggest worry. Doctors haven't been much help on this concern, they just tell me to get better sleep and *somehow* that hasn't been a watertight solution, so I rely on myself to manage it in different ways. The narcolepsy-type response I have you'd think would give me a no-brainer "oh yes, I should probably buy this nursing pillow so I don't worry about immediate dangers if I fall asleep while holding my baby", but I also see some moms who come out saying "literally you can use a regular pillow and it works fine" so I get a bit stuck there. There's also the question of what I would possibly use the nursing pillow for when I don't need it, do I just...sell it? That seems like a waste of money, which we are tight on. So I'd really appreciate any personal opinions about nursing pillows; whether you think they're necessary, whether you'd definitely or totally recommend them for my situation or a pillow would do the exact same thing I'm looking for (comfort and baby's safety), AND I'd especially appreciate if there are any recommendations for nursing pillows that have uses outside of being a nursing pillow, as the good quality ones I tend to see everywhere..only have that one use, I'd much rather have something multipurpose.

**[pbu08-0325]** — Reader context: With pre-existing nerve issues, feels failed by care that labels pain abnormal without answers.

> I have nerve issues. I have a mild case of cerebral palsy in my legs. Before I got pregnant, I was having more nerve issues and also nerve issues in my head and having really bad migraines. I’ve noticed since I found out it was pregnant the nerves in my legs, back, and head have increased. So I have a feeling it has something to do with that. I have been trying to advocate for myself and it just seems like healthcare is failing me. They’re definitely telling me it’s not normal, but I’m not getting many answers. I have also have had bad chills at night and a few fevers. The one night I cried myself to sleep because my entire body hurt like my back to my ankles.
> 
> Has anyone experienced this so early on? And when I tell you, this is not just pregnancy back pain. It is way more than that. I can barely walk or bend over. I would say the pain is about an eight or nine. I’ve been just so conflicted and in so much pain that I don’t even know what to do. Everything I read online puts me in the high risk category for me but baby should be okay. I just can’t do normal day tasks.

**[pbu08-0363]** — Reader context: Sensory difficulties and pregnancy distress include suicidal thoughts despite ongoing therapy.

> I am autistic and already have sensory issues and this shit is putting me over the edge. I fantasize about jumping off my balcony and weird fucked up shit like that. And before you ask. Yes I go to therapy because yes I struggle enough on my own without being pregnant .

**[pbu08-0409]** — Reader context: Moving for family support means accepting repeated body criticism during pregnancy.

> The only issue is my mom is a difficult person to be around. She's been very critical of me this entire pregnancy. Making comments about my weight, eating habits, catasptrophizing every symptom I've had, name calls me, and ruins every baby name. I was 118lb and only gained 20 pounds this whole pregnancy... I've struggled with an ED before, so some of the comments cut deep. I've laid down boundaries, asked her to stop multiple times and she won't. when called out on her behavior, she plays the victim and shuts down for hours or even days.

**[pbu21-0221]** — Reader context: Asks for practical hygiene preparation for surgery at a larger body size.

> My question is when it comes to the recovery side of things (specifically using the bathroom) how did you plus size girlies do it? The reach around to wipe I'm sure isn't easy... I do have a peri bottle but something about not wiping just makes me feel dirty. Has anyone else struggled with this or these thoughts? How did you get through it? Any tips for my upcoming recovery bathroom trips? I would love any helpful suggestions. Thank you in advance!!!

**[pbu21-0281]** — Reader context: Disrupted water routine causes meltdown and shame during pregnancy.

> I am autistic. I had a bad meltdown this morning because my routine was disturbed. I always drink bottled water so I can track how much I drink and it is familiar to me. I am currently 30 weeks pregnant and can’t lift anything heavy. We ran out of water last night so, I asked my husband to come with me very early this morning to the store to help me. He didn’t get home til very late last night so he did not want to get up and needed more sleep before he works another long shift today. I took that TERRIBLY. I started panicking and my husband suggested I just go buy a gallon of water. I didnt want to buy a gallon of water. Straying from the normal routine felt like the end of the world. We ended up having a really heated argument and I had a terrible meltdown where I was hitting my hands on my head and thighs, screaming and crying. My baby is perfectly fine. I didn’t hit my belly but, now I feel immense shame and regret for what I did. I couldnt control myself. I know I am going to be a great parent but right now I feel like the worst. Please say anything negative. I just need support. I am not in danger to myself or anyone else and would never intentionally do anything to hurt myself at this point in my life.

**[pbu21-0295]** — Reader context: Chronic pain relief conflicts with pregnancy concerns and clinician’s advice.

> Also what the f\*\*\* can I do going forward without my hot baths and flower!!! It’s seriously my life line for pain management; I’ve tried every damn trick in the book with 0 luck on helping my pain.   
> My current holisticy OBGYN doctor said she’d rather have me consume a small amount of marijuana and keep my food down and not be in constant pain, but I feel so conflicted. What do I do. Will my baby even be okay?

**[pbu21-0300]** — Reader context: Wildfire smoke overwhelms protective measures and adds guilt about disability and pregnancy.

> Anyway, despite air purifiers and trying to seal my house the smoke is so thick and bad it’s pouring in the last hour and I’ve got my N95 on. I worked as a nurse for a decade so I’m even fit tested for this one, lungs so bad they kicked me out of my career. I wish this wasn’t happening the year I was pregnant, I feel guilt for my disability and being pregnant. Like I chose a bad year, shouldn’t have done it. I don’t know hopeless and depressed I guess, third trimester is hard enough. I’ll probably feel better if I get my door sealed.

**[pbu21-0402]** — Reader context: Partner questions parenting capacity because of chronic illness.

> 10. My partner brought up how my chronic pain and fatigue from my chronic illnesses sometimes limits my productivity and physical ability, and that it makes him have a lack of confidence in my ability to take care of a baby. This ableist attitude expressed by him was crushingly disappointing and hurtful. It ripped the rug out from under me and made me question why he’s even with me if he feels that I am so deficient. Like does he see me as a viable partner to raise a family with or has he just been keeping me around bc he thinks I’m nice to look at? This ableism from him made me briefly feel like I should terminate everything— the pregnancy, the trip, and the relationship, and just be a recluse. But he apologized after seeing how he broke me, and has resumed being the nurturing supportive partner he normally is, so I forgave him and chose to reinstate my trust in him.

**[pbu32-0044]** — Reader context: Reflects on postpartum eating disorder while planning feeding for second baby.

> I had a really difficult time mentally postpartum. I was unhappy with my body to begin with, and two weeks after giving birth, I started eating 1,200 calories a day because I desperately wanted to lose weight. Over the next year, I lost 115 lbs and became dangerously anorexic.

**[pbu32-0082]** — Reader context: Fourth pregnancy; new GD restrictions collide with already limited diet.

> It’s taking all of the fun out of food, I’m constantly worrying about baby when my readings come back above 7.8 as I’m newly diagnosed and trying to see what works for me and what doesn’t. I’m struggling with this as I have suspected autism and I have a very limited diet anyway.

**[pbu32-0253]** — Reader context: Distance and toddler care limit access while vomiting returns after running out of medication.

> My local hospital is a 6 hour drive away, and i haven't been able to leave my bathroom floor all day lol plus I have a toddler. Only time we do hospital trips is if death is nearing honestly ahaha (farming family here)

**[pbu32-0309]** — Reader context: First pregnancy with weight loss and demanding counseling work; usual nausea remedy feels unavailable.

> I also have epilepsy and Hashimotos disease so my medication has been changing a lot (I’ve been lurking on subs and I can’t take Unisom because of the epilepsy).

**[pbu08-0115]** — Reader context: Pregnancy brings unfamiliar disgust toward household dogs and fear it will persist.

I didn't have morning sickness, so it isn't that, and I never felt this way before pregnancy, so just wanted some advice or someone to tell me they also felt this way so I know it's a phase and not permanent, which is worrying me. Most behaviours that are bothering me are hygiene related. Also, any advice on how to correct any of these behaviours may help. Here are the main things that bother me during my pregnancy:

**[pbu08-0116]** — Reader context: Fear of dogs hurting the baby changes behavior at home.

I got sooo angry when this happened. I never shouted or hurt the dogs, but I'd spend the day in another room away from them. I didn't want to be around them. I had an irrational fear during the time that my dogs were going to hurt my baby by jumping up.

**[pbu08-0126]** — Reader context: Prior losses led to abstinence throughout one pregnancy and fear of resuming intimacy.

>

**[pbu08-0142]** — Reader context: Medical clearance after tearing has not removed fear of sex, pain or infection.

**[pbu08-0247]** — Reader context: New aversion to partner touch and sex differs from a prior pregnancy.

But I just hate him, I feel disgusted when he kisses me, any touch from him is like he is sucking my soul with his hands and I feel 0 joy when he comes him :( he doesn’t know any of this I’m afraid I’ll hurt him if he knew, I didn’t have this during my first pregnancy so I’m not sure how to explain it to him. Even sex.. I used to want it 4-5 times a week now it is once every other week, I’d rather masturbate… how did the other pregnant women handle this?

**[pbu08-0360]** — Reader context: Asks about intensified sexual desire for conception even after partner becomes pregnant.

**[pbu08-0352]** — Reader context: A miscarriage dream feels predictive because a similar dream preceded an earlier loss.

Could it be me being nervous for the future or something? It’s weird because my first pregnancy I had a miscarriage dream when fetus wasn’t viable and ended in a miscarriage. Hopefully it isn’t what I think it is that’s going to end up happening again.

**[pbu21-0366]** — Reader context: Unexpected later positive test is linked in the author’s mind to a niece’s comment.

> My brother brushed it off as she just remembers when you used to be pregnant.. but she was only just turned 3 at that time, a year ago and she never spoke about it before EVER. About a week later (which was only yesterday) I decided let me just take one more test mostly out of delusion because I had just tested 5-6 days ago and got a negative. To my surprise it was positive (i took multiple tests to check) and it being the exact time as my pregnancy began from my first loss and my niece somehow knowing it before me it just all feels very strange. Could be nothing lol but, Just thought I’d share

### Missing treatment 11: The share also includes professional or public-commentary voices that should retain their role: a doula asking how to support bereaved clients, a policy announcement about loss leave, a physician's criticism of docusate, and reaction to unverified celebrity publicity. These are not interchangeable with a parent's purchase request or independently established clinical/policy evidence.
