## Money

This inventory includes stated amounts, completed or contemplated purchases, gifts, willingness to spend or refusal, income/leave/insurance limits and resource constraints. The full passages preserve every amount and condition within each record. A service already used or a product acquired does not by itself establish that this parent paid for it. The $20K coercive offer, abortion-pill price, veterinary costs and public-policy announcement retain their different meanings.

Amounts to locate quickly: surrogacy up to $200k in others’ experiences [pbu08-0139]; maternity pants/shorts $115–150 a pair [pbu08-0178], with conditional willingness to spend [pbu08-0179]; $20K offered to pressure termination [pbu08-0266]; $3,399 NIPT bill and discount/assistance inquiry [pbu08-0394] [pbu08-0395] [pbu08-0396]; a job alternative paying $5/hr more [pbu21-0131]; 500–800 average pay with interval unstated and upwards of 30k savings [pbu21-0155]; $200 per pediatric visit [pbu21-0160]; $100 private ultrasound [pbu21-0273]; shower $80 per person and around $640 total [pbu21-0330]; about 110 dollars for abortion pills with a request for a cheaper option [pbu21-0362]; three hundred dollars saved from tips toward genetic testing [pbu21-0387]; uninsured midwife about $4–5k and payment plans [pbu21-0400]; hundreds of dollars refused for baby-vitals surveillance [pbu32-0031]; thousands spent on a two-day trip [pbu32-0036]; four paid prenatal-leave weeks at issue [pbu32-0149]; around 28k over 26 weeks paid leave, currency unstated [pbu32-0337]; about $1200 just for dog X-rays, deferred [pbu32-0338]. The full quotes below preserve which amounts are paid, proposed, offered, refused or reported from others.

**Refunds:** no quote in this share explicitly mentions a refund. **Guide valuation:** no quote establishes payment or willingness to pay for the proposed Mamivio guide.

**[pbu08-0013]** — Reader context: Unplanned pregnancy, variable earnings and job losses undermine stable housing.

> I’m 37 weeks tomorrow and my partner and I have been struggling nearly this entire pregnancy establishing financial stability. this was unplanned and at the time of finding out about the pregnancy I lived in a chaotic home with both of my parents who drink and fight. the dogs are always barking their heads off and it is a mess literally. We moved in together in my first trimester and it was the most peaceful and self healing I have ever encountered. we’re both quiet and just simple partners who really enjoy our peace. I found my depression nearly vanished, my anxiety was fully manageable and I felt… alive more so than I ever had. fast forward to my third trimester now and our financial stability has become rocky again. he does sales so it’s always just a guessing game if he makes quota and can support us and the jobs I’ve been able to find hardly pay me enough for groceries. then I lose my job twice. now Im too far along to work.

**[pbu08-0014]** — Reader context: At term, caregiving for a grandmother overwhelms the author and disrupts bonding.

> we chose that option as the best for our daughter but I realized only a week in how bad of a decision that was because of how rapidly her memory declines and trying to manage my own mental health has now plummeted. Her dog pees all over, yaps constantly, and now it’s all on me. I’m crying all the time, finding myself having to go on car rides to disappear for three hours and just feel so anxious and stressed I cant even bond with my belly anymore. I’m due in two weeks and I am terrified of how much I’ve taken on. I’m more so just upset I didn’t work harder, save more money, or just overall push myself before pregnancy to become stable. If anyone has any mental health advice or  stories similar at all please share. we’re stuck in a 1 year lease now with her and financially couldnt do it ourselves so at the time this seemed like the best option.

**[pbu08-0016]** — Reader context: Better finances and preparation do not remove fear of repeating newborn distress.

> This time has been different because we both have better jobs, make more money and are way more prepared. I’ve been so excited for her, but lately I’m starting to feel less excited and nervous because I’m worried I will be the same way I was with my son.   
> I’m 31 now and in a better place in my life. I wake up in the middle of the night and can’t fall back asleep cos I’m thinking about what will happen once she’s here. I want it to be different this time, I don’t want to be anxious and scared of my baby like I was the first time.  
> Wondering if anyone has gone through anything similar.

**[pbu08-0030]** — Reader context: Wants support and celebration alongside wanting motherhood itself.

> Of course I wanted a baby. I wanted a baby more than anything. I also wanted a family and I wanted a village. I’m having this baby because I want to be a mom and experience all the joy of raising a little human, but can’t I \*also\* want to be celebrated and gifted and cared for? Especially when I’ve watched SO MANY other women in my life have that experience? What’s wrong with wanting what I’ve seen other women in his family have?

**[pbu08-0033]** — Reader context: Miscarriage nightmares and anxiety during a wait for insurance coverage.

> hey everyone i’m 8 weeks and 2 days pregnant and keep having scary dreams of miscarrying. i’m still waiting for my insurance to kick in here in the next week so my anxiety has been on 10.

**[pbu08-0052]** — Reader context: Insurance calls compete with feeding, pumping, changing and eating.

> I thought I was doing fine with it until today when I found out my insurance is all messed up. So I am having to call insurance while changing diapers and pumping. All the while I have my husband reminding me to eat. Which yes I get I need to eat but where do people find the time?? She's 2 weeks old today and up every 2ish hours to eat, change diapers, burp ect. And then I pump. That basically takes up all my time and then people also tell me to sleep.

**[pbu08-0061]** — Reader context: Single parent wants another baby but housing and finances constrain the decision.

> My daughter’s father isn’t involved in her life due to a history of domestic violence and legal issues, so I’ve been raising her on my own. If I continue this pregnancy, I’d eventually need to move out of my current home, figure out finances/housing, and introduce my daughter to someone I’ve only known for a short time.
> 
> If housing and money weren’t concerns, I honestly think want to keep this baby. Any advice is appreciated

**[pbu08-0069]** — Reader context: Repeatedly checks for screening results, then buys baby supplies after reassurance.

> At twelve weeks, I had my NIPT test done. Eleven vials of blood were taken that day to check for any possible issues and to find out the gender. I held my breath, for what seemed like eternity. Checking the portal multiple times a day for the results. Two weeks later, the results came in at 4:30 in the morning. I woke my husband up so we could open them together. I couldn’t wait. I had thought about doing a gender reveal, but by then I was already too tired to plan another party or gathering. So, there we were, half awake, opening the results together. It was a boy! More than anything, I felt relief. The test showed no issues, and for the first time in weeks, I felt like I could breathe. I went on to purchasing pacifiers, onsies, blankets, teddy bear stuffies and more, all in colors ranging from green to blue.

**[pbu08-0071]** — Reader context: Contradictory advice arrives while the author is waiting for an insured OB referral.

> He told me I need to stop taking my trazodone- which I asked about during appt and he initially said was fine and then I get a message from the office this morning, telling me to discontinue. And to ask my OBGYN what they recommend. My office knows I’ve been waiting for a referral to go through to an OB that covers my insurance. So they know I don’t currently have an OB, but essentially don’t care.

**[pbu08-0092]** — Reader context: Unexpected closely spaced pregnancy raises worries about recurrent HG, support and money.

> I am eight months postpartum and have been feeling sick and unable to eat. I had HG during my previous pregnancy. I don’t know what to do; I’m already so stressed with my eight-month-old. But I just got this positive test that came out immediately. I am not sure if I should keep this baby. There are many factors at play.  I live in another state with my husband and son, so I won't have much help, and I will have a 1.5 year old by then. And of course, there’s the added financial strain of another baby. But my son wouldn't be an only child, and I think he wouldn’t be close to his cousins because they're so far away. This came as a shock, and I still don't know how I feel. It’s only been a few days, but I am around 7 weeks and just emotionally stunned right now

**[pbu08-0097]** — Reader context: Previously effective ADHD medication seems ineffective after pregnancy; cost limits switching.

> So, before getting pregnant I was on straterra for my adhd. It did wonders for me and I could actually function like a normal human being. Well then I got pregnant and obviously had to stop taking them. I started taking them again when I could no longer breast feed my daughter and they just haven’t been the same ever since and it’s been months already. 40mg was great for me, now I’m on 80mg and legit still have such bad adhd symptoms. Yes I have talked to my psychiatrist about switching meds but omg adhd meds are expensive if you don’t have insurance (I lost my insurance as I quit my job and I’ve been trying to get Medicaid ever since) so I just feel like I’m stuck at the moment. Moms with adhd, did you go through the same thing after pregnancy and birth? Do you just change completely after pregnancy I don’t get. Can my body not metabolize the same as it once did? I’m struggling so bad at the moment 😭

**[pbu08-0124]** — Reader context: Reply adds collapse, transfusion and unexpectedly tiny baby clothing to an otherwise positive birth narrative.

> After I gave birth I was so desperate to poo I stood up and passed out due to sheer exhaustion and ended up needing a blood transfusion. My baby was born so teeny tiny (2nd percentile), that non of his clothes fit and my husband had to do a mad dash to m&s and buy their 'Tiny Baby' range. He was so small he fell asleep every time I breastfed him because he had absolutely no energy 💔. He's 20 weeks old today, and he's the absolute best thing that ever happened to me, after 4 recurrent miscarriages ❤️

**[pbu08-0129]** — Reader context: Difficult prior pregnancies and an uncertain future shape a grieving termination decision.

> I just ordered the pills off of aid access and i feel so heavy and devastated. this is not what i wanted. i know it's the right choice though. i have 2 under two and this was a major slip up on my husband. pregnancy was so awful for me both times i'd never be able to go through it for a third time. we are getting out of military next year so who knows what the future holds especially with two kids. i feel so selfish doing this and this awful heavy feeling in my chest. my poor innocent baby. it hits so much harder since i have two.  before i had kids i probably wouldn't have been this worked up about it, but growing life two times and going through pregnancy- it's changed me. this is my first and last abortion and i am just so devastated. what kind of personality would my baby have? would they look like me? would they look just like their brother and sister? i can't believe I'm taking this life away but i know deep down it's the best decision. my punishment will be having this live with this choice for the rest of my life. sorry i just needed to get this off my chest.

**[pbu08-0131]** — Reader context: After assault and bleeding, withholds the cause from hospital because of prior disbelief.

> My partner physically assaulted me a few weeks postpartum (dragged and punched me in the back). I later went to hospital due to heavy bleeding after this. I didn’t tell the hospital what had truly happened since they had a track record of not believing me anyways, and I just felt too scared and depressed to do anything anymore. He also refused to take his full paid parental leave and went back to work after only 2 weeks postpartum.

**[pbu08-0139]** — Reader context: Partner's traumatic recollection changes plans for another birth and prompts adoption or surrogacy discussion.

> I feel like I have gained an understanding for what happened now. We would love to have another baby,I want to experience pregnancy again ,give my baby another sibling and grow as a family. I feel I would be selfish of me to want to do that you know? My husband had made it clear yes to another baby but that was extremely traumatic for him. I understand like I said I don’t remember any of that but I respect that. We’d rather have me here than not. So we talked about adoption and or having a surrogate . Adoption seems easiest route financially and we have lots of love to give. Having a surrogate could be up to $200k based off others experiences who were able to do that route. Has anyone ever gone through this or something similar? What are your thoughts?

**[pbu08-0141]** — Reader context: Wants supplement options for recovery and breastfeeding beyond the OB's prenatal recommendation.

> I’m almost a month postpartum and EBF my little boy. I’ve asked my OB for recommendations on supplements and he really only directed me to continue my prenatal. I’m totally cool with this but I was hoping to get some ideas/input/experiences from yall when it comes to other options for supplements? I have a few days left of my prenatal and trying to decide what to purchase!   
> My priorities would be recovery and breastfeeding support however I’m really interested in any thoughts you have!  
> TIA!!

**[pbu08-0153]** — Reader context: Seeks affordable ways to support his wife's grief on Mother's Day.

> What are some ideas to help my wife grieve while still celebrating her Mother’s Day with our 2 kids? Money is a barrier for us but we can still afford to go out if that’s the best course of action. I don’t really care if we do celebrate, I just want what’s best for her at this moment. I am so lost at this point and could use the ideas. Thank you all

**[pbu08-0155]** — Reader context: Past loss and paid-only support complicate a father's wish for another child.

> Some trauma. We could technically have three kids by now. An early miscarriage. A perfect baby, home run. And around a year and a half ago we went through losing a pregnancy in one of the worst ways imaginable. I have my home run baby. I sometimes feel like expecting another is just greedy, gambling with my wife's health.
> We're totally on our own. MIL comes around a bit but everything that resembles help, support, etc. is paid. Nanny, sitters, etc.

**[pbu08-0161]** — Reader context: Many bought going-home outfits become the choice of a stillborn baby's only outfit.

> The nurses ask what clothes to put her in. Earlier in the week of the due date, I was going to the likes of Next and Zara to find the perfect ‘going home’ outfit. I couldn’t decide so bought so many different outfits. My Wifey asks me which one they should put her in and I choose the outfit. Tears stream down my face as I deliberate over which one. I choose such a cute floral one. 100% pure cotton. The only outfit she will ever wear in her life.

**[pbu08-0173]** — Reader context: A newly bought supplement becomes a safety question minutes after a positive test.

> lol i just tested positive like 5 min ago but I bought creatine yesterday to go back to the gym and im wondering if thats even safe to take?? I looked it up and it said there’s limited research didn’t know if any pregnant gym girlies know much about it

**[pbu08-0178]** — Reader context: Targeted maternity-clothing ads offer expensive items after earlier clothes were given away.

> This is likely my last pregnancy (number 3) And I gave away the majority of my maternity clothes thinking we were done at 2! I keep getting targeted for Soon Maternity clothes, where they advertise shorts and pants you can wear pregnant and postpartum with their elastic waist band. They’re on the pricey side, at $115-150 a pair.

**[pbu08-0179]** — Reader context: Willing to pay for maternity clothes if they remain useful after weight loss.

> Has anyone bought from them? Do you recommend it? Can you really wear them postpartum? I’m willing to spend the money on a single pair of shorts and pants if it’s something I can truly wear even after I’ve lost the baby weight. Curious of your thoughts!

**[pbu08-0186]** — Reader context: Fear of falling asleep while feeding makes nursing-pillow usefulness, price and multipurpose value urgent.

> I tend to have a sort of narcolepsy response where I involuntarily fall asleep during important tasks if I don't get good sleep, so this has been my biggest worry. Doctors haven't been much help on this concern, they just tell me to get better sleep and *somehow* that hasn't been a watertight solution, so I rely on myself to manage it in different ways. The narcolepsy-type response I have you'd think would give me a no-brainer "oh yes, I should probably buy this nursing pillow so I don't worry about immediate dangers if I fall asleep while holding my baby", but I also see some moms who come out saying "literally you can use a regular pillow and it works fine" so I get a bit stuck there. There's also the question of what I would possibly use the nursing pillow for when I don't need it, do I just...sell it? That seems like a waste of money, which we are tight on. So I'd really appreciate any personal opinions about nursing pillows; whether you think they're necessary, whether you'd definitely or totally recommend them for my situation or a pillow would do the exact same thing I'm looking for (comfort and baby's safety), AND I'd especially appreciate if there are any recommendations for nursing pillows that have uses outside of being a nursing pillow, as the good quality ones I tend to see everywhere..only have that one use, I'd much rather have something multipurpose.

**[pbu08-0216]** — Reader context: Appointment attendance brings no questions or interest; plans to leave before birth.

> He has not bought anything for her. He has shown up to appointments but not asked a single question, or shown interest in any other way. He has not tried to make any improvements on his drinking or mental wellbeing. I AM leaving as i will not raise my daughter around this, and will not continue to hope for him to get better. I just don’t know what to do.

**[pbu08-0229]** — Reader context: Fear of herbal exposure, attachment and abortion guilt creates an uncertain wait for ultrasound.

> I’ve been drinking a lot of butterfly pea and mugwort tea, and after reading stories online, I’ve become terrified that I may have harmed my pregnancy. I’m currently 6 weeks and 1 day pregnant. I ordered the abortion pill when I was feeling overwhelmed, but now part of me feels like I want to keep the baby, which is making everything even more emotionally confusing.
> 
> I feel stuck because I’m scared to wait for the 8-week ultrasound, knowing they likely won’t be able to detect possible abnormalities that early. I’m afraid of getting attached and later finding out something is wrong, but I’m also afraid that if I take the pill, I’ll carry deep guilt afterward.

**[pbu08-0242]** — Reader context: Relative postpones a gift in case the baby dies.

> She said she wanted to gift us something she appreciated with her kids but she didn't want to buy it before I gave birth in case the kid died during these last months or during birth. I was too stunned to think of a proper answer so I said in that case we either have to store the things somewhere or sell a lot of unused baby things.

**[pbu08-0249]** — Reader context: Pays for prenatal paternity testing but partner still requests a post-birth test.

> I’m (32/F) currently 34wks pregnant and I wanted to know if anyone had done a paternity test before the baby was born and what did your results say. Did the results come out accurate once you had the baby? I paid for one and the father still wants to get his own once the baby is born. I don’t like that but it’s up to him.  
>  Can anyone let me know what their results said?

**[pbu08-0261]** — Reader context: Bought postpartum supplies on sale and now plans to use them for pregnancy symptoms.

> I (luckily) started collecting postpartum goodies when they went on sale, so I actually have tucks pads! I’ll start using these ASAP

**[pbu08-0264]** — Reader context: Without maternal support, seeks a starting point and ways to eat and sleep after stopping cannabis.

> I really don’t know what I’m doing with being pregnant or with having a kid so I’m really just scared asking for advice somewhere to start things to get. I don’t know. I did go to the store and get some things that I heard we’re good for nausea, but I’m not 100% sure they will work so anybody’s ideas tips anything let me know another thing is I’m big on consuming lots of soda so I do have to not be like that anymore and on top of that I do smoke weed I haven’t since I found out, but it is definitely challenging because I literally do it to eat to sleep because I can’t really do either without it and just to be calm so it has been quite hard on me just within the last 24 hours I haven’t ate or slept..

**[pbu08-0266]** — Reader context: Partner shifts from support to a monetary offer and threat of disappearance if pregnancy continues.

> When I told him I was pregnant, he initially said he’d support me either way. Now that I’m leaning toward keeping it, he’s completely changed—he says he can’t handle another child, feels overwhelmed, and is now strongly pressuring me to abort. It’s escalated to offering me money ($20K) to terminate and saying he’ll disappear if I continue.

**[pbu08-0267]** — Reader context: Stable income and strong support do not remove fear of becoming a single mother of two.

> He later apologized and said he’s panicking, but the pressure and emotional whiplash have been intense.  
> At this point I don’t think our relationship survives either decision. I’m trying to decide what I can live with long-term. Deep down, I don’t think I can go through with an abortion, but I’m scared to be a single mom of two.
> 
> I have a stable job (work from home), solid income, and a strong support system—but this still feels terrifying and not the life I planned.  
> Has anyone been in a situation like this where:  
> you chose to continue alone and it worked out, or  
> the partner eventually stepped up later?
> 
> I just need some real-life perspectives.

**[pbu08-0311]** — Reader context: Exhausted and hungry, asks partner to cook and hears his tiredness outweighs hers.

> money has been really tight while i’m waiting to get food stamps. there have been times where he’ll spend the last of his money on nicotine, weed, or a sandwich for himself while i’m hungry. one time we had ground beef at home and i hadnt eaten anything all day, but i was so exhausted and nauseous that i couldn’t bring myself to cook it. i asked if he could make it, and he said no. i work full time too. when i told him i was too tired to cook, he said, “oh yeah, you’re tired? try doing what i have to do every day.” (he works in a warehouse 40 hours a week)

**[pbu08-0334]** — Reader context: At term, regrets continuing an unwanted pregnancy and worries about pausing education.

> He told me he admits that we might’ve not been financially in the best shape to have a baby.   
> I admitted I felt this was at 12 weeks but kept the baby anyway.   
> I’m 40 weeks + 3 days and this feeling is awful.   
> I feel like I never enjoyed this pregnancy, because it was unwanted. I don’t want this baby. But I love my partner. I feel weak for not sticking true to myself or listening to my gut feeling, and getting the abortion. I feel defeated thought it’s no one’s fault but my own.

**[pbu08-0335]** — Reader context: Bought organizers but partner prevents preparing the home for the baby.

> I’m currently 37 weeks and have nothing ready for baby boy, and it’s not by choice. I have repeatedly tried to bring things in the home to get things ready, purchased shelves, organizing baskets, bottle organizers, etc. just to prepare and nest the way I’d like. However, that’s not seeming to work with my partner. He does not want the baby items cluttering the home and feels we have to make more space before bringing things in which I have took the initiative to do on several occasions, exhausted and all. He’s very busy so the things he says he will do, often does not get done especially considering we are at a crunch for time.

**[pbu08-0336]** — Reader context: Stores feeding equipment and recovery supplies in a hot car or storage unit at the partner's insistence.

> So far, I have kept everything baby needs either in my car (in the middle of summer heat) or in a storage unit. Just today, I decided to just bring in the bottle sterilizer, my pumps, baby bottles, some creams, & postpartum supplies and got lots of negative remarks and feedback on that. He told me to put the stuff back in my car. I don’t want the things to get damaged and it’s exhausting running back and forth to a storage unit alone putting baby items back and forth.

**[pbu08-0337]** — Reader context: Threatened with eviction late in pregnancy, cannot find acceptable shelter.

> He tells me to leave every other day or whenever I upset him really, but I have no family, friends, or no where to go especially this late in pregnancy. It’s so draining. I have been looking for shelter but haven’t really had luck simply because I don’t want this for me or my baby and I feel like I deserve to prepare to welcome my baby in peace. I have very bad diagnosed anxiety, BPD, & Bipolar 2 so the fact that I cannot do anything is driving me crazy and into a depression truly.

**[pbu08-0338]** — Reader context: Plans to exclude an abusive former partner from delivery while high-risk pregnancy limits employment.

I rather wish he didnt exist in my life at all. When I brought up co parenting and asked if he'd be able to watch her on his own, he decided to attack me instead by saying im still living at home with no job. my pregnancy is high risk so I wasnt able to get a job due to not being allowed on my feet for more than 20mins. everytime I brought up co parenting,  he would ignore the question and just attack me instead.

**[pbu08-0394]** — Reader context: Reports the unexpected bill for prenatal screening.

> Just got my $3,399 NIPT Bill

**[pbu08-0396]** — Reader context: Says testing was purchased mainly for reassurance after missed miscarriage.

**[pbu08-0416]** — Reader context: Fears medication killed the baby while awaiting a first scan and Medicaid.

> Basically I’m worried if I killed my baby or not. I’m only 7 weeks and haven’t had an ultrasound yet and I’m still waiting to get Medicaid. It’s my first time ever being pregnant and I keep hearing these horror stories of miscarriages.

**[pbu21-0007]** — Reader context: Disagrees with wife about early sex testing and how loss would feel.

> She's also really anxious about knowing the gender and wants to pay for an at home sneak peek gender test because she's scared that, if we lose this pregnancy, she at least wants to know what gender they would've been (I tell her that this pregnancy will be fine and there's nothing to worry about, and even if something did happen, I think knowing the gender would only make it harder, mainly because my wife wants a girl really badly, and if we were to find out it's a girl and miscarry, that would be way harder for my wife than if we didn't know at all. Another part of it is that I've heard these sneak peeks can be inaccurate and easily contaminated especially if there are males in the household; overall it seems better to way for NIPT from what I've seen considering how much my wife wants a girl; on top of this I don't want to pay for extra testing that may be inaccurate because we're trying to save money which has always been my strength but not my wife's).

**[pbu21-0012]** — Reader context: Bought a swimming ring but changing positions still did not help.

> The problem she’s facing is that it’s not only on her back and her right side this problem will occurs, it’s on her left side as well as her stomach (we bought a swimming ring where she lays her belly) and sometimes also when she just sits a little leaned back this happens. It’s so rough for her and she can barely sleep because of this.

**[pbu21-0023]** — Reader context: Ordered then cancelled abortion pills.

> Today I tried ordering abortion pills bc another baby does not need to be brought into this mess. I cancelled them bc it doesn’t feel right. I feel selfish for wanting to keep this baby as my last before I get my tubes removed.

**[pbu21-0025]** — Reader context: Changing supplement brands caused an unnoticed fivefold dose increase.

> Im 18 weeks pregnant and i just realized that throughout my pregnancy I’ve been taking 5x more vitamin d3 than what I thought. I bought a different brand of vit d3, one pill is 5000iu. my previous brand. each pill was 1000iu, I used to take 4 of them. so I’ve stupidly been taking 20k iu of vitamin d3 per day.

**[pbu21-0038]** — Reader context: Mother’s pressure to terminate centers career and health concerns.

> Unfortunately, telling my mum became one of the hardest parts of this pregnancy.  
> She believes we’re making a mistake. She has repeatedly told me that having three children so close together will end my career, that nobody will hire me after this, that my degrees will be wasted, and that I’ll spend the rest of my life changing diapers. She has also expressed concern that my body won’t cope with a third C-section and insists that I’ll regret keeping this baby.  
> Some of what she says comes from genuine concern. I know she’s worried about my health and our finances. Those are valid concerns, and I don’t dismiss them.  
> But hearing my own mother repeatedly tell me that I’m incapable of succeeding after becoming a mother of three has been incredibly painful.  
> I already carry enough self-doubt. I don’t need someone I love reinforcing it.

**[pbu21-0043]** — Reader context: Feels selfish continuing a wanted pregnancy when partner did not want children.

> My partner doesn’t want kids but has been surprisingly supportive of if I decide to keep it. He’s told me he wouldn’t leave and would probably come around with time. However, I know that’s not what he wants. I don’t want him to stay or feel trapped in a relationship or be a parent because I’m forcing it on him. It also means he would have to cover more financial costs, which he has expressed is fine with him, but it doesn’t feel right to make him do that. I feel so selfish for not wanting to get an abortion but the thought of it is truly heartbreaking to me.

**[pbu21-0051]** — Reader context: Supportive partner and parents cannot remove depression or the cost barrier to outsourcing.

> I’m (30F) 24w pregnant with my 2nd and struggling so much with my mental health this pregnancy. I’ve always struggled with anxiety/depression/adhd but it seems to be significantly worse this pregnancy. I’m working full time & parenting a high energy 3 year old this time around but I can’t seem to get past my intrusive thoughts and debilitating fatigue. I truly have no desire to do anything but sleep right now. I feel like I’m drowning in responsibilities and can’t keep up with life. My husband is extremely supportive and keeps telling me to give myself grace right now but I feel so much frustration because I truly can’t keep up with life right now. Laundry, house work, getting baby’s room ready are all hanging over my head and I can’t seem to muster up any motivation to do anything. I feel horrible because my husband also works a pretty demanding, full time job and has picked up a lot of my slack. Outsourcing isn’t super feasible right now (like laundry or cleaning) because we’re pinching Pennie’s in order to pay off some debt before baby is here. Our parents help out as much as they can too but I’m just feeling so depressed and no desire to do anything.

**[pbu21-0054]** — Reader context: Feels guilty for using conception vitamins before discovering pregnancy on holiday.

> I am currently on holidays and I only took with me the pre natal vitamins (proceive conception). On holidays I discovered that I was pregnant and I kept taking the proceive conception just to have the adequate amount of folic acid. I haven’t gone to the doctor yet but I know it’s been roughly 20 days after conception. The thing now is that Im feeling extremely guilty for not being prepared and not taking the first trimester vitamins for the last three weeks or so, and also for not having them with me. But again, I had no clue I was pregnant. I am flying back home tomorrow and on Monday will buy the first trimester medicine.

**[pbu21-0087]** — Reader context: A routine 35-week appointment changed date, team and hospital without warning.

> The long story is this: my partner (40F) and I (40F) live approximately 4-5 hours away from our family.  We both have full time jobs, friend groups, and have purchased a home where we currently live.  We went in for a routine ultrasound and NST at 35 weeks.  We anticipated that l would have a scheduled c section at 37 weeks due to a previous uterine surgery. However, big shocker, I was told I had a chorioamniotic separation during the visit and that I needed to have my son that day (or the next day), not with my original OB team and not at the hospital we planned to deliver due to how early it was and the potential need for the NICU (our original hospital had a lower level of care and no room).  C section is had, baby boy is born with some breathing trouble (all resolved), and a little trouble feeding.  I’m discharged after 4 days, baby discharged from NICU and sent home after 5 days.

**[pbu21-0113]** — Reader context: Recurrent bleeding remains stressful despite repeated reassuring assessments.

> I was just wondering if any other mamas had polyps whilst pregnant and what their experience had been like. Is this going to keep happening? I’m trying to be rational, but it’s really getting to me and stressing me out, especially with no clear answers. I had a missed miscarriage in January so I already feel like I’m holding my breath, let alone with this ongoing! We have a private scan in a week and then our 20 week scan about 9 days after that.

**[pbu21-0123]** — Reader context: Orders crutches and takes over housework while pregnancy pain persists.

> We're now pretty sure that it's PGP / SPD, as the pain has not gone away for 2 weeks. The last few days in particular have been horrible for her. The pain has been really bad at times. I'm really concerned about her, and I'm helping as much as possible to make sure that she doesn't have to lift a finger round the house (although I do need to go into the office 3-4 days a week so won't always be able to be around). I've ordered some crutches arriving tomorrow which I'm hoping might help.

**[pbu21-0125]** — Reader context: Public announcement claims future leave changes; not a parent experience or verified entitlement.

> So we’re changing the law. From April, women and their partners will be entitled to two weeks off after any pregnancy loss. And we’re making wider changes, with new rights to time off when you lose someone you love.

**[pbu21-0131]** — Reader context: Fears recurrent depression with partner refusing a better job and shorter hours.

> My fiance only got a week off work after baby was born and even though he got a better job offer with better pay and way more reasonable hours ($5/hr more and steady 10hr days instead of 12hr days) he still refuses to leave his current job and signed a contract for another several months after I begged him not to. It’s like he wanted to have a baby just to be able to say he’s a dad and now she and I are both just accessories to him and the novelty of parenthood has worn off.

**[pbu21-0132]** — Reader context: Recalls paid leave treated as vacation and no care on returning home.

> The way he treated me postpartum was so awful that I cannot go one day without thinking about it and I have told him that. When I told him that, he got offended that I would say such a thing. He watched me have two natural births, he watched me in excruciating pain two separate times. And even after seeing me like that, he still wouldn't even change a diaper in the hospital. Hell? He wouldn't even go into Walmart to get me my mommy diapers!  He wouldn't do anything, literally not one single thing to help me when we got home also. He wouldn't keep wood in the fire, he wouldn't even bring wood in for me to add myself, he wouldn't hold the baby so I could shower. He got paid leave that he called a "free vacation."  He expected dinner the night we got home, didn't do a single chore to help out. He never went to a single pregnancy or baby appointment. Never made dinner, never went shopping, I can't think of one thing he did besides go to work sometimes and pay the bills, the easiest part if you ask me.

**[pbu21-0133]** — Reader context: Decision to terminate followed expectation of raising four children alone.

> In January of this year I made the hard decision to have an abortion, something I never thought I myself would do. But the thought being a single parent to four kids and him gaining off of it? I couldn't fathom it. He said he would support me whatever my decision, I told him I was getting the pills because I would not be a single parent again. He never said another word about it. It's been a rough 6 months since I've done it, and I've had to deal with it alone because he does not care to talk about my well-being.

**[pbu21-0136]** — Reader context: Older child’s emerging needs and unaffordable daycare complicate current pregnancy.

> I’m also pregnant again and really having a hard time being excited about it. We can barely afford one kid in daycare, we definitely can’t afford two. I’m going to have to take a long leave from my dream job until one of them is old enough for preschool; or we move to Florida and move in with my family-in-law.

**[pbu21-0143]** — Reader context: Only-income partner cannot provide full-time care.

> Her father works. We live together. He's helpful when he's around but he's our only income, so he can't just commit to caring for the baby full time.

**[pbu21-0152]** — Reader context: Prior family caregiving makes renewed loss of independence feel especially painful.

> I feel sad and guilty because I should be ecstatic but I feel sad that I am losing independence again, I never got to have that surprise engagement, the wedding, the honeymoon, the years just with my partner. I never got to the point in my new career where I was comfortable and earning well. I was pushing so hard for a better title better pay and that was my focus, but now I’m stopping it all again to care for someone else, when I really needed to care for me for a bit longer

**[pbu21-0155]** — Reader context: Names pay, savings and graduate-school fears.

> First off, I’m worried about my finances. I work as a waiter that pays decently well(500-800 on average). It doesn’t come with any benefits but it has allowed me to have a decent saving (upwards of 30k). I just don’t know if it’ll be enough. I’m worried about whether or not I’ll be able to go to grad school. I’m worried that I won’t have any time for myself as selfish as it sounds. This all just came out of left field.

**[pbu21-0157]** — Reader context: Reports another hospital transfer to afford emergency surgery.

> But the cost was very high, so we transferred again to another hospital that was more affordable.

**[pbu21-0160]** — Reader context: Repeated newborn visits cost $200 each before admission.

> 2 weeks in, insert horrible eczema. 5 pediatric visits keep getting “baby acne”. Billing $200 a click in 3 weeks. Finally it’s so bad and extremities turning blue. Go to ER. Get admitted.

**[pbu21-0164]** — Reader context: Uncertainty about continuing pregnancy includes money and shared housing.

> I don't know what to do, I don't know if I should continue this pregnancy or not. My partner is supportive either way, but I just don't think our current situation with money and roommates are ideal. We have a spare room, it's just, I don't know.

**[pbu21-0171]** — Reader context: Booked private imaging to avoid another emergency-department wait.

> This time the pain was on my left side, which scared me even more because I worried it might be my pancreas. I booked a private ultrasound before heading to the hospital. I just couldn’t face another long wait in A&E. It turned out my gallbladder was worse, and the stones had started moving into the bile ducts. I was told to go to the ER immediately.

**[pbu21-0173]** — Reader context: After surgery, bought a support belt and found short walks helpful.

> I was just on paracetamol pain relief wise, position Id say would be best elevated on your back, foods I kept bland and small portions but often. Movement is the best healer and having a walk, even of it’s around the house (which was my case) helped me loads. I had a horrible pain around belly button area, I bought a belt that supports lower belly only which helped me to stay on my feet longer :)

**[pbu21-0230]** — Reader context: Personal readiness standards conflict with attachment and partner’s rejection of adoption.

> Hi everyone, I just dont know what to do. I've always been pro choice, and I still stand by that. However, I recently discovered I'm pregnant. For some reason, I can't bring myself to get an abortion. I am 20, work in the car business, and live with my parents comfortably. My bf goes to culinary school, and is a wonderful person all around. I've known him for a little over 5 years now. I was never a person who loved kids, but I was certain that if I DID have kids, I would be in my own home, married, and financially stable. I spoke to my bf about adoption and he was completely against it. Asked how I would be okay giving my flesh and blood away. He said either abort  or we raise it. My parents said they would support me with whatever decision I make. I know if I keep it though, it's incredibly unfair to them. I don't know where to turn, and I can't make a decision. Any ideas or tips would be greatly appreciated!

**[pbu21-0253]** — Reader context: Pregnancy-related incapacity and distance from partner provoke guilt about the baby’s future.

> I know plenty of women work through the majority of their pregnancy, but so many massive things happened in my life all at once that I feel entirely lost. I’ve considered getting a part time job back home just to have a little money but I don’t know if I feel physically able. I feel so much guilt. I don't have savings lined up, I feel like a failed college kid relying on her parents, and the first few months of my baby's life will be spent 4.5 hours away from their father. I’m also terrified that my pre-existing depression will amplify into severe postpartum depression, and I'm scared I won't be able to pull my weight financially when we do get a house. I just feel so guilty toward my baby. What if I’m setting them up for failure from the start?

**[pbu21-0264]** — Reader context: Pays for partner’s food and faces gambling, theft and forced responsibility for a fine.

> The last few weeks he's continuously gambled and hid it from me, he has no money so I've had to pay for all his food including all my food (which is a lot in the first trimester because food aversions have been wild and he literally expects it/doesn't thank me at all), he even got a speeding fine in my car and forced me to take the demerit points because he's on a probation license, he's also been stealing consistently and forcing me to pick him up on it.

**[pbu21-0273]** — Reader context: Paid $100 for private reassurance while awaiting first care appointment.

> Also because doctors are so backed up here I had to wait so long for my appointment I’m literally like 13 weeks?! so a few weeks ago I paid 100$ to get a private ultrasound because I had a miscarriage at 11 weeks back in January, I brought the photos apparently that was completely useless to them.

**[pbu21-0281]** — Reader context: Disrupted water routine causes meltdown and shame during pregnancy.

> I am autistic. I had a bad meltdown this morning because my routine was disturbed. I always drink bottled water so I can track how much I drink and it is familiar to me. I am currently 30 weeks pregnant and can’t lift anything heavy. We ran out of water last night so, I asked my husband to come with me very early this morning to the store to help me. He didn’t get home til very late last night so he did not want to get up and needed more sleep before he works another long shift today. I took that TERRIBLY. I started panicking and my husband suggested I just go buy a gallon of water. I didnt want to buy a gallon of water. Straying from the normal routine felt like the end of the world. We ended up having a really heated argument and I had a terrible meltdown where I was hitting my hands on my head and thighs, screaming and crying. My baby is perfectly fine. I didn’t hit my belly but, now I feel immense shame and regret for what I did. I couldnt control myself. I know I am going to be a great parent but right now I feel like the worst. Please say anything negative. I just need support. I am not in danger to myself or anyone else and would never intentionally do anything to hurt myself at this point in my life.

**[pbu21-0290]** — Reader context: Wanted family image conflicts with practical instability and intended separation.

> I’m torn between the life I imagined - becoming a mom at this age, creating my own version of family - and the reality of my current situation: unstable relationship, unstable finances, no job, no car, and a partner I was planning to leave.

**[pbu21-0291]** — Reader context: Cannot take time off during possible miscarriage because leave must be saved.

> this pregnancy is so wanted. I did all the right things. devastated to discover light spotting last night. my OB was helpful and I’m working on getting labs and ultrasound. but I’m so sad, I really hope this isn’t the start of a miscarriage. I’m mad at my body for not being able to do this basic biological function. and I’m still trying to hold on to hope that everything will be ok. and as much as I wanted to take today off, I have to hoard my sick time because my office offers so little parental leave.

**[pbu21-0307]** — Reader context: Bereavement reduces concentration for registry decisions and newborn preparation.

> I am trying to get the essentials where the baby sleeps and lays to be greenguard gold certified and as nontoxic as possible.   
> I’m worried about buying things that I don’t need as we have a small home and live minimally. With her loss, it’s just been really hard to concentrate on getting this registry together. I have only had time to work and just trying to keep myself healthy and read about preparing for labor and how to actually care for the newborn when they come.

**[pbu21-0309]** — Reader context: Prior stillbirth delays readiness and car-seat purchase even after prior loss milestone.

> I just really need to vent. I am currently 35 weeks pregnant with my second baby boy after a 3rd trimester stillbirth in 2022. I honestly thought that after I made it past the point in which I lost his big brother that I would begin to feel more excited for this baby, but I have honestly felt anything but. It’s been so bad that I literally JUST purchased his car seat & have been dragging my foot packing his hospital bag.

**[pbu21-0319]** — Reader context: Starts disability leave and preparation while uncertain about newborn prognosis.

> What a wild ride this has been, I’m now out of work on STD and will be working on our hospital bag and nursery while attempting to prioritize rest and remaining stress free. We have no idea what this means for our son once he’s born, the flutter can disappear on its own outside of the womb or we could be on medicine for awhile.

**[pbu21-0323]** — Reader context: Conflicting restrictions follow job loss and threaten ability to buy insulin.

> My OB reached out to me via email because she saw I was in the ER, but she said that there was nothing I could do about it nor did she give me any restrictions or precautions to take. I am really at a loss for what I am supposed to do next. Do I continue to follow better rest protocol?  My pregnancy was already high risk because I have type one diabetes so I’m already seeing maternal fetal medicine. I’m so scared and unsure of what to do, I don’t know when I’ll be able to afford insulin again since losing my job, and I’m terrified of losing my baby.

**[pbu21-0324]** — Reader context: Pregnancy during divorce brings pressure from a new partner to terminate.

> Well my life is currently a disaster. Going through a divorce. Been separated for a year. Just bought a house. I Met someone we were careful and I just found out I’m pregnant. I have a 10 yo 7 yo and 5 yo. The guy I’m been dating wants me to get an abortion. He says he doesn’t want kids. I morally don’t know if I can do it. I don’t know what to do.

**[pbu21-0330]** — Reader context: A small shower’s per-person cost informs invitation boundaries.

> I’m pregnant with my second baby and my mom is treating me to a very small baby shower/afternoon tea at a nice hotel. There are only 8** **people** **total all adult women I’m personally close with... and it costs $80** **per** **person, so my mom is already paying around $640 for everyone. He now has an attitude because I didn’t invite his mom or sister and says it’s weird.

**[pbu21-0331]** — Reader context: At four weeks postpartum, in-laws expected cleaning for other adults.

> We lived with his parents for three months when my first son was born because we were looking for a house to rent. She called some big family meeting with them in the next room. She spent that entire time telling my boyfriend how it's disrespectful for me not to be cleaning up after her other adult children who also lived there. Mind you this happened when I was like four weeks postpartum. I cleaned up my messes and my boyfriend's messes and made sure I kept a little profile because I already didn't wanna be there as.

**[pbu21-0347]** — Reader context: Anticipated maternity clothing needs prompt considering buy-now-pay-later spending.

> All this was triggered by me having an absolute crash out that nothing in my closet fits comfortably and I'm going to have to buy now pay later a capsule wardrobe of maternity cloths. I'm 16 weeks, no sense in siving up when ill just have to do it again in a month.

**[pbu21-0355]** — Reader context: Shopping for maternity clothes was an investment in feeling good during a last pregnancy.

> In early August, I found out I was expecting my 4th baby (3rd pregnancy, I had twins first time). It was unexpected and I was so excited to be having another baby. Since it was my 3rd and probably last pregnancy, I decided to spoil myself and went on a shopping spree (mostly secondhand but some brand new) since most of my maternity clothes I didn’t like and I just really wanted to feel good this pregnancy.

**[pbu21-0356]** — Reader context: Clothes bought for pregnancy become painful reminders after miscarriage.

> Last week I suffered a miscarriage at 6+6. I‘m pretty devastated and I now have all of these clothes that I bought and won’t be able to use. I really need to get them out of my closet because they are too hard to look at now and are associated with my loss.

**[pbu21-0362]** — Reader context: Seventeen-year-old names $110 pill cost and asks for cheaper termination options.

> We live in a state where abortions are illegal, and my mom is heavily against abortion (believes it’s murder and yada yada). Neither my boyfriend or I make enough money to move out and support a baby right now. I’m guessing I’m about a month pregnant as I got my period in the beginning of July. I was considering ordering abortion pills from a neighboring state as I don’t need parental consent to buy them, and they’re about 110 dollars. However I’m unsure of how far along I am and feel insane guilt aborting the baby. Is there any advice anyone could offer me on how to feel better about my decision? Is there a cheaper way to terminate the pregnancy?

**[pbu21-0387]** — Reader context: Poem links wanting the baby with saving tips toward genetic testing.

> I want this baby.  
> And I hate that wanting it  
> doesn’t magically make the numbers work,  
> doesn’t make the rent cheaper,  
> doesn’t put savings in my account,  
> doesn’t make me suddenly grown enough  
> to know exactly what I’m doing.  
> I’ve only known you for a little while,  
> but already  
> I’ve started saving for you.  
> Three hundred dollars  
> from tips I could have spent,  
> put away because I thought,  
> *ten weeks, genetic testing,*  
> *I need to be ready.*

**[pbu21-0397]** — Reader context: Recurrence of fear of leaving home threatens work during pregnancy.

> Does anyone else develop a severe fear of going out? I lost my baby at 21 weeks in July and I’m currently 5wks pregnant. After my son I was still slightly agoraphobic but I started having the desire to go outside and then boom now I don’t wanna talk to people or leave my house.  
> I remember with my son at the height of it all I didn’t shower for days and stood in my house so long I didn’t even realize the way time was passing. The nights I stood at my baby daddy’s house there was no sunlight so I didn’t even. Know what time of day it was. I would ignore calls and messages. People had to contact my bd just to check on me.  
> I was so hopeless I basically quit my job and even let the power get shut off in my house. I lost that much drive.   
> This time around I am very much trying to challenge myself to leave my house. I’m typing this from the shower in anticipation of starting the day. I’m so afraid to go to work tonight. I don’t want my days to feel impossible. Can anyone give advice on how to handle this?

**[pbu21-0399]** — Reader context: Apprentice fears losing job after disclosure despite knowing discrimination rules exist.

> My biggest worry right now is my job. I feel like everything is riding on my performance at work and if I get fired or not once I reveal my pregnancy(which is going to have to be soon, with how far along I already am). I know there’s laws in place to prevent discrimination but I can’t help but not trust those. Now to disclose my tricky situation, I’m a female plumbing apprentice, not only that, but I started in june without knowing I was already a few weeks pregnant by then. Anddd… to worsen it, my uncle is in a sort of management position there and is the one who recommended me. There are a few things that worsen it even more! I like my uncle, I don’t think he dislikes me but we aren’t the closest, this is mainly because i’m not a very social person even among family. I feel guilty in a way, especially because i’m very grateful for him recommending me, that i’m pregnant because it will make things awkward for him, not just me.

**[pbu21-0400]** — Reader context: Home-birth midwife cost and fear of being judged block planning.

> My birth plan is cause for concern also. There is pretty much no plan and I haven’t taken any courses or anything even though i’m this far along. The baby is perfectly healthy and I have another checkup tomorrow. My boyfriend wants me to have a natural home birth, and I also want that, it’s just that a midwife won’t be covered by my insurance, and it will cost about $4\~5k. Of course many of them offer payment plans, which helps a lot, but it’s stressful thinking about how I will need to pay for rent, food, gas, insurance, all the other baby necessities, etc. soon as well. While his mom will help a little, I’ll be paying for most of everything as I’m the one with a full time job(again, praying I don’t get fired). Back to the midwife problem, aside from the cost, i’m also very worried I won’t be able to find one I feel very comfortable with. I feel like i’ll feel judged by most of them as I have some slightly unconventional requests(nothing really weird but also not completely sorted out just yet) for my birth.

**[pbu21-0401]** — Reader context: Expects to miss FMLA eligibility and worries about time with newborn.

> The only thing I think I forgot to mention until now is how i’m also worried that I won’t have much time to spend with my child, especially after giving birth because, like I said, I work overtime most weeks. I also won’t qualify for FMLA until a few months after I give birth, and even then that only gets me a few unpaid weeks off. Lol, i’m very conflicted on how i’ll be treated at work once I disclose my pregnancy! I feel like they’ll either be very supporting or i’ll be fired, no in between🥹

**[pbu32-0008]** — Reader context: Nine weeks pregnant after missed miscarriage; soreness creates purchase hesitation.

> I very rarely wear bras if I’m honest but obviously as I’m getting more pregnant they need a bit more support but feel like it’s wild to buy anything maternity yet especially after last time so do people just size up?

**[pbu32-0031]** — Reader context: Toddler's crib escape prompts monitor replacement before second baby.

> I’m also not interested in spending hundreds of dollars on something that is going to monitor my baby’s vitals or make my family feel like we’re living in a surveillance state (mostly because I am perfectly capable of doing both of those things myself) - I simply want to improve our system and ensure its more reliable that the Vtech WiFi Monitor we were gifted before our first before the next little one arrives.

**[pbu32-0036]** — Reader context: Unconsulted trip conflicts with baby preparations and need to move.

> No baby preparations have begun yet but I’ve been making moves to work towards starting because we need to *literally move* out of our current living space*…* we have SO MANY other things that need to be purchased and done. He dropped thousands on a trip that’s going to last 2 days and decided to bring everyone whom he will have to pay everything for.

**[pbu32-0039]** — Reader context: Infant-care job requires sitting on floor; jeans squeeze at thirteen weeks.

> I don't want maternity jeans, I don't care for leggings, and I don't like sweatpants that are tight at the bottom. I'm also a tall girl at 5'10, and need pants that would fit my entire legs lol. They don't have to be ultra professional because our dress code is pretty chill. The cheaper the better 🙏🏼 lol.

**[pbu32-0057]** — Reader context: Readmitted twice after postpartum blood-pressure spike; medication adjustment ongoing.

> My husband returns to work next week. I asked another family member to fly in and help next week (also have a toddler) because I’m truly scared to be home alone with both kids at this point.

**[pbu32-0062]** — Reader context: Sixteen weeks pregnant with HG, four children, recent hospitalization and burns.

> We’ve exhausted our finances from the hospital stay and my husband staying out of work to help with the kids. I’m tired and just ready to feel better.

**[pbu32-0063]** — Reader context: Partner notices overwhelmed author's needs after hospital stay.

> Luckily my husband noticed something was wrong and went and got me take out after he cooked the kids food. I am so blessed 🥹 I hope things get better soon!

**[pbu32-0069]** — Reader context: Twelve-day-old's parents monitor weight at home.

> We saw a dip in his weight at 5lb 8oz( 3 days after birth) my wife decided to buy a baby scale.

**[pbu32-0077]** — Reader context: Considers paying privately for NIPT in addition to NHS screening.

> After two miscarriages, I'm just looking to gain as much information about this current pregnancy as I can so that I can give it the best chance possible.

**[pbu32-0078]** — Reader context: No stated purchase or price; prospective judgment about private NIPT.

> I feel like it's a lot of money to justify but of course it's worth it for peace of mind.

**[pbu32-0091]** — Reader context: Online research creates uncertainty after buying prescribed progesterone for spotting.

> After doing some research, I’ve been seeing that progesterone after 10 weeks has no real benefit. I also have no history of loss as this is my first pregnancy. I bought the cyclogest and started using it but I’m not sure if I should continue.

**[pbu32-0109]** — Reader context: New passage links reported PTSD and paid support absent during emergency section.

> So not only did I get absolutely traumatized with a PTSD diagnosis from the delivery process and everything that went on as I crossed the threshold of motherhood alone, I paid a lot of money for service I never received.

**[pbu32-0118]** — Reader context: Recently had second child; says fathers receive little mental-health attention.

**Role qualification:** [pbu21-0125] is a public-policy announcement, not a parent’s personal benefit entitlement verified here. [pbu32-0336] names a pump acquired without an amount or payment claim. [pbu32-0331] criticizes a marketed remedy without describing a purchase.

## Requests

### Outright asks for products, resources, plans, lists or specialists

These requests vary from a postpartum section-recovery program to packing and registry help, grief resources, maternity clothing, a reliable monitor, a supply-focused lactation service and cheaper abortion access. Clinical and support-service requests are included alongside practical preparation. Asking for any of these does not establish willingness to buy a general pregnancy guide.

**[pbu08-0055]** — Reader context: Seeks vetted, digestible resources for a first-time single mother leaving an abusive relationship.

> Now I am not a parent yet so I’m not about to push any parenting ideology down anyone’s throat. There’s way too much info out there. But we agreed that running from her emotions and keeping busy to drown out the pain is not going to help. I’m legit worried for her and her baby.. does anyone have any suggestions for resources that are in general GOOD for first time parents, and also anything for single mothers of sons? Anything to know about post partum depression? Anything about surviving narcissistic abuse during or after pregnancy? Anything about lifespan development and milestones… podcasts/audiobooks/youtubers/youtube videos???

**[pbu08-0135]** — Reader context: Seeks a postpartum section-recovery workout program where apps mainly offer prenatal workouts.

> I’ve noticed lots of apps have prenatal workout series but not much postpartum. I had a c-section 13 weeks ago and I’ve done a grand total of one workout since lol. Not that I was crazy active before but I’d love a good postpartum c section program to follow to help heal as well as possible!

**[pbu08-0141]** — Reader context: Wants supplement options for recovery and breastfeeding beyond the OB's prenatal recommendation.

> I’m almost a month postpartum and EBF my little boy. I’ve asked my OB for recommendations on supplements and he really only directed me to continue my prenatal. I’m totally cool with this but I was hoping to get some ideas/input/experiences from yall when it comes to other options for supplements? I have a few days left of my prenatal and trying to decide what to purchase!   
> My priorities would be recovery and breastfeeding support however I’m really interested in any thoughts you have!  
> TIA!!

**[pbu08-0153]** — Reader context: Seeks affordable ways to support his wife's grief on Mother's Day.

> What are some ideas to help my wife grieve while still celebrating her Mother’s Day with our 2 kids? Money is a barrier for us but we can still afford to go out if that’s the best course of action. I don’t really care if we do celebrate, I just want what’s best for her at this moment. I am so lost at this point and could use the ideas. Thank you all

**[pbu08-0179]** — Reader context: Willing to pay for maternity clothes if they remain useful after weight loss.

> Has anyone bought from them? Do you recommend it? Can you really wear them postpartum? I’m willing to spend the money on a single pair of shorts and pants if it’s something I can truly wear even after I’ve lost the baby weight. Curious of your thoughts!

**[pbu08-0186]** — Reader context: Fear of falling asleep while feeding makes nursing-pillow usefulness, price and multipurpose value urgent.

> I tend to have a sort of narcolepsy response where I involuntarily fall asleep during important tasks if I don't get good sleep, so this has been my biggest worry. Doctors haven't been much help on this concern, they just tell me to get better sleep and *somehow* that hasn't been a watertight solution, so I rely on myself to manage it in different ways. The narcolepsy-type response I have you'd think would give me a no-brainer "oh yes, I should probably buy this nursing pillow so I don't worry about immediate dangers if I fall asleep while holding my baby", but I also see some moms who come out saying "literally you can use a regular pillow and it works fine" so I get a bit stuck there. There's also the question of what I would possibly use the nursing pillow for when I don't need it, do I just...sell it? That seems like a waste of money, which we are tight on. So I'd really appreciate any personal opinions about nursing pillows; whether you think they're necessary, whether you'd definitely or totally recommend them for my situation or a pillow would do the exact same thing I'm looking for (comfort and baby's safety), AND I'd especially appreciate if there are any recommendations for nursing pillows that have uses outside of being a nursing pillow, as the good quality ones I tend to see everywhere..only have that one use, I'd much rather have something multipurpose.

**[pbu08-0205]** — Reader context: Wants informed chronic-pain care without being presumed to have substance-use problems.

> I am seeking an MFM in Atlanta area that knows more about this than "offering methadone" I'm not in recovery or have substance abuse issues. The ob I saw KEPT insinuating I do, which was rude and not helpful. I barely know her and I've been managing my meds with a lot of drug testing and supervision for years. I am open to tapering/getting off, but I want a kind, compassionate ob who has experience with this and knows how withdrawal/untreated pain/opioid rx could affect myself and unborn baby.

**[pbu08-0260]** — Reader context: Severe hemorrhoid pain and surgical referral arrive far earlier than expected.

> 
> Does anyone have any advice? I thought this would be my life after labor, not nearly 20 weeks before!

**[pbu08-0264]** — Reader context: Without maternal support, seeks a starting point and ways to eat and sleep after stopping cannabis.

I did go to the store and get some things that I heard we’re good for nausea, but I’m not 100% sure they will work so anybody’s ideas tips anything let me know another thing is I’m big on consuming lots of soda so I do have to not be like that anymore and on top of that I do smoke weed I haven’t since I found out, but it is definitely challenging because I literally do it to eat to sleep because I can’t really do either without it and just to be calm so it has been quite hard on me just within the last 24 hours I haven’t ate or slept..

Im currently 31 + 1. Anything helpful would be appreciated.

**[pbu08-0307]** — Reader context: Fear from clinician advice delays asking the OB while heartburn persists despite lifestyle and medication changes.

### Expressed needs, attempted requests and care arrangements

These preserve requests already made to care teams, desired services, access barriers, decision help and plans the parent wants assistance with. Some are descriptions of attempts or needs rather than an open product-recommendation question; keep that distinction in any later demand count.

**[pbu08-0116]** — Reader context: Fear of dogs hurting the baby changes behavior at home.

I had an irrational fear during the time that my dogs were going to hurt my baby by jumping up.

**[pbu08-0118]** — Reader context: Following spacing advice, wants lived experience of recovery after three or more sections.

> I've had two C-sections - my first was an unplanned emergency and my second was planned. I healed really well after my second. My surgeon said everything looked good during the operation and I have spoken to a consultant who confirmed that he'd be happy for me to have another baby if I wanted to. He advised waiting 18 months before trying to conceive again and I am following this advice.
> 
> We'd love to have a third (or even a fourth!) baby if we're able to, so I'm interested in hearing from people who've had three or more C-sections.
> 
> I'd love to know:
> 
> How was your recovery after your third (or fourth+) C-section compared to the others?  
> How long did you wait before conceiving again?  
> Did you have any complications, or was everything fairly straightforward?  
> Is there anything you wish you'd known beforehand?

**[pbu08-0136]** — Reader context: Links distress to pregnancy losses and postpartum recurrence; seeks relief after multiple medications and talk therapy.

> Fortunately never had anxiety/depression until I had back to back pregnancy losses in late 2021. After that my brain went haywire : Suicidal pure OCD, intrusive thoughts, fear of losing control, etc. I was able to somewhat "manage" it with time, but I am now 6 months post partum with my 2nd and these thoughts are out of control and somewhat turning into "wow I do not know if I will make it out of this". I am scared, horrified for my life. I have failed all SSRIS thus far, lamictal, clomipramine, caplyta, abilify. I am so lucky I have an incredible support system who are helping me, but I need to help myself and these thoughts need to ease. I need relief desperately. I am looking in to two extreme options: Ketamine or Zurzuvae. \*I have also tried talk therapy with no relief\*. I am also more than willing to do any therapy to help myself, but I desperately need even 20 percent relief in the interim in order to do so. Anyone have relief with either?

**[pbu08-0139]** — Reader context: Partner's traumatic recollection changes plans for another birth and prompts adoption or surrogacy discussion.

I understand like I said I don’t remember any of that but I respect that. We’d rather have me here than not. So we talked about adoption and or having a surrogate . Adoption seems easiest route financially and we have lots of love to give. Having a surrogate could be up to $200k based off others experiences who were able to do that route. **[pbu08-0393]** — Reader context: Fears missing a helpful treatment while questioning clinician advice after recurrent losses.

**[pbu21-0052]** — Reader context: Repeated encouragement to give grace does not resolve inability to function.
